\mathcal{O}_{\left]X\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}}\rightarrow{\bf R}\!\left]f\right[_{u\mathrm{dR}*}\mathcal{O}_{\left]X^{\prime}\right[_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}}

and tensor with
{\bf R}\!\left]f\right[_{u\mathrm{dR}*}\mathscr{G}
. Next, I claim that (
3.4.1
) is an isomorphism. Since
u^{*}
commutes with extension by zero, as does
{\bf R}\!\left]f\right[_{u\mathrm{dR}!}={\bf R}\!\left]f\right[_{u\mathrm{dR}*}
, the usual dévissage argument reduces to the case when
\mathscr{F}
is locally free. In this case, as remarked above,
{\bf R}\!\left]f\right[_{u\mathrm{dR}*}
coincides with the functor
f_{*}
from Proposition
3.4.1
, and the assertion then just reduces to elementary computations in the representation theory of finite groups. I can therefore define

\eta_{\mathscr{G}}\colon\mathscr{G}\rightarrow\left]f\right[^{*}_{u}{\bf R}\!\left]f\right[_{u\mathrm{dR}*}\mathscr{G}

by tensoring the unit

\eta_{\mathcal{O}_{\left]X^{\prime}\right[_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}}}\colon\mathcal{O}_{\left]X^{\prime}\right[_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}}\rightarrow\left]f\right[^{*}_{u}{\bf R}\!\left]f\right[_{u\mathrm{dR}*}\mathcal{O}_{\left]X^{\prime}\right[_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}}

from Proposition
3.4.1
with
\mathscr{G}
.

Now, checking that
\varepsilon
and
\eta
give rise to an adjunction amount to checking that the natural transformations

\displaystyle{\bf R}\!\left]f\right[_{u\mathrm{dR}*}\mathscr{G}

\displaystyle\overset{\eta}{\longrightarrow}{\bf R}\!\left]f\right[_{u\mathrm{dR}*}\left]f\right[_{u}^{*}{\bf R}\!\left]f\right[_{u\mathrm{dR}*}\mathscr{G}\overset{\varepsilon}{\longrightarrow}{\bf R}\!\left]f\right[_{u\mathrm{dR}*}\mathscr{G}

\displaystyle\left]f\right[_{u}^{*}\mathscr{F}

\displaystyle\overset{\eta}{\longrightarrow}\left]f\right[_{u}^{*}{\bf R}\!\left]f\right[_{u\mathrm{dR}*}\left]f\right[_{u}^{*}\mathscr{F}\overset{\varepsilon}{\longrightarrow}\left]f\right[_{u}^{*}\mathscr{F}

are the identity. Unwinding the definitions, this follows from Proposition
3.4.1
.

The final claim, concerning the compositions of the units and counits giving the identity maps, reduces, by the construction, to the locally free case considered in Proposition
3.4.1
.
∎

4.
Overholonomic
\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}
-modules

In this section, I will briefly recall the basic theory of overholonomic
\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}
-modules on formal schemes and varieties, mostly following the exposition in
[
AC18
, §1]
. I will also introduce the dual constructible t-structure on the category of overholonomic complexes of
\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}
-modules, which will be the one matching up with the natural t-structure on
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons}}
via the overconvergent Riemann–Hilbert correspondence.

4.1.
Cohomological formalism of overholonomic
\boldsymbol{\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}}
-modules: formal schemes

Let
\mathfrak{P}
be a smooth formal scheme, and
\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}
Berthelot’s ring of overconvergent differential operators of
\mathfrak{P}
. Then Caro has defined in
[
Car09b
]
the notion of an overholonomic complex of
\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}
-modules, giving rise to a full, triangulated subcategory

{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol}}(\mathfrak{P})\subset{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}).

The category
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}})
admits a
\sigma
-linear Frobenius pullback functor

F^{*}\colon{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}})\rightarrow{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}),

which is t-exact for the natural t-structure
[
Ber00
, Théoréme 4.2.4]
.

4.1.1 Definition
.

An object
\mathcal{M}\in{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}})
is said to be ‘of Frobenius type’ if it’s cohomology sheaves are iterated extensions of objects admitting some Frobenius structure.

Again, the terminology here is slightly non-standard. I will denote by

{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})\subset{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol}}(\mathfrak{P})

the full subcategory on objects which are of Frobenius type. Thanks to work of Caro and Caro–Tsuzuki, this category admits a good formalism of cohomological operations, which I now describe. First of all, there are the duality and tensor product functors

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{\mathfrak{P}}\colon

\displaystyle{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})

\displaystyle-\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}-\colon

\displaystyle{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})\times{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})\rightarrow{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol}}(\mathfrak{P})

defined in
[
Ber02
, §4.3.10]
and
[
Car15
, §2.1]
. These preserve overholonomicity by
[
Car09b
, Corollaire 3.4]
and
[
Car15
, Théorème 4.2.4]
respectively. Caro then defines

\widetilde{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}:=\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}[-\dim\mathfrak{P}]

which will turn out to match up better with the tensor product of constructible isocrystals.

For any locally closed subscheme
X\hookrightarrow P
, Caro has defined the idempotent endofunctor

{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{X}\colon{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})\rightarrow{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})

of sections with support on
X
, see
[
AC18
, §1.1.5]
and the references given there. This works as follows. If
X
is closed in
P
, then we can write it as an intersection of divisors
X=\cap_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}
. For each
D_{i}
Berthelot defined functor
{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{D_{i}}
in
[
Ber02
, §4.4.4-4.4.5]
, and Caro defines

{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{X}:={\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{D_{1}}\circ\ldots\circ{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{D_{n}}.

He shows that this doesn’t depend on the choice of the
D_{i}
, and that the natural morphism

{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{X}\rightarrow\mathrm{id}

is an isomorphism when evaluated on overholonomic complexes supported (set theoretically) on
X
. He further shows that the morphism

{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{X}\rightarrow\mathrm{id}

has a functorial cone, denoted
(^{\dagger}X)
. If
X
is locally closed, with closure
Y
in
P
, Caro then defines

{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{X}:=(^{\dagger}Y\setminus X)\circ{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Y}.

The order of composition here can be reversed, and the result is this same if
Y
and
Y\setminus X
are replaced by arbitrary closed subschemes
Z
and
T
of
P
such that
X=Z\setminus T
. The functor
{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{X}
only depends on
X_{\rm red}
.

If
u\colon\mathfrak{P}\rightarrow\mathfrak{Q}
is a morphism of smooth formal schemes, then Berthelot defined in
[
Ber02
, §4.3]
the functors

\displaystyle u^{!}\colon

\displaystyle{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{Q}{\mathbb{Q}}})\rightarrow{\bf D}^{b}(\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}})

\displaystyle u_{+}\colon

\displaystyle{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}})\rightarrow{\bf D}^{b}(\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{Q}{\mathbb{Q}}}).

Caro showed in
[
Car09b
, Théorème 3.8]
that
u^{!}
preserves overholonomicity, and moreover in
[
Car09b
, Théoréme 3.9]
that so does
u_{+}
whenever
u
is proper. In this case, there is the natural duality isomorphism

u_{+}\circ{\bf D}_{\mathfrak{P}}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}{\bf D}_{\mathfrak{Q}}\circ u_{+}

by
[
Vir04
, Corollaire 7.3]
, and
(u_{+},u^{!})
form an adjoint pair by
[
Vir04
, Théorème 7.4, and following N.B. i)]
. For arbitrary
u
,
u^{!}
is compatible with tensor product in the sense that

u^{!}(-\widetilde{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Q}}}-)\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}u^{!}(-)\widetilde{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}u^{!}(-),

see
[
Car15
, Proposition 2.1.9]
. If

\textstyle{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{u^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{Q}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{Q}}

is a Cartesian diagram of smooth formal schemes, with
u
proper, then there is a natural base change isomorphism

f^{!}u_{+}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}u^{\prime}_{+}f^{\prime!}.

Indeed, the general case can be reduced to the two special cases where
f
is either smooth or a closed immersion. When
f
is smooth this is
[
Car04
, Proposition 3.1.8]
, and when
f
is a closed immersion it amounts to proving that

{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Q^{\prime}}\circ u_{+}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}u_{+}\circ{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{u^{-1}(Q^{\prime})}

which was shown in
[
Car04
, Théorème 2.2.17]
.

4.2.
Cohomological formalism of overholonomic
\boldsymbol{\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}}
-modules: pairs and varieties

To obtain a theory for pairs and varieties, Caro makes the following definition.

4.2.1 Definition
.

Let
(X,Y,\mathfrak{P})
be a frame, with
\mathfrak{P}
smooth. We say that
\mathcal{M}\in{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol}}(\mathfrak{P})
is supported on
X
if there exists an isomorphism

\mathcal{M}\cong\mathbf{R}\underline{\Gamma}_{X}^{\dagger}\mathcal{M}.

Let

{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X,Y,\mathfrak{P})\subset{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})

denote the full subcategory of objects supported on
X
.

4.2.2 Remark
.

(1)

Note that if
X
is closed, then the natural direction of this isomorphism is
\mathbf{R}\underline{\Gamma}_{X}^{\dagger}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}
, and if
X
is open it is
\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathbf{R}\underline{\Gamma}_{X}^{\dagger}\mathcal{M}
.

(2)

If
\mathcal{M}\in{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})
is any object, then
\mathbf{R}\underline{\Gamma}_{X}^{\dagger}\mathcal{M}
is supported on
X
, since
\mathbf{R}\underline{\Gamma}_{X}^{\dagger}
is idempotent.

These categories admit similar cohomological operations to the versions for formal schemes, although for a theory that is provably independent of
\mathfrak{P}
, it is necessary to work with l.p. frames.

4.2.3 Proposition
.

Let

\textstyle{Y^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{P}}

be a morphism of l.p. frames such that
g
is proper and
u
is smooth. Then the functors
u_{+}
and
\mathbf{R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{X}\circ u^{!}
induce inverse equivalences of categories

\mathbf{R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{X}\circ u^{!}\colon{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol}}(X,Y,\mathfrak{P})\leftrightarrows{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol}}(X,Y^{\prime},\mathfrak{P}^{\prime})\colon u_{+}.

Proof.

This is
[
Car12
, Lemma 2.5]
.
∎

It follows that
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X,Y,\mathfrak{P})
depends only on the pair
(X,Y)
, and not on the choice of l.p. frame
(X,Y,\mathfrak{P})
enclosing it, and may therefore be denoted
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X,Y)
. Similarly, if
Y
is proper, then
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X,Y,\mathfrak{P})
only depends on
X
, and is this denoted
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X)
. Again, let me repeat an earlier warning that
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X)
consists of ‘overconvergent’ objects on
X
, the analogous category of ‘convergent’ objects on
X
would be denoted
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X,X)
.

I now explain how the cohomological operations on
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X,Y)
are defined, following
[
AC18
, §1]
. First of all, there are the dual and tensor product functors

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{X}\colon

\displaystyle{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X,Y)^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X,Y)

\displaystyle-\widetilde{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}-\colon

\displaystyle{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X,Y)\times{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X,Y)\rightarrow{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X,Y)

which are defined by taking an l.p. frame
(X,Y,\mathfrak{P})
and setting

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{X}

\displaystyle:=\mathbf{R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{X}\circ\mathbf{D}_{\mathfrak{P}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}{\widetilde{\otimes}}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{N}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{M}{\widetilde{\otimes}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}\mathcal{N}.

The resulting functors only depend on
(X,Y)
up to canonical isomorphism
[
AC18
, §1.1.6]
. Note that the notation here only refers to
X
and not the full pair
(X,Y)
.

If
(f,g)\colon(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime})\rightarrow(X,Y)
is a morphism of (strongly realisable) pairs then there are functors

f^{!},f^{+}:{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X,Y)\rightarrow{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime}),

and if
g
is proper there are functors

f_{!},f_{+}:{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime})\rightarrow{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X,Y).

These are defined as follows: choose a morphism

(f,g,u)\colon(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime},\mathfrak{P}^{\prime})\rightarrow(X,Y,\mathfrak{P})

of l.p. frames extending
u
, and set

\displaystyle f^{!}:=\mathbf{R}\underline{\Gamma}_{X^{\prime}}^{\dagger}\circ u^{!},\;\;

\displaystyle\;\;f^{+}=\mathbf{D}_{X^{\prime}}\circ f^{!}\circ\mathbf{D}_{X}

\displaystyle f_{+}=u_{+}\;\;

\displaystyle\;\;f_{!}=\mathbf{D}_{X}\circ f_{+}\circ\mathbf{D}_{X^{\prime}}.

There is a morphism of functors

f_{!}\rightarrow f_{+}

which is an isomorphism whenever
f
(as well as
g
) is proper
[
AC18
, §1.1.9]
. Again, let me emphasize that these functors are all defined for morphisms of (strongly realisable) pairs, even if the notation only refers to the morphism
f\colon X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
. There is also the compatibility

f^{!}(-\widetilde{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}-)\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}f^{!}(-)\widetilde{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}}f^{!}(-)

as in
[
AC18
, §1.1.9]
, as well as the projection formula

f_{+}(f^{!}(-)\widetilde{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}}(-))\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}(-)\widetilde{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}f_{+}(-)

by
[
AC18
, Proposition A.6]
Both
(f^{+},f_{+})
and
(f_{!},f^{!})
are adjoint pairs
[
AC18
, Lemma 1.1.10]
, and if

\textstyle{(U^{\prime},V^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(s^{\prime},t^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{(f^{\prime},g^{\prime})}
\textstyle{(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(f,g)}
\textstyle{(U,V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(s,t)}
\textstyle{(X,Y)}

is a Cartesian morphism of pairs, with
g
proper, then by
[
AC18
, Lemma 1.3.10]
there is a natural isomorphism

s^{!}f_{+}\cong f^{\prime}_{+}s^{\prime!}

of functors

{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime})\rightarrow{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(U,V).

There is of course a similar formalism for strongly realisable varieties, obtained by choosing a strongly realisable pair
(X,Y)
with
Y
proper, and setting
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X)={\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X,Y)
.

4.3.
Relation with locally free isocrystals

If
(X,Y,\mathfrak{P})
is a frame, with
\mathfrak{P}
smooth over
\mathcal{V}
and
X
smooth over
k
, then Caro defined in
[
Car11
]
a fully faithful functor

\mathrm{sp}_{X+}\colon\mathbf{Isoc}_{F}(X,Y)\rightarrow{\bf D}^{b}(\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}),

and it is the main result of
[
CT12
]
that this lands inside
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})
. In fact, for reasons explained in
[
AL22
]
(see also §
10
below) I will want to rename this functor
\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{X+}
, and then define

\mathrm{sp}_{X+}:=\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{X+}[-\dim X]

to be the shifted version of Caro’s functor. Caro also showed in
[
Car11
]
that
\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{X+}
is compatible with duality and pullback, in the following sense.

For compatibility with duality, let
(-)^{\vee}
denote the dual functor for locally free isocrystals. It was proved in
[
Car11
, Proposition 5.2.7]
that there is a natural isomorphism

\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{X+}\circ(-)^{\vee}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{X}\circ{\bf D}_{\mathfrak{P}}\circ\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{X+}.

Note that if
(X,Y,\mathfrak{P})
is an l.p. frame, then
{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{X}\circ{\bf D}_{\mathfrak{P}}
is precisely the definition of
{\bf D}_{X}
, but I do not want to make this assumption on the frame here. Compatibility with duality might therefore be slightly abusively written as

\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{X+}\circ(-)^{\vee}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}{\bf D}_{X}\circ\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{X+}.

For compatibility with pullback, suppose that

\textstyle{X^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{Y^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{P}}

is a morphism of frames, with
\mathfrak{P},\mathfrak{P}^{\prime},X
and
X^{\prime}
all smooth. Thanks to the compatibility with duality already quoted, it was proved in
[
Car11
, Proposition 4.2.4]
, that there is a natural isomorphism

{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{X^{\prime}}\circ u^{!}\circ\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{X+}[\dim X]\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{X^{\prime}+}\circ f^{*}[\dim X^{\prime}].

Again, if both of these frames are l.p. frames, then
{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{X^{\prime}}\circ u^{!}
is precisely the definition of
f^{!}
, but, again, I don’t want to make this assumption here.

The following definition of the ‘dual’ of
\mathrm{sp}_{+}
was given in
[
AL22
]
.

4.3.1 Definition
.

Let
(X,Y,\mathfrak{P})
be a frame with
\mathfrak{P}
smooth over
\mathcal{V}
and
X
smooth over
k
. Then define

\mathrm{sp}_{X!}:=\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{X+}[\dim X]={\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{X}\circ{\bf D}_{\mathfrak{P}}\circ\mathrm{sp}_{X+}\circ(-)^{\vee}.

4.3.2 Remark
.

it may seem rather odd to have defined both
\mathrm{sp}_{+}
and
\mathrm{sp}_{!}
separately as shifts of Caro’s functor
\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{+}
. The point is that the definitions are only this simple when
X
is smooth. Both
\mathrm{sp}_{+}
and
\mathrm{sp}_{!}
generalise to the case when
X
is not necessarily smooth, however, they are no longer just shifts of one another. Instead, it is the duality relation between
\mathrm{sp}_{+}
and
\mathrm{sp}_{!}
which persists. The analogy to bear in mind is that of a lisse
\ell
-adic sheaf
\mathscr{F}
on
X
. If
X
is smooth, then the Verdier dual
{\bf D}_{X}(\mathscr{F})
is just a shift of
\mathscr{F}^{\vee}
, however, this is no longer necessarily the case when
X
is singular.

Thus compatibility with pullbacks can be (again, slightly abusively) written as

f^{!}\circ\mathrm{sp}_{X!}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\mathrm{sp}_{X^{\prime}!}\circ f^{*}.

As expected, everything in
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X,Y,\mathfrak{P})
is generically in the essential image of
\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{X+}
.

4.3.3 Proposition
.

Let
\mathfrak{P}
be a smooth formal scheme, and
\mathcal{M}\in{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})
supported on a reduced closed subscheme
Y\hookrightarrow P
. Then there exists a divisor
D\subset P
such that
X:=Y\setminus D
is smooth, non-empty, and each
\mathcal{H}^{q}(\mathcal{M}(^{\dagger}D))
is in the essential image of

\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{X+}\colon\mathbf{Isoc}_{F}(X,Y)\rightarrow{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P}).

4.3.4 Remark
.

Implicit in the statement is the fact that
\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{X+}
lands in the category of
\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}
-modules, not just complexes. Also note that since
D
is a divisor, the functor
(^{\dagger}D)
is exact for the natural t-structure on
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol}}(\mathfrak{P})
, and in fact
\mathcal{H}^{q}(\mathcal{M}(^{\dagger}D))\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}(^{\dagger}D)\otimes_{\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}}\mathcal{H}^{q}(\mathcal{M})
. It’s also worth pointing out that
Y
is not assumed to be irreducible, but
X
need not necessarily be dense in
Y
(just non-empty).

Proof.

This follows from
[
Car11
, Lemme 6.2.1]
.
∎

This can be used to deduce the following important ‘conservativity’ result for extraordinary stalks.

4.3.5 Proposition
.

Let
\mathfrak{P}
be a smooth formal scheme, and
\mathcal{M}\in{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})
. If
{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{x}\mathcal{M}=0
for all closed points
x\in P
, then
\mathcal{M}=0
.

Proof.

Let
Y
denote the support of
\mathcal{M}
(in the set-theoretic sense), and choose
D
as in Proposition
4.3.3
above. By Noetherian induction on
Y
, I can assume that
\mathbf{R}\underline{\Gamma}_{D}^{\dagger}\mathcal{M}=0
, or in other words that
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}(^{\dagger}D)
.

Set
\mathfrak{U}:=\mathfrak{P}\setminus D
. Since
\mathbf{R}\underline{\Gamma}_{D}^{\dagger}\mathcal{M}=0
, it suffices to show that
\mathcal{M}\!\!\mid_{\mathfrak{U}}=0
. Indeed, if
\mathcal{M}\!\!\mid_{\mathfrak{U}}=0
, then
\mathcal{M}
is supported on
D
, in which case
\mathcal{M}=\mathbf{R}\underline{\Gamma}_{D}^{\dagger}\mathcal{M}=0
. After replacing
\mathfrak{P}
with
\mathfrak{U}
, I can therefore assume that the support
Y
of
\mathcal{M}
is smooth, and that each
\mathcal{H}^{q}(\mathcal{M})
is in the essential image of

\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{Y+}\colon\mathbf{Isoc}_{F}(Y,Y)\rightarrow{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P}),

say
\mathcal{H}^{q}(\mathcal{M})=\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{Y+}\mathscr{F}_{q}
.
If
i_{x}:x\rightarrow Y
denotes the inclusion of a closed point of
Y
, then

{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{x}\circ\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{Y+}[\dim Y]\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{x+}\circ i_{x}^{*}.

Hence the fact that
{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{x}\mathcal{M}=0
implies that
i_{x}^{*}\mathscr{F}_{q}=0
for all
q
, and all closed points
x\in Y
. Therefore
\mathscr{F}_{q}=0
, and so
\mathcal{H}^{q}(\mathcal{M})=0
as required.
∎

4.4.
t-structures on overholonomic
\boldsymbol{\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}}
-modules

For any prime
\ell\neq p
, and any variety
X
, there are two well-known t-structures on the triangulated category
{\bf D}^{b}_{c}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
of bounded constructible complexes of
\ell
-adic sheaves on
X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
: the ordinary t-structure, with heart the category
\mathbf{Con}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
of constructible
\ell
-adic sheaves on
X
, and the perverse t-structure on
{\bf D}^{b}_{c}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
, with heart the category
\mathbf{Perv}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
of perverse sheaves on
X
.

The perverse t-structure is self dual under the Verdier duality functor
{\bf D}_{X}
, however, the constructible t-structure is not. This means that there is a third natural t-structure on
{\bf D}^{b}_{c}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
, the dual constructible t-structure, whose heart
\mathbf{DCon}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
is canonically anti-equivalent to
\mathbf{Con}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
, via
\mathbf{D}_{X}
. This third t-structure appears less often in the literature than the other two, and is, in a sense, of lesser importance, since most of its relevant properties can be deduced from that of the constructible t-structure by dualising. However, it will be important for us, since it is the analogue of the
dual constructible
t-structure on
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}
that will match up with the natural t-structure on
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}
.

These three t-structures all have analogues in the world of overholonomic
\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}
-modules, which I will now describe in the case of a smooth formal scheme
\mathfrak{P}
.

4.4.1.
Holonomic t-structure

Let
\mathfrak{P}
be a smooth formal scheme. The first t-structure on
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})
is simply the natural one coming from the inclusion
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})\hookrightarrow{\bf D}^{b}(\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}})
. That this is indeed a t-structure follows from the fact that a complex of
\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}
-modules is overholonomic iff its cohomology sheaves are. We denote by
{\bf D}^{\geq q},{\bf D}^{\leq q}
the full subcategories of objects concentrated in degrees
\geq q
and
\leq q
respectively, and by
\tau^{\geq q},\tau^{\leq q}
the truncation functors. By combining
[
Car09b
, Proposition 3.3 4)]
with
[
Vir00
, §4]
, we see that the holonomic t-structure is self-dual, in the sense that

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\in{\bf D}^{\geq 0}

\displaystyle\iff\mathbf{D}_{X}\mathcal{M}\in{\bf D}^{\leq 0}.

I will denote the heart of the holonomic t-structure by
\mathbf{Hol}_{F}(\mathfrak{P})
, and refer to its objects as holonomic modules on
\mathfrak{P}
. Cohomology functors will be denoted by

\mathcal{H}^{q}:{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})\rightarrow\mathbf{Hol}_{F}(\mathfrak{P}).

These are just the restriction to
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})
of the natural cohomology functors

\mathcal{H}^{q}\colon{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}})\rightarrow\mathbf{Mod}(\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}).

4.4.2.
Constructible t-structure

The constructible t-structure on
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})
is analogous to the t-structure on the derived category
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{rh}}(\mathscr{D}_{X})
of regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}
-modules on a smooth complex variety
X
induced by the ordinary t-structure on
{\bf D}^{b}_{c}(X^{\mathrm{an}},{\mathbb{C}})
via the (covariant) Riemann–Hilbert correspondence

\mathcal{M}\mapsto\omega_{X^{\mathrm{an}}/{\mathbb{C}}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathscr{D}_{X^{\mathrm{an}}}}\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{an}}\cong\Omega^{\bullet}_{X^{\mathrm{an}}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{\mathrm{an}}}}\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{an}}[\dim X].

For overholonomic
\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}
-modules, this t-structure was defined for curves in
[
LS14
]
and in general in
[
Abe18
]
. Concretely, if
\mathcal{M}\in\mathbf{Hol}_{F}(\mathfrak{P})
, define the support
\mathrm{Supp}(\mathcal{M})
of
\mathcal{M}
to be the smallest closed subscheme
Z\subset P
such that
\mathcal{M}|_{P\setminus Z}=0
. Then define a pair of subcategories
({}^{\rm c}{\bf D}^{\geq 0},{}^{\rm c}{\bf D}^{\leq 0})
of
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\in{}^{\mathrm{c}}{\bf D}^{\geq 0}

\displaystyle\iff\dim\mathrm{Supp}\,\mathcal{H}^{n}(\mathcal{M})\leq n\;\;\forall n\geq 0,\;\;\mathcal{H}^{n}(\mathcal{M})=0\;\;\forall n<0

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\in{}^{\mathrm{c}}{\bf D}^{\leq 0}

\displaystyle\iff\mathcal{H}^{-n}({\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Z}{\bf D}_{\mathfrak{P}}\mathcal{M})=0\text{ for any closed subscheme }Z\hookrightarrow P\text{ with }\dim Z<n.

I will postpone for now the proof that this is a t-structure, since I will deduce it from the corresponding claim for the dual constructible t-structure, which I will introduce next.

4.4.3.
Dual constructible t-structure

Whereas the holonomic t-structure is self-dual, the constructible t-structure is
not
. There is therefore a third t-structure
({}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\geq 0},{}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\leq 0})
on
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})
by setting

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\in{}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\geq 0}

\displaystyle\iff\mathbf{D}_{\mathfrak{P}}\mathcal{M}\in{}^{{\rm c}}{\bf D}^{\leq 0}

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\in{}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\leq 0}

\displaystyle\iff\mathbf{D}_{\mathfrak{P}}\mathcal{M}\in{}^{{\rm c}}{\bf D}^{\geq 0}.

Explicitly,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\in{}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\geq 0}

\displaystyle\iff\mathcal{H}^{-n}({\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Z}\mathcal{M})=0,\;\;\forall Z\hookrightarrow P,\;\;\dim Z<n

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\in{}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\leq 0}

\displaystyle\iff\dim\mathrm{Supp}\,\mathcal{H}^{-n}(\mathcal{M})\leq n\;\;\forall n\geq 0,\;\;\mathcal{H}^{-n}(\mathcal{M})=0\;\;\forall n<0.

Note that the condition for
\mathcal{M}
to lie in
{}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\geq 0}
may be tested on irreducible
Z
. To prove that this is indeed a t-structure, I follow the approach of
[
Abe18
, Proposition 1.3.3]
.

4.4.4 Lemma
.

Let
Y\rightarrow P
be a closed subscheme. Then the functors
(^{\dagger}Y)
and
{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Y}
both preserve
{}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\geq 0}
and
{}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\leq 0}
.

Proof.

The claims for
{}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\geq 0}
are relatively straightforward. Indeed, the fact that
{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Y}
preserves
{}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\geq 0}
simply follows from the fact that
{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Z}{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Y}={\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Y\cap Z}
, and
\dim Y\cap Z\leq\dim Z
.

For
(^{\dagger}Y)
, take
Z\hookrightarrow P
irreducible with
\dim Z<n
,
\mathcal{M}\in{}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\geq 0}
, and consider the exact sequence

\mathcal{H}^{-n}(\mathbf{R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Z}\mathcal{M})\rightarrow\mathcal{H}^{-n}(\mathbf{R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Z}\mathcal{M}(^{\dagger}Y))\rightarrow\mathcal{H}^{-n+1}(\mathbf{R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Z\cap Y}\mathcal{M}).

The left hand term here is zero, I need to show that so is the right hand term. If
\dim Z\cap Y<\dim Z
, then
\dim Z\cap Y<n-1
, and thus
\mathcal{H}^{-n}(\mathbf{R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Z}\mathcal{M}(^{\dagger}Y))=0
. Since
Z
is irreducible, the only way that this can fail to happen is if
Z\subset Y
, in which case
\mathbf{R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Z}\mathcal{M}(^{\dagger}Y)=0
.

The hardest part is to prove that
{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Y}
preserves
{}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\leq 0}
. To show this, write
Y
as an intersection of divisors, this reduces to the case when
Y
itself is a divisor. In this case, the functor
(^{\dagger}Y)
is t-exact for the ordinary t-structure, and hence
{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Y}
has cohomological amplitude
[0,1]
. Write
\mathcal{H}^{i,\dagger}_{Y}
for the cohomology sheaves of
{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}
. If
\mathcal{M}\in{}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\leq 0}
, it then follows that
\mathcal{H}^{-n}({\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Y}\mathcal{M})=0
whenever
n<-1
. When
n\geq-1
, there is an exact sequence

0\rightarrow\mathcal{H}^{1,\dagger}_{Y}(\mathcal{H}^{-(n+1)}(\mathcal{M}))\rightarrow\mathcal{H}^{-n}({\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{X}\mathcal{M})\rightarrow\mathcal{H}^{0,\dagger}_{Y}(\mathcal{H}^{-n}(\mathcal{M}))\rightarrow 0.

Since
\mathcal{M}\in{}^{\rm dc}{\bf D}^{\leq 0}
, we know that

\dim\mathrm{Supp}\,\mathcal{H}^{-n}(\mathcal{M})\leq n,\;\;\;\;\dim\mathrm{Supp}\,\mathcal{H}^{-(n+1)}(\mathcal{M}))\leq n+1

and hence trivially

\dim\mathrm{Supp}\,\mathcal{H}^{0,\dagger}_{Y}(\mathcal{H}^{-n}(\mathcal{M}))\leq n,\;\;\;\;\dim\mathrm{Supp}\,\mathcal{H}^{1,\dagger}_{Y}(\mathcal{H}^{-(n+1)}(\mathcal{M}))\leq n+1.

What is needed is to show that

\dim\mathrm{Supp}\,\mathcal{H}^{1,\dagger}_{Y}(\mathcal{H}^{-(n+1)}(\mathcal{M}))\leq n

(which should be interpreted as saying that
\mathcal{H}^{1,\dagger}_{Y}(\mathcal{H}^{0}(\mathcal{M}))=0
when
n=-1
). It is therefore enough to show that if
\mathcal{N}\in\mathbf{Hol}_{F}(\mathfrak{P})
, then

\dim\mathrm{Supp}(\mathcal{N})\leq n+1\implies\dim\mathrm{Supp}\,\mathcal{H}^{1,\dagger}_{Y}(\mathcal{N})\leq n,

for all
n\geq-1
(again, meaning that
\mathcal{H}^{1,\dagger}_{Y}(\mathcal{N})
=0 when
n=-1
). To prove this, write
\mathrm{Supp}(\mathcal{N})=Z_{1}\cup Z_{2}
, where
Z_{1}\subset Y
, and no irreducible component of
Z_{2}
is contained in
Y
. Then
\dim Y\cap Z_{2}\leq n
, and I claim that
\mathcal{H}^{1,\dagger}_{Y}(\mathcal{N})
is supported on
Y\cap Z_{2}
. Indeed,
\mathcal{H}^{1,\dagger}_{Y}(\mathcal{N})
is clearly supported on
Y
, so it will suffice to show that it is zero on
P\setminus Z_{2}
. But after restricting to
P\setminus Z_{2}
, we have
\mathrm{Supp}(\mathcal{N})\subset Y
, whence
\mathbf{R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Y}\mathcal{N}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{N}
, and so
\mathcal{H}^{1,\dagger}_{Y}(\mathcal{N})=\mathcal{H}^{1}(\mathcal{N})=0
.

Finally, the fact that
(^{\dagger}Y)
preserves
{}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\leq 0}
now follows from taking cohomology sheaves of the exact triangle

\mathbf{R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Y}\rightarrow\mathrm{id}\rightarrow(^{\dagger}Y)\overset{+1}{\longrightarrow}

and using the already proved result for
\mathbf{R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Y}
.
∎

4.4.5 Theorem
.

The pair of full subcategories
({}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\geq 0},{}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\leq 0})
defines a t-structure on
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})
.

Proof.

To set up a Noetherian induction, I will actually prove that
({}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\geq 0},{}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\leq 0})
defines a t-structure on
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(Y,Y,\mathfrak{P})\subset{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})
for any closed subscheme
Y\hookrightarrow P
, which I may as well assume to be reduced.

I first reduce to the case that
Y
is irreducible. Indeed, if
Y=Y_{1}\cup Y_{2}
is a union of proper, non-empty closed subschemes, then any
\mathcal{M}\in{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(Y,Y,\mathfrak{P})
sits in an exact triangle

{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Y_{1}\cap Y_{2}}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Y_{1}}\mathcal{M}\oplus{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Y_{2}}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}\overset{+1}{\rightarrow}.

Assuming that
({}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\geq 0},{}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\leq 0})
defines a t-structure on
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(Y_{i},Y_{i},\mathfrak{P})
for
i=1,2
, I can check the axioms
[
BBD82
, Définition 1.3.1]
explicitly as follows. First of all (ii) is clear, and (i) follows from the fact that if two out of the three morphisms in a morphism of exact triangles are zero, then so is the third. For (iii), I can appeal to
[
BBD82
, Proposition 1.1.11]
to form the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\tau_{\leq 0}{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Y_{1}\cap Y_{2}}\mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\tau_{\leq 0}{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Y_{1}}\mathcal{M}\oplus\tau_{\leq 0}{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Y_{2}}\mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{+1}
\textstyle{{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Y_{1}\cap Y_{2}}\mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Y_{1}}\mathcal{M}\oplus{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Y_{2}}\mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{+1}
\textstyle{\tau_{>0}{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Y_{1}\cap Y_{2}}\mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{+1}
\textstyle{\tau_{>0}{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Y_{1}}\mathcal{M}\oplus\tau_{>0}{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Y_{2}}\mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{+1}
\textstyle{\mathcal{N}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{+1}
\scriptstyle{+1}

with all rows and columns exact triangles. It follows directly from the definitions that
\mathcal{L}\in{}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\leq 0}
. Since the condition to lie in
{}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{>0}
can be checked on irreducible closed subschemes
Z\hookrightarrow P
, the fact that
\mathcal{N}\in{}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{>0}
follows from the fact that both
\underline{{\bf R}}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{Y_{1}}\mathcal{N}
and
\underline{{\bf R}}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{Y_{2}}\mathcal{N}
are in
{}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{>0}
.

I may therefore assume that
Y
is irreducible. In this case, take a divisor
D\subset P
, with smooth, non-empty complement
X=Y\setminus D
on
Y
, and define

T(Y,D):=\left\{\left.\mathcal{M}\in{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(Y,Y,\mathfrak{P})\,\right|\,\mathcal{H}^{q}((^{\dagger}D)\mathcal{M})\in\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{+}(\mathbf{Isoc}_{F}(X,Y))\;\;\forall q\right\}.

Then by Proposition
4.3.3
,

{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(Y,Y,\mathfrak{P})=\underset{D}{\mathrm{colim}}\,T(Y,D),

and it therefore suffices to show that
({}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\geq 0},{}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\leq 0})
defines a t-structure on
T(Y,D)
. Now set set
Z:=(Y\cap D)_{\mathrm{red}}
, by Noetherian induction I can assume that
({}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\geq 0},{}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\leq 0})
defines a t-structure on
T(Z):={\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(Z,Z,\mathfrak{P})
. I now set
T(X):=T(Y,D)\cap{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X,Y,\mathfrak{P})
, thus

\displaystyle(^{\dagger}D)=(^{\dagger}Z)

\displaystyle\colon T(Y,D)\rightarrow T(X)

\displaystyle{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{D}={\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Z}

\displaystyle\colon T(Y,D)\rightarrow T(Z),

and the localisation triangle

{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}_{D}^{\dagger}\rightarrow\mathrm{id}\rightarrow(^{\dagger}D)\overset{+1}{\rightarrow},

together with Lemma
4.4.4
, shows that
\mathcal{M}\in T(Y,D)
is in
{}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\geq 0}
or
{}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\leq 0}
if and only if both
{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}_{D}^{\dagger}\mathcal{M}
and
\mathcal{M}(^{\dagger}D)
are. Moreover, since
D
is a divisor, and
X
is smooth, it follows that on
T(X)
,
({}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\geq 0},{}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\leq 0})
is simply the shift of the ordinary t-structure
({\bf D}^{\geq 0},{\bf D}^{\leq 0})
by the dimension of
X
, and therefore defines a t-structure on
T(X)
.

I now check the axioms
[
BBD82
, Définition 1.3.1]
. Of course (ii) is straightforward. To prove (i), the fact that
({}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\geq 0},{}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\leq 0})
defines a t-structure on both
T(X)
and
T(Z)
means that I can apply the localisation triangle

{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}_{D}^{\dagger}\rightarrow\mathrm{id}\rightarrow(^{\dagger}D)\overset{+1}{\rightarrow},

twice to reduce to showing that

\mathrm{Hom}({\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{D}\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N}(^{\dagger}D))=0,\;\;\;\;\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{M}(^{\dagger}D),{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{D}\mathcal{N})=0

whenever
\mathcal{M}\in T(Y,D)\cap{}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{<0}
and
\mathcal{N}\in T(Y,D)\cap{}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\geq 0}
. The first is straightforward, since

\mathrm{Hom}({\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{D}\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N}(^{\dagger}D))=\mathrm{Hom}({\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{D}\mathcal{M},{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{D}\mathcal{N}(^{\dagger}D))=\mathrm{Hom}({\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{D}\mathcal{M},0)=0.

For the second, note that
\mathcal{M}(^{\dagger}D)\in T(X)\cap{}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{<0}
, and hence
\mathcal{H}^{n}(\mathcal{M}(^{\dagger}D))=0
if
n\geq-\dim X
. On the other hand,
{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{D}\mathcal{N}\in T(Z)\cap{}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\geq 0}
, and hence
\mathcal{H}^{n}({\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{D}\mathcal{N})=0
if
n<-\dim Z
. Thus
\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{M}(^{\dagger}D),{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{D}\mathcal{N})=0
as required.

Finally, to prove (iii), I consider, for any
\mathcal{M}\in T(Y,D)
, the shifted localisation triangle

\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}(^{\dagger}D)\rightarrow{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{D}\mathcal{M}[1]\overset{+1}{\rightarrow}.

Since
({}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\geq 0},{}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\leq 0})
defines a t-structure on both
T(X)
and
T(Z)
, I can extend this to a diagram

(4.4.1)

\textstyle{\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{M}(^{\dagger}D)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\left(\tau_{\leq 0}{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{D}\mathcal{M}\right)[1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}(^{\dagger}D)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{D}\mathcal{M}[1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{+1}
\textstyle{\tau_{>0}\mathcal{M}(^{\dagger}D)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{+1}
\textstyle{\left(\tau_{>0}{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{D}\mathcal{M}\right)[1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{+1}

Now consider the morphism

(4.4.2)

\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{M}(^{\dagger}D)\rightarrow\left(\tau_{>0}{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{D}\mathcal{M}\right)[1]

Since
\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{M}(^{\dagger}D)\in{}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\leq 0}\cap T(X)
, it follows that

\mathcal{H}^{n}(\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{M}(^{\dagger}D))=0,\;\;\;\;\forall n>-\dim X

On the other hand, since
(\tau_{>0}{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{D}\mathcal{M})[1]\in{}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\geq 0}\cap T(Z)
it follows that

\mathcal{H}^{n}((\tau_{>0}{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{D}\mathcal{M})[1])=0,\;\;\;\;\forall n<-\dim Z.

Since
Y
is irreducible,
\dim Z<\dim X
, and it immediately follows that

{\rm Hom}(\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{M}(^{\dagger}D),(\tau_{>0}{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{D}\mathcal{M})[1])=0.

Hence the diagram (
4.4.1
) can be completed (and rotated) to obtain a diagram

\textstyle{\tau_{\leq 0}{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{D}\mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{M}(^{\dagger}D)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{+1}
\textstyle{{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{D}\mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}(^{\dagger}D)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{+1}
\textstyle{\tau_{>0}{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{D}\mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{+1}
\textstyle{\mathcal{N}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{+1}
\textstyle{\tau_{>0}\mathcal{M}(^{\dagger}D)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{+1}
\scriptstyle{+1}

with all rows and columns exact triangles. Then
\mathcal{L}\in{}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{\leq 0}
and
\mathcal{N}\in{}^{\mathrm{dc}}{\bf D}^{>0}
, completing the proof.
∎

It follows that the pair
({}^{\rm c}{\bf D}^{\geq 0},{}^{\rm c}{\bf D}^{\leq 0})
also defines a t-structure on
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})
. I will denote the hearts by of these two t-structures by
\mathbf{Con}_{F}(\mathfrak{P})
and
\mathbf{DCon}_{F}(\mathfrak{P})
, and cohomology functors by
{}^{\rm c}\mathcal{H}^{q}
and
{}^{\rm dc}\mathcal{H}^{q}
. The truncation functors will be
{}^{\rm c}\tau
and
{}^{\rm dc}\tau
, and objects of the hearts will be called constructible and dual constructible modules on
\mathfrak{P}
respectively.

4.4.6 Example
.

Let
(X,Y,\mathfrak{P})
be a frame, with
\mathfrak{P}
and
X
smooth, and suppose
\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{Isoc}_{F}(X,Y)
. Then
\mathrm{sp}_{!}\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{DCon}_{F}(\mathfrak{P})
. If there exists a divisor
D\subset\mathfrak{P}
such that
X=Y\setminus D
, then
\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{+}\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{Hol}_{F}(\mathfrak{P})
. If
X=Y
then
\mathrm{sp}_{+}\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{Con}_{F}(\mathfrak{P})
.

As part of the proof that
({}^{\rm dc}{\bf D}^{\geq 0},{}^{\rm dc}{\bf D}^{\leq 0})
is indeed a t-structure, we saw that the functors
{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Y}
and
(^{\dagger}Y)
for a closed subscheme
Y\hookrightarrow P
are t-exact for the dual constructible t-structure, I will abbreviate this as being ‘dct-exact’. If
u\colon\mathfrak{P}\rightarrow\mathfrak{Q}
is a closed immersion of smooth formal schemes, it is easy to check that
u_{+}
is dct-exact.

4.4.7 Proposition
.

Let
u\colon\mathfrak{P}\rightarrow\mathfrak{Q}
be any morphism of smooth formal schemes. Then
u^{!}
is dct-exact.

4.4.8 Remark
.

This is the dual of the fact that
u^{+}
is t-exact for the constructible t-structure.

Proof.

If
u
is a closed immersion, then
u_{+}u^{!}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}{\bf R}{\underline{\Gamma}}^{\dagger}_{P}
. Hence the dct-exactness of
u^{!}
follows from that of
{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{P}
and
u_{+}
. If
K^{\prime}\subset K^{\prime\prime}
are finite unramified extensions of
K
, with rings of integers
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{V}^{\prime\prime}
, then the proposition clearly holds for the morphism

u\colon\mathrm{Spf}\left(\mathcal{V}^{\prime\prime}\right)\rightarrow\mathrm{Spf}\left(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\right)
. Hence the proposition holds whenever
u
is a
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
-valued point of
\mathfrak{Q}
, for any such
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
.

But using Proposition
4.3.5
, the general case follows from the particular case of
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
-valued points for
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}/\mathcal{V}
unramified. Indeed, taking
\mathcal{M}\in{}^{\rm dc}{\bf D}^{\geq 0}
(resp.
\mathcal{M}\in{}^{\rm dc}{\bf D}^{\leq 0}
), to prove that
u^{!}\mathcal{M}\in{}^{\rm dc}{\bf D}^{\geq 0}
(resp.
\mathcal{M}\in{}^{\rm dc}{\bf D}^{\leq 0}
), it suffices to show that
{}^{\rm dc}\tau_{<0}u^{!}\mathcal{M}=0
(resp.
{}^{\rm dc}\tau_{>0}u^{!}\mathcal{M}=0
). But now, taking any
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
-valued point
i\colon\mathrm{Spf}\left(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\right)\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}
, I can just calculate

i^{!}{}^{\rm dc}\tau_{<0}u^{!}\mathcal{M}={}^{\rm dc}\tau_{<0}u(i)^{!}\mathcal{M}=u(i)^{!}{}^{\rm dc}\tau_{<0}\mathcal{M}=0

(resp.
i^{!}{}^{\rm dc}\tau_{>0}u^{!}\mathcal{M}=0
), and therefore conclude using Proposition
4.3.5
.
∎

A similar method to Proposition
4.4.7
can be used to prove that
\widetilde{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}
is dct-exact. Indeed, dct-exactness can be checked after taking extraordinary stalks, and
\widetilde{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}
commutes with extraordinary pullback. This therefore reduces to the trivial case when
\mathfrak{P}=\mathrm{Spf}\left(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\right)
for
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}/\mathcal{V}
unramified.

If
u\colon\mathfrak{P}\rightarrow\mathrm{Spf}\left(\mathcal{V}\right)
is the structure morphism, then
\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathfrak{P}}:=u^{!}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{V}{\mathbb{Q}}}\in\mathbf{DCon}_{F}(\mathfrak{P})
. I will call this the
constant
dual constructible module on
\mathfrak{P}
. Be warned that
\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathfrak{P}}
is
not
the
\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}
-module
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}
, instead it is a shift of this module by the dimension of
\mathfrak{P}
. This shifted version of the ‘constant’ module will instead match up with the constant locally free isocrystal on
\mathfrak{P}
via the overconvergent Riemann–Hilbert correspondence.

4.5.
Dual constructible modules on pairs and varieties

For pairs
(X,Y)
, I will only define the dual constructible t-structure. This is done by taking an l.p. frame
(X,Y,\mathfrak{P})
and simply restricting the dual constructible t-structure on
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})
to
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X,Y,\mathfrak{P})
.The heart of this structure will be denoted
\mathbf{DCon}_{F}(X,Y)
, and referred to as the category of dual constructible modules on
(X,Y)
.

4.5.1 Remark
.

The analogous definition is not the correct one for either the holonomic or constructible t-structures, and this fact largely explains why it is the dual constructible t-structure which matches up with the natural one on the category of constructible complexes. In fact, the increasing list of hypotheses in Example
4.4.6
can be removed by replacing
\mathbf{Hol}_{F}(\mathfrak{P})
and
\mathbf{Con}_{F}(\mathfrak{P})
by appropriate analogues for the pair
(X,Y)
.

If
(f,g)\colon(X,Y)\rightarrow(\mathrm{Spec}\left(k\right),\mathrm{Spec}\left(k\right))
denotes the structure morphism, I define

\mathcal{O}_{(X,Y)}^{*}:=f^{!}\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Spf}\left(\mathcal{V}\right){\mathbb{Q}}}

to be the constant dual constructible module on
(X,Y)
. If
(X,Y,\mathfrak{P})
is an l.p. frame, then
\mathcal{O}_{(X,Y)}^{*}=\mathbf{R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{X}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{*}
. If
Y
is proper over
k
, then
\mathcal{O}^{*}_{(X,Y)}\in{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X,Y)={\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X)
is independent of the choice of
Y
, in which case I will write it as
\mathcal{O}^{*}_{X}
. Also be warned that this is
not
the same as Caro’s object defined in
[
Car09b
, §4.23]
. Indeed, his is a constant holonomic module (that is, it lies in the heart of the holonomic t-structure on
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X,Y,\mathfrak{P})
, that we haven’t defined), whereas ours is a constant dual constructible module. Our
\mathcal{O}^{*}_{X}
is the direct analogue of the Verdier dual
{\bf D}_{X}(\underline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell,X})
of the constant sheaf in
\ell
-adic étale cohomology.

4.5.2 Lemma
.

The object
\mathcal{O}^{*}_{(X,Y)}\in{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X,Y)
is a unit for the tensor product
\widetilde{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{(X,Y)}}
.

Proof.

Let
(X,Y,\mathfrak{P})
be an l.p. frame. In the case
X=Y=P
, it is clear that
\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathfrak{P}}
is a unit for the tensor product
\widetilde{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}
. If
X=Y
, then this provides a natural transformation

\mathcal{O}^{*}_{(Y,Y)}\widetilde{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{(Y,Y)}}(-)\rightarrow(-)

of endofunctors of
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(Y,Y)
. To check that this natural transformation is an isomorphism, it suffices to do so on extraordinary stalks, which reduces to the trivial case

(Y,Y)=(\mathrm{Spec}\left(k^{\prime}\right),\mathrm{Spec}\left(k^{\prime}\right))

for
k^{\prime}/k
a finite extension. In general, this in turn induces a natural transformation

(-)\rightarrow\mathcal{O}^{*}_{(X,Y)}\widetilde{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{(X,Y)}}(-)

of endofunctors of
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X,Y)
which is proved to be an isomorphism in the same way.
∎

As in the case of formal schemes, the tensor product
\widetilde{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}
is dct-exact. If
(f,g)\colon(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime})\rightarrow(X,Y)
is a morphism of pairs, then
f^{!}
is always dct-exact. If
g
is a closed immersion, so
f
is a locally closed immersion, then
f_{+}
is dct-exact. In particular, if
(X,Y)
is a pair,

j\colon U\rightarrow X\leftarrow Z\colon i

are complementary open and closed subschemes, and
\mathcal{M}\in\mathbf{DCon}_{F}(X,Y)
, then the localisation triangle

(4.5.1)

i_{+}i^{!}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}\rightarrow j_{+}j^{+}\mathcal{M}\overset{+1}{\rightarrow}

can be viewed as a short exact sequence of dual constructible modules. There is then the following version of dévissage for dual constructible modules.

4.5.3 Proposition
.

Every
\mathcal{M}\in\mathbf{DCon}_{F}(X,Y)
admits a finite composition series

0=\mathcal{M}_{0}\subset\mathcal{M}_{1}\subset\ldots\subset\mathcal{M}_{n}=\mathcal{M},

such that for each
1\leq\alpha\leq n
, there exists a smooth locally closed subscheme
i_{\alpha}\colon X_{\alpha}\rightarrow X
, with closure
\overline{X}_{\alpha}
in
P
, a locally free isocrystal
\mathscr{F}_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{Isoc}_{F}(X_{\alpha},\overline{X}_{\alpha})
, and an isomorphism

\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}/\mathcal{M}_{\alpha-1}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}i_{\alpha+}\mathrm{sp}_{X_{\alpha}!}\mathscr{F}_{\alpha}.

Proof.

Thanks to the localisation exact sequence (
4.5.1
), this follows from Proposition
4.3.3
.
∎

I will end this section with the following
\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}
-module analogue of Theorem
3.4.2
.

4.5.4 Proposition
.

Let

\textstyle{X^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{Y^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y}

be a morphism of strongly realisable pairs, such that
g
is proper,
f
is finite étale, and
X
is smooth. Then the natural morphism
f_{!}\rightarrow f_{+}
of functors
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime})\rightarrow{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(X,Y)
is an isomorphism, and there exists a trace isomorphism

f^{+}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}f^{!}

of functors
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X,Y)\rightarrow{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime})
. All four functors
(f^{+},f_{+},f_{!},f^{!})
are dct-exact.

4.5.5 Remark
.

The hypothesis that
X
is smooth is almost certainly unnecessary, but I will only need the result under this assumption, which makes the proof marginally simpler.

Proof.

That
f_{!}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}f_{+}
follows from the fact that
f
is proper. To construct the trace morphism

f^{+}\rightarrow f^{!},

I will construct the adjoint

\mathrm{id}\rightarrow f_{+}f^{!}.

Indeed, since
\mathcal{O}^{*}_{(X,Y)}
is a unit for the tensor product, the projection formula shows that

f_{+}f^{!}\mathcal{M}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}f_{+}f^{!}\mathcal{O}^{*}_{(X,Y)}\widetilde{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{M},

so it suffices to construct

f_{+}f^{!}\mathcal{O}^{*}_{(X,Y)}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}^{*}_{(X,Y)}.

But now
\mathcal{O}^{*}_{(X,Y)}
clearly extends to an object of the
overconvergent
category
\mathbf{DCon}_{F}(X)\subset{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X)
, and so I can just restrict the trace morphism constructed in
[
Abe18
, §1.5]
from the overconvergent to the partially overconvergent category.

To prove that
f^{+}\rightarrow f^{!}
is an isomorphism,
[
AC18
, Lemma 1.2.3]
shows that I can replace
(X,Y)
by
(X,X)
, in other words I can work in the convergent category. Thus, after localising on
X
, I can assume that it lifts to a smooth formal scheme
\mathfrak{X}
, and that finite étale cover
f\colon X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
lifts to a finite étale cover
u\colon\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathfrak{X}
. In this case, it is a straightforward computation that
u^{!}\circ{\bf D}_{\mathfrak{X}}\cong{\bf D}_{\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}}\circ u^{!}
, and the claim follows.

For the dct-exactness claims, the case of
f^{!}\cong f^{+}
was handled in Proposition
4.4.7
, and the case of
f_{+}\cong f_{!}
then follows because it is both a left and a right adjoint to
f^{!}\cong f^{+}
.
∎

4.5.6 Remark
.

Since
f^{!}=f^{+}
and
f_{!}=f_{+}
, it follows that any
\mathcal{M}\in\mathbf{DCon}_{F}(X,Y)
is a direct summand of
f_{+}f^{+}\mathcal{M}\in\mathbf{DCon}_{F}(X,Y)
, and any
\mathcal{N}\in\mathbf{DCon}_{F}(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime})
is a direct summand of
f^{+}f_{+}\mathcal{N}\in\mathbf{DCon}_{F}(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime})
.

Needless to say, there are analogues of all of the results in §
4.5
with the strongly realisable pair
(X,Y)
replaced by a strongly realisable variety
X
.

5.
Quasi-coherent complexes and rigidification

A key tool in the comparison between
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons}}
and
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol}}
will be a completed version of the module pullback functor along the specialisation morphism

\mathrm{sp}\colon\mathfrak{P}_{K}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}

for
\mathfrak{P}
a flat formal scheme. This will only work for complexes which are quasi-coherent in the sense of Berthelot (I will recall the definition below), and the goal in this section is to describe this construction.

5.1.
Quasi-coherent complexes

Let
\mathfrak{P}
be a flat formal scheme, and set
P_{n}:=\mathfrak{P}\times_{\mathcal{V}}\mathcal{V}/\mathfrak{m}^{n+1}
. Thus
P_{0}=P
. I will write
{\bf D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})
for the derived category of complexes of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}
-modules which are
quasi-coherent
in the sense of
[
Ber02
, §3.2]
. Thus a complex of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}
-modules
\mathcal{M}
is quasi-coherent iff:

(1)

\mathcal{O}_{P_{0}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}\mathcal{M}
is a quasi-coherent complex of
\mathcal{O}_{P_{0}}
-modules;

(2)

the natural map
\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathbf{R}\underset{n}{\mathrm{lim}}\,\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}\mathcal{M}
is an isomorphism in
{\bf D}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})
.

It follows by induction on
n
that
\mathcal{M}_{n}:=\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}\mathcal{M}
is a quasi-coherent complex of
\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}
-modules for all
n
. In fact, Berthelot phrases the definition in terms of the topos
\mathfrak{P}_{\bullet}
of
{\mathbb{N}}
-indexed projective systems of sheaves on
\mathfrak{P}
. This is ringed via the projective system
\mathcal{O}_{P_{\bullet}}=\{\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}\}_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
, and there is a morphism of ringed toposes

l_{\mathfrak{P}}\colon(\mathfrak{P},\mathcal{O}_{P_{\bullet}})\rightarrow(\mathfrak{P},\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}),

where the pushforward functor takes the inverse limit, and the (module) pullback functor tensors over
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}
with
\mathcal{O}_{P_{\bullet}}
. A complex
\mathcal{M}
is then quasi-coherent iff
({\bf L}l^{*}_{\mathfrak{P}}\mathcal{M})_{0}\in{\bf D}(\mathcal{O}_{P_{0}})
is quasi-coherent, and the natural map
\mathcal{M}\rightarrow{\bf R}l_{\mathfrak{P}*}{\bf L}l_{\mathfrak{P}}^{*}\mathcal{M}
is an isomorphism.

5.1.1 Example
.

Any (possibly unbounded) complex with coherent cohomology sheaves is quasi-coherent. Thus
{\bf D}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})\subset{\bf D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})
as a full subcategory.

5.1.2 Remark
.

If
\mathcal{A}
is an
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}
-algebra, a complex of
\mathcal{A}
-modules will be called quasi-coherent if it is so as a complex of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}
-modules. The (derived) category of quasi-coherent complexes of
\mathcal{A}
-modules will be denoted
{\bf D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{A})
, and is viewed as a full subcategory of
{\bf D}(\mathcal{A})
. Note that I do not assume that
\mathcal{A}
is itself quasi-coherent as a complex of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}
-modules. For example, if
\mathfrak{P}
is smooth, I will later want to take
\mathcal{A}=\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(m)}_{\mathfrak{P}}
and
\mathcal{A}=\mathscr{D}^{(m)}_{\mathfrak{P}}
, although of course in this case the forgetful functor
{\bf D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(m)}_{\mathfrak{P}})\rightarrow{\bf D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathscr{D}^{(m)}_{\mathfrak{P}})
is an equivalence.

One important way of constructing quasi-coherent complexes is the following lemma.

5.1.3 Lemma
.

Suppose that
\left\{\mathcal{M}_{n}\right\}_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
is an inverse system of complexes of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}
-modules, such that:

(1)

each
\mathcal{M}_{n}
is quasi-coherent complex of
\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}
-modules;

(2)

for each
n
, the induced map

\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{P_{n+1}}}\mathcal{M}_{n+1}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{n}

is an isomorphism in
{\bf D}(\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}})
.

Then
\mathcal{M}:=\mathbf{R}\underset{n}{\mathrm{lim}}\,\mathcal{M}_{n}\in{\bf D}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})
is quasi-coherent.

Proof.

I need to show that the map

\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{n}

is an isomorphism in
{\bf D}(\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}})
. By tensoring both sides over
\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}
with the exact sequence

0\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{P_{n-1}}\overset{\times\varpi}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{P_{0}}\rightarrow 0

and using condition (2), I can argue by induction on
n
to reduce to the case
n=0
. Since
\mathcal{O}_{P_{0}}
is a perfect complex of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}
-modules, I can then calculate the LHS as

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{P_{0}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}\mathbf{R}\underset{n}{\mathrm{lim}}\,\mathcal{M}_{n}

\displaystyle\cong\mathbf{R}\underset{n}{\mathrm{lim}}\,\left(\mathcal{O}_{P_{0}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}\mathcal{M}_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\cong\mathbf{R}\underset{n}{\mathrm{lim}}\,\left(\mathcal{O}_{P_{0}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}}\mathcal{M}_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\cong\mathbf{R}\underset{n}{\mathrm{lim}}\,\left(\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}\mathcal{M}_{0}\right).

Now each complex
\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}\mathcal{M}_{0}
is quasi-isomorphic to the mapping cone of

\mathcal{M}_{0}\overset{0}{\rightarrow}\mathcal{M}_{0},

and moreover the transition maps
\mathcal{O}_{P_{n+1}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}\mathcal{M}_{0}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}\mathcal{M}_{0}
are realised by the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{M}_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0}
\scriptstyle{0}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}_{0}.}

It therefore follows that

\mathbf{R}\underset{n}{\mathrm{lim}}\,\left(\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}\mathcal{M}_{0}\right)\cong\mathcal{M}_{0}

as required.
∎

Mapping complexes between quasi-coherent complexes have the following straightforward description.

5.1.4 Lemma
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N}\in{\bf D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})
. Then the natural map

\mathbf{R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}(\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N})\rightarrow\mathbf{R}\underset{n}{\mathrm{lim}}\,\mathbf{R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}}(\mathcal{M}_{n},\mathcal{N}_{n})

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

This is a simple calculation:

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}(\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}(\mathcal{M},\mathbf{R}\underset{n}{\mathrm{lim}}\,\mathcal{N}_{n})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}\underset{n}{\mathrm{lim}}\,\mathbf{R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}(\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N}_{n})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}\underset{n}{\mathrm{lim}}\,\mathbf{R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}}(\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N}_{n})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}\underset{n}{\mathrm{lim}}\,\mathbf{R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}}(\mathcal{M}_{n},\mathcal{N}_{n}).\qed

Following
[
Ber02
, §3.4]
, I can define a completed tensor product

\displaystyle-\widehat{\otimes}^{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}-

\displaystyle:{\bf D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})\times{\bf D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})\rightarrow{\bf D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}\mathcal{N}

\displaystyle:=\mathbf{R}\underset{n}{\mathrm{lim}}\,\left(\mathcal{M}_{n}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}}\mathcal{N}_{n}\right),

the result is a quasi-coherent complex by Lemma
5.1.3
. Formally, the definition is

\mathcal{M}\widehat{\otimes}^{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}\mathcal{N}:={\bf R}l_{\mathfrak{P}*}({\bf L}l_{\mathfrak{P}}^{*}\mathcal{M}\otimes^{{\bf L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{P_{\bullet}}}{\bf L}l_{\mathfrak{P}}^{*}\mathcal{N}).

Similarly, if
\pi:\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}
is a morphism of flat formal schemes, with induced maps
\pi_{n}:P^{\prime}_{n}\rightarrow P_{n}
for each
n
, there is a functor

\displaystyle\mathbf{L}\hat{\pi}^{*}

\displaystyle:{\bf D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})\rightarrow{\bf D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\mathbf{L}\hat{\pi}^{*}\mathcal{M}

\displaystyle:=\mathbf{R}\underset{n}{\mathrm{lim}}\,\mathbf{L}\pi_{n}^{*}\mathcal{M}_{n},

again, it follows from Lemma
5.1.3
that this is indeed a quasi-coherent complex. The formal definition is given by extending
\pi
to a morphism

\pi_{\bullet}\colon(\mathfrak{P}^{\prime},\mathcal{O}_{P_{\bullet}^{\prime}})\rightarrow(\mathfrak{P},\mathcal{O}_{P_{\bullet}})

and then defining

{\bf L}\hat{\pi}^{*}:={\bf R}l_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}*}\circ{\bf L}\pi^{*}_{\bullet}\circ{\bf L}l_{\mathfrak{P}}^{*}.

Note that

\mathbf{L}\hat{\pi}^{*}\mathcal{M}\widehat{\otimes}^{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}}\mathbf{L}\hat{\pi}^{*}\mathcal{N}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{L}\hat{\pi}^{*}(\mathcal{M}\widehat{\otimes}^{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}\mathcal{N}).

Moreover, letting
\mathbf{L}\pi^{*}
denote abstract module pullback along
\pi
, then the natural maps

\mathbf{L}\pi^{*}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathbf{L}\pi^{*}_{n}\mathcal{M}_{n}

for
n\geq 0
induce a map

\mathbf{L}\pi^{*}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathbf{L}\hat{\pi}^{*}\mathcal{M}

in
{\bf D}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}})
. Of course,
{\bf L}\pi^{*}
won’t preserve quasi-coherence in general.

5.1.5 Lemma
.

The functor
\mathbf{R}\pi_{*}:{\bf D}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})\rightarrow{\bf D}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}})
preserves quasi-coherence, and

\mathbf{L}\hat{\pi}^{*}:{\bf D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})\leftrightarrows{\bf D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}):\mathbf{R}\pi_{*}

form an adjoint pair.

Proof.

Suppose that
\mathcal{N}\in{\bf D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}})
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}\pi_{*}\mathcal{N}

\displaystyle\cong\mathbf{R}\pi_{*}\mathbf{R}\underset{n}{\mathrm{lim}}\,\mathcal{N}_{n}

\displaystyle\cong\mathbf{R}\underset{n}{\mathrm{lim}}\,\mathbf{R}\pi_{n*}\mathcal{N}_{n}.

Now, since each
\mathcal{N}_{n}
is quasi-coherent as an
\mathcal{O}_{P^{\prime}_{n}}
-module, each
\mathbf{R}\pi_{n*}\mathcal{N}_{n}
is quasi-coherent as an
\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}
-module. Since
\mathbf{R}\pi_{n*}
has finite cohomological dimension, the base change formula

\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{P_{n+1}}}\mathbf{R}\pi_{n+1*}\mathcal{N}_{n+1}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{R}\pi_{n*}\mathcal{N}_{n}

can be proved by reducing to the case of bounded complexes and applying
[
SGA71
, IV, Proposition 3.1.0]
. Thus I can apply Lemma
5.1.3
to deduce that
\mathbf{R}\pi_{*}\mathcal{N}
is quasi-coherent. Now, thanks to Lemma
5.1.4
, the chain of identifications

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}}(\mathbf{L}\hat{\pi}^{*}\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}\underset{n}{\mathrm{lim}}\,\mathbf{R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{P^{\prime}_{n}}}(\mathbf{L}\pi_{n}^{*}\mathcal{M}_{n},\mathcal{N}_{n})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}\underset{n}{\mathrm{lim}}\,\mathbf{R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}}(\mathcal{M}_{n},\mathbf{R}\pi_{n*}\mathcal{N}_{n})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}(\mathcal{M},\mathbf{R}\pi_{*}\mathcal{N})

shows that
({\bf L}\hat{\pi}^{*},{\bf R}\pi_{*})
do indeed form an adjoint pair as claimed.
∎

For open immersions, the abstract module pullback is already complete.

5.1.6 Lemma
.

Let
j:\mathfrak{U}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}
be an open immersion of flat formal schemes. Then the natural morphism of functors
j^{-1}\rightarrow\mathbf{L}\hat{j}^{*}
is an
isomorphism.

Proof.

First note that
j^{-1}
has a left adjoint
j_{!}
, and thus commutes with limits. The fact that
j_{!}
is exact means that
j^{-1}
moreover commutes with derived limits, since
j^{-1}
preserves
K
-injective complexes in the category of inverse systems. I then simply compute

j^{-1}\mathcal{M}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}j^{-1}\mathbf{R}\underset{n}{\mathrm{lim}}\,\mathcal{M}_{n}=\mathbf{R}\underset{n}{\mathrm{lim}}\,j_{n}^{-1}\mathcal{M}_{n}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{R}\underset{n}{\mathrm{lim}}\,\mathbf{L}j_{n}^{*}\mathcal{M}_{n}=\mathbf{L}\hat{j}^{*}\mathcal{M}

as required.
∎

5.1.7 Remark
.

The observation that
j^{-1}
commutes with derived limits (together with the analogous assertion that
j^{-1}
commutes with derived tensor products) implies that quasi-coherence of a complex
\mathcal{M}\in{\bf D}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})
can be checked locally on
\mathfrak{P}
.

Another situation in which there is no need to complete is the following.

5.1.8 Lemma
.

Let
\pi\colon\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}
be a finite morphism of flat formal schemes, which is an isomorphism on the underlying topological spaces. Then the morphism

{\bf L}\pi^{*}\rightarrow{\bf L}\hat{\pi}^{*}
of functors
{\bf D}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})\rightarrow{\bf D}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}})
is an isomorphism.

5.1.9 Remark
.

It seems reasonable to suppose that the lemma holds for more general finite morphisms, but I will only need this special case.

Proof.

The lemma amounts to showing that, for any
\mathcal{M}\in{\bf D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})
, the natural morphism

\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}\otimes^{{\bf L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}{\bf R}\underset{n}{\mathrm{lim}}\,\mathcal{M}_{n}\rightarrow{\bf R}\underset{n}{\mathrm{lim}}\,(\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}^{\prime}}\otimes^{{\bf L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}}\mathcal{M}_{n})

is an isomorphism. This can be checked locally on
\mathfrak{P}
, so I can assume that both
\mathfrak{P}
and
\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}
are affine. In this case,
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}
admits a (possibly infinite, but bounded above) resolution by finite free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}
-modules. By truncating, and using the fact that
{\bf R}\underset{n}{\mathrm{lim}}\,
has finite cohomological dimension (at least for systems
\{\mathcal{M}_{n}\}_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
with each
\mathcal{M}_{n}
quasi-coherent) I can therefore replace
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}
by
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{\oplus m}
, and each
\mathcal{O}_{P^{\prime}_{n}}
by
\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}^{\oplus m}
, in which case the claim is clear.
∎

Quasi-coherent complexes satisfy the following version of the projection formula.

5.1.10 Lemma
.

Let
\pi:\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}
be a morphism of flat formal schemes. Then for any
\mathcal{M}\in{\bf D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})
the map

\mathcal{M}\widehat{\otimes}^{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}\mathbf{R}\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\mathbf{R}\pi_{*}\mathbf{L}\hat{\pi}^{*}\mathcal{M}

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

It suffices to show that the map

\mathcal{M}_{n}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}}\mathbf{R}\pi_{n*}\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\mathbf{R}\pi_{n*}\mathbf{L}\pi_{n}^{*}\mathcal{M}_{n}

is an isomorphism, for each
n
. This question is local on
P_{n}
, which I can therefore assume to be affine. Now, the standard construction of
K
-flat resolutions gives a resolution of
\mathcal{M}_{n}
with terms direct sums of sheaves of the form
j_{!}\mathcal{O}_{U}
for an open subscheme
j\colon U\rightarrow P_{n}
. But since
\mathcal{M}_{n}
is quasi-coherent, and
P_{n}
is affine, I can actually find a
K
-flat resolution whose terms are all free
\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}
-modules. Now using the fact that
{\bf R}\pi_{n*}
has finite cohomological dimension and commutes with filtered colimits, I can reduce to the tautological case
\mathcal{M}_{n}=\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}
.
∎

I will let
{\bf D}_{\mathrm{qc},{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})
denote the isogeny category of quasi-coherent complexes, and
{\bf D}_{\mathrm{coh},{\mathbb{Q}}}^{b}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})
its full subcategory spanned by bounded, coherent complexes. There is a functor

{\bf D}_{\mathrm{qc},{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})\rightarrow{\bf D}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}})

defined on objects by
\mathcal{M}\mapsto\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}
. In general, this need not be fully faithful, but it will be after restricting to
{\bf D}_{\mathrm{coh},{\mathbb{Q}}}^{b}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})
.

5.1.11 Lemma
.

Let
\mathfrak{P}
be a flat formal scheme, and
\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N}\in\mathbf{Coh}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})
coherent
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}
-modules. Then the natural map

\mathrm{Ext}^{q}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}(\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathbb{Q}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Ext}^{q}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathcal{N}_{{\mathbb{Q}}})

is an isomorphism, for all
q\geq 0
.

Proof.

Since
\mathfrak{P}
is quasi-compact, the question is local on
\mathfrak{P}
, which I may therefore assume to be affine. In this case,
\mathcal{M}
admits a resolution (possibly unbounded below) by finite free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}
-modules. This reduces to the case
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}
, which is clear.
∎

5.1.12 Corollary
.

Let
\mathfrak{P}
be a flat formal scheme. Then the functor

{\bf D}^{b}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{coh}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})\rightarrow{\bf D}^{b}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}),

defined on objects by
\mathcal{M}\mapsto\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}
, is fully faithful.

I can now deduce an invariance result for quasi-coherent complexes under admissible blowups.

5.1.13 Proposition
.

Let
\pi:\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}
be an admissible blowup of flat formal schemes. Then, for any
\mathcal{M}\in{\bf D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})
, the map

\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathbf{R}\pi_{*}\mathbf{L}\hat{\pi}^{*}\mathcal{M}

is an isogeny. That is, it becomes invertible in
{\bf D}_{\mathrm{qc},{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})
.

Proof.

Thanks to the projection formula (Lemma
5.1.10
above), the given map can be identified with

\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}\widehat{\otimes}^{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}\mathbf{R}\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}.

Since isogenies are preserved by applying the functor
\mathcal{M}\widehat{\otimes}^{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}-
, it suffices to treat the case
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}
. In this case, after tensoring with
{\mathbb{Q}}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}}

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}{\mathbb{Q}}}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}\pi_{*}\mathbf{R}\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}_{K}}=\mathbf{R}\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}*}\mathbf{R}\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}_{K}}.

Since
\pi:\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}_{K}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}_{K}
is an isomorphism, it follows that

\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}\rightarrow\mathbf{R}\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}

is a morphism of bounded complexes on
\mathfrak{P}
, with coherent cohomology sheaves, which becomes a quasi-isomorphism after applying
-\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathbb{Q}}
. It therefore follows from Corollary
5.1.12
that it is an isogeny.
∎

I will also need to consider inductive systems of quasi-coherent complexes, the formalism of which works exactly as in
[
Ber02
, §4.2]
. Thus
\mathfrak{P}^{(\bullet)}
(resp.
\mathfrak{P}^{(\bullet)}_{\bullet}
) will denotes the topos of
{\mathbb{N}}
-indexed inductive systems of sheaves on
\mathfrak{P}
(resp. on
\mathfrak{P}_{\bullet}
), as in §
1.6
, which is ringed via the constant ind-object
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}
(resp.
\mathcal{O}_{P_{\bullet}}
). Berthelot then defines a double localisation
\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})
of the derived category of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}
-modules on
\mathfrak{P}^{(\bullet)}
. Roughly speaking this corresponds to tensoring with
{\mathbb{Q}}
and then taking the colimit over the inductive system. If I need to emphasize the fact that I am considering categories of inductive systems of complexes, rather than just complexes, I will write the rings on
\mathfrak{P}^{(\bullet)}
and
\mathfrak{P}_{\bullet}^{(\bullet)}
as
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{(\bullet)}}
and
\mathcal{O}_{P^{(\bullet)}_{\bullet}}
respectively, thus
\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})
is a localisation of the category
{\bf D}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{(\bullet)}})
.

As in
[
Ber02
, §4.2]
, I will denote by
\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})\subset\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})
the full subcategory on objects which are levelwise quasi-coherent. Exactly as above, any morphism
\pi\colon\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}
of flat formal schemes gives rise to a commutative square

\textstyle{(\mathfrak{P}^{\prime(\bullet)}_{\bullet},\mathcal{O}_{P^{\prime}_{\bullet}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{l_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime(\bullet)}}}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{(\bullet)}_{\bullet}}
\textstyle{(\mathfrak{P}^{\prime(\bullet)},\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{(\bullet)}}
\textstyle{(\mathfrak{P}^{(\bullet)}_{\bullet},\mathcal{O}_{P_{\bullet}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{l_{\mathfrak{P}^{(\bullet)}}}
\textstyle{(\mathfrak{P}^{(\bullet)},\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})}

of ringed toposes, and
{\bf R}l_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime(\bullet)}*}\circ{\bf L}\pi^{(\bullet),*}_{\bullet}\circ{\bf L}l^{*}_{\mathfrak{P}^{(\bullet)}}
descends to a functor

{\bf L}\hat{\pi}^{*}\colon\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})\rightarrow\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}})

on the localised categories. Informally, this just applies
{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}
levelwise, and then passes to the localisation. There is of course a similar definition of the completed tensor product
