-\widehat{\otimes}^{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}-\colon\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})\times\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})\rightarrow\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}).

Note that the formula

\mathbf{L}\hat{\pi}^{*}\mathcal{M}\widehat{\otimes}^{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}}\mathbf{L}\hat{\pi}^{*}\mathcal{N}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{L}\hat{\pi}^{*}(\mathcal{M}\widehat{\otimes}^{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}\mathcal{N})

still holds for objects of
\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}
.

5.2.
The rigidification functor

The categories
{\bf D}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}
and
\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}
are now the natural source of the functor of completed pullback along the specialisation map. Roughly speaking, for any flat formal scheme
\mathfrak{P}
, I define

\displaystyle\mathbf{L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}=\mathbf{L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{*}

\displaystyle:{\bf D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})\rightarrow{\bf D}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}})

\displaystyle\mathbf{L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{*}\mathcal{M}

\displaystyle:=\underset{\pi:\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}}{\mathrm{colim}}\,\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}^{-1}\mathbf{L}\hat{\pi}^{*}\mathcal{M}

where the colimit is over all admissible blowups
\pi:\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}
. Tensoring with
{\mathbb{Q}}
and then passing to the colimit gives a functor

(5.2.1)

\mathbf{L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}:\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})\rightarrow{\bf D}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}}).

As above, the formal definition of
{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}
requires the use of sheaves on diagrams of spaces. I first let
I_{\mathfrak{P}}
denote the category of admissible blowups of
\mathfrak{P}
, and

\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}}\colon I_{\mathfrak{P}}\rightarrow{\bf FSch}

the tautological diagram in the category of formal schemes. I then consider:

•

the topos
{\mathcal{B}}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}}
of
{\mathbb{N}}
-indexed inductive systems of sheaves on the diagram
\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}}
, ringed via the sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{P}}}
whose restriction to each
\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}\in I_{\mathfrak{P}}
is the constant inductive system
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}
;

•

the topos
{\mathcal{B}}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}_{\bullet}}
of
{\mathbb{N}}
-indexed inductive systems of
{\mathbb{N}}
-indexed projective systems of sheaves on
\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}}
, ringed via the sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{{\mathcal{B}}_{P_{\bullet}}}
whose restriction to each
\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}\in I_{\mathfrak{P}}
is the constant inductive system
\mathcal{O}_{P^{\prime}_{\bullet}}
.

As in the case of a single formal scheme
\mathfrak{P}
, if I need to emphasize the fact that I am considering categories of inductive systems, I will sometimes write these two rings as
\mathcal{O}_{{\mathcal{B}}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}}}
and
\mathcal{O}_{{\mathcal{B}}^{(\bullet)}_{P_{\bullet}}}
respectively, for example in considering the derived category
{\bf D}(\mathcal{O}_{{\mathcal{B}}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}}})
(and in particular to distinguish it from
{\bf D}(\mathcal{O}_{{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{P}}})
). There are then natural morphisms

\displaystyle l_{\mathcal{B}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}}}

\displaystyle\colon(\mathcal{B}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}_{\bullet}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}_{P_{\bullet}}})\rightarrow(\mathcal{B}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}}})

\displaystyle\pi

\displaystyle\colon({\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{P}},\mathcal{O}_{{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{P}}})\rightarrow(\mathfrak{P},\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})

\displaystyle\pi^{(\bullet)}_{\bullet}

\displaystyle\colon(\mathcal{B}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}_{\bullet}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}_{P_{\bullet}}})\rightarrow(\mathfrak{P}_{\bullet}^{(\bullet)},\mathcal{O}_{P_{\bullet}}).

Just as in the case of a single admissible blowup
\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}
, I can then define the completed pullback functor

{\bf L}\hat{\pi}^{*}\colon{\bf D}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{(\bullet)}})\rightarrow{\bf D}(\mathcal{O}_{{\mathcal{B}}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}}})

as the composite
{\bf R}l_{\mathcal{B}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}}*}\circ{\bf L}\pi^{(\bullet)*}_{\bullet}\circ{\bf L}l^{*}_{\mathfrak{P}^{(\bullet)}}
. Finally, there is a morphism

\mathrm{sp}_{{\mathcal{B}}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}}}\colon(\mathfrak{P}_{K},\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}})\rightarrow({\mathcal{B}}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}},\mathcal{O}_{{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{P}}})

whose inverse image functor takes the colimit over both
I_{\mathfrak{P}}^{\rm op}
and
{\mathbb{N}}
. The functor (
5.2.1
) I am after is then defined by (easily!) checking that the composite functor

\displaystyle{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}={\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}_{\mathfrak{P}}

\displaystyle\colon{\bf D}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{(\bullet)}})\rightarrow{\bf D}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}})

\displaystyle{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}

\displaystyle:={\bf L}\mathrm{sp}_{{\mathcal{B}}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}}}^{*}\circ{\bf L}\hat{\pi}^{*}

descends to the quotient
\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})
of
{\bf D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{(\bullet)}})
. Note that the functor
{\bf L}\mathrm{sp}_{{\mathcal{B}}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}}}^{*}
appearing in the definition applies
\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}^{-1}
on each admissible blowup
\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}
, then takes the colimit over both
I_{\mathfrak{P}}^{\rm op}
and
{\mathbb{N}}
, and then finally tensors with
{\mathbb{Q}}
. In particular, all of the composite factors of
{\bf L}\mathrm{sp}_{{\mathcal{B}}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}}}^{*}
are exact and derive trivially. The natural maps

{\bf L}\pi^{*}\rightarrow{\bf L}\hat{\pi}^{*}

defined for each
\pi\colon\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}
fit together to give a morphism of functors

(5.2.2)

{\bf L}\mathrm{sp}^{*}\rightarrow{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}

with source the abstract module pullback along
\mathrm{sp}\colon(\mathfrak{P}_{K},\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}})\rightarrow(\mathfrak{P},\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})
.

5.2.1 Example
.

If
\mathcal{M}
is a coherent
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}
-module such that
\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}
is a locally projective
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}
-module, then the morphism (
5.2.2
) induces an isomorphism

\mathrm{sp}^{*}\mathcal{M}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}\mathcal{M}

in
{\bf D}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}})
.

5.3.
Functoriality of
\bm{{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}_{\mathfrak{P}}}

Now suppose that
u\colon\mathfrak{P}\rightarrow\mathfrak{Q}
is a morphism of flat formal schemes over
\mathcal{V}
. Then taking the strict transform induces a functor

I_{\mathfrak{Q}}\rightarrow I_{\mathfrak{P}},

and it is straightforward to check that the hypotheses of Lemma
1.6.2
are satisfied. This therefore gives rise to a morphism of (ringed) sites

u\colon\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{Q}}.

Of course, there are analogous morphisms for the sites of inductive and projective systems of sheaves on
\mathfrak{P}
and
\mathfrak{Q}
. The commutative squares

\textstyle{\mathfrak{P}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{sp}}
\scriptstyle{u}
\textstyle{\mathcal{B}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\scriptstyle{u}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{Q}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{sp}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{B}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{Q}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{Q}}

now give rise to a base change morphism

(5.3.1)

{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}_{\mathfrak{Q}}^{*}\circ{\bf R}u_{*}\rightarrow{\bf R}u_{*}\circ{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}_{\mathfrak{P}}

of functors

\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})\rightarrow{\bf D}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Q}_{K}}).

When
\mathfrak{Q}=\mathrm{Spf}\left(\mathcal{V}\right)
is a point,
u\colon\mathfrak{P}\rightarrow\mathrm{Spf}\left(\mathcal{V}\right)
is the structure morphism, and
\mathcal{M}\in\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})
, I then define

{\bf R}\Gamma(\mathfrak{P},\mathcal{M}):={\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}_{\mathrm{Spf}\left(\mathcal{V}\right)}{\bf R}u_{*}\mathcal{M}\in{\bf D}(K).

Concretely, if
\mathcal{M}=\{\mathcal{M}^{(m)}\}_{m\in{\mathbb{N}}}\in\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})
, then

{\bf R}\Gamma(\mathfrak{P},\mathcal{M})=\mathbf{R}\Gamma(\mathfrak{P},\underset{m}{\mathrm{colim}}\,\mathcal{M}^{(m)}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathbb{Q}})=\underset{m}{\mathrm{colim}}\,\mathbf{R}\Gamma(\mathfrak{P},\mathcal{M}^{(m)})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathbb{Q}}.

Before proving that (
5.3.1
) is an isomorphism, I need to show that
{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}
is compatible with open immersions.

5.3.1 Lemma
.

Let
j:\mathfrak{U}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}
denote an open immersion of flat formal schemes. Then the natural map

j^{-1}\mathbf{L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{*}\rightarrow\mathbf{L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}_{\mathfrak{U}}^{*}j^{-1}

is an isomorphism of functors
\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})\rightarrow{\bf D}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{U}_{K}}^{+})
.

Proof.

It suffices to prove the analogous statement with
\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})
replaced by
{\bf D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})
. The functor
\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}\mapsto\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}\times_{\mathfrak{P}}\mathfrak{U}
from admissible blowups of
\mathfrak{P}
to those of
\mathfrak{U}
is cofinal, so I can compute

\displaystyle\mathbf{L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}_{\mathfrak{U}}^{*}\mathbf{L}\hat{j}^{*}\mathcal{M}

\displaystyle=\underset{\pi\colon\mathfrak{U}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathfrak{U}}{\mathrm{colim}}\,\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{U}^{\prime}}^{-1}\mathbf{L}\hat{\pi}^{*}\mathbf{L}\hat{j}^{*}\mathcal{M}

\displaystyle=\underset{\pi\colon\mathfrak{U}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathfrak{U}}{\mathrm{colim}}\,\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{U}^{\prime}}^{-1}j^{-1}\mathbf{L}\hat{\pi}^{*}\mathcal{M}

\displaystyle=\underset{\pi\colon\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}}{\mathrm{colim}}\,\mathrm{sp}_{\pi^{-1}(\mathfrak{U})}^{-1}j^{-1}\mathbf{L}\hat{\pi}^{*}\mathcal{M}

\displaystyle=j^{-1}\underset{\pi\colon\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}}{\mathrm{colim}}\,\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}^{-1}\mathbf{L}\hat{\pi}^{*}\mathcal{M}

\displaystyle=j^{-1}\mathbf{L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}_{\mathfrak{P}}\mathcal{M}

using Lemma
5.1.6
for the second (canonical) isomorphism.
∎

5.3.2 Theorem
.

Let
u\colon\mathfrak{P}\rightarrow\mathfrak{Q}
be a flat morphism of flat formal schemes. Then

{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}_{\mathfrak{Q}}^{*}\circ{\bf R}u_{*}\rightarrow{\bf R}u_{*}\circ{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}_{\mathfrak{P}}

is an isomorphism of functors
\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})\rightarrow{\bf D}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Q}_{K}})
.

Proof.

Since both
\mathfrak{P}
and
\mathfrak{P}_{K}
are quasi-compact, cohomology commutes with filtered colimits. It therefore suffices to prove the analogous statement with
\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})
replaced by
{\bf D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})
.

First suppose that
\mathfrak{Q}=\mathrm{Spf}\left(\mathcal{V}\right)
is a point. Then the base change morphism amounts to a morphism

\mathbf{R}\Gamma(\mathfrak{P},\mathcal{M})\rightarrow\mathbf{R}\Gamma(\mathfrak{P}_{K},\mathbf{L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}_{\mathfrak{P}}\mathcal{M})

in
{\bf D}(K)
. To prove that this is an isomorphism, recall from
[
Hub94
, §4]
that, as topological spaces,

\mathfrak{P}_{K}=\underset{I_{\mathfrak{P}}}{\mathrm{lim}}\,\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}.

In this case, since each topological space
\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}
is coherent and sober, and the transition maps are quasi-compact, it follows from
[
FK18
, Chapter 0, Proposition 3.1.19]
that the map

\underset{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}{\mathrm{colim}}\,\mathbf{R}\Gamma(\mathfrak{P}^{\prime},\mathbf{L}\hat{\pi}^{*}\mathcal{M})\rightarrow\mathbf{R}\Gamma(\mathfrak{P}_{K},\mathbf{L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}\mathcal{M})

is an isomorphism. In fact, the result in
[
FK18
]
is stated for sheaves, rather than complexes, but it is straightforward to extend it to complexes using
K
-injective resolutions. It now simply suffices to observe that, by Proposition
5.1.13
, the map

\mathbf{R}\Gamma(\mathfrak{P},\mathcal{M})\rightarrow\mathbf{R}\Gamma(\mathfrak{P}^{\prime},\mathbf{L}\hat{\pi}^{*}\mathcal{M})

is an isomorphism for all
\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}
.

For the general case, it suffices to show that the given map induces an isomorphism on derived global sections for any quasi-compact open
V\subset\mathfrak{Q}_{K}
. To prove this, I can replace
\mathfrak{Q}
by an admissible blowup without changing either side, thus I can assume that
V=\mathfrak{V}_{K}
for an open subscheme
\mathfrak{V}\subset\mathfrak{Q}
. By Lemma
5.3.1
, I can then replace
\mathfrak{Q}
by
\mathfrak{V}
, and hence assume that
V=\mathfrak{Q}_{K}
. In this case, the base change map fits into the following commutative diagram.

\textstyle{\mathbf{R}\Gamma(\mathfrak{Q}_{K},{\bf R}u_{*}{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}_{\mathfrak{P}}\mathcal{M})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\bf R}\Gamma(\mathfrak{P}_{K},{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}_{\mathfrak{P}}\mathcal{M})}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}\Gamma(\mathfrak{Q}_{K},{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}_{\mathfrak{Q}}{\bf R}u_{*}\mathcal{M})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\bf R}\Gamma(\mathfrak{Q},{\bf R}u_{*}\mathcal{M})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\bf R}\Gamma(\mathfrak{P},\mathcal{M})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

The two horizontal maps are clearly isomorphisms, and the two vertical maps are isomorphisms by the already treated case
\mathfrak{Q}=\mathrm{Spf}\left(\mathcal{V}\right)
. Hence the top left diagonal map is an isomorphism.
∎

It’s worth explicitly stating the special case
\mathfrak{Q}=\mathrm{Spf}\left(\mathcal{V}\right)
.

5.3.3 Corollary
.

Let
\mathfrak{P}
be a flat formal scheme, and
\mathcal{M}\in\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})
. Then the natural map

{\bf R}\Gamma(\mathfrak{P},\mathcal{M})\rightarrow{\bf R}\Gamma(\mathfrak{P}_{K},{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{*}\mathcal{M})

is an isomorphism in
{\bf D}(K)
.

Eventually, I will want to upgrade Theorem
5.3.2
to include
\mathscr{D}
-module structures, and in order to do so I will need a slightly different description of the functor
{\bf R}u_{*}
that works under the additional assumption that
u
is flat. Restricting
\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}}
along the functor
I_{\mathfrak{Q}}\rightarrow I_{\mathfrak{P}}
gives rise to a diagram
\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}/\mathfrak{Q}}\colon I_{\mathfrak{Q}}\rightarrow\bf{FSch}
which by the flatness hypothesis on
u
sends an admissible blowup
\mathfrak{Q}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathfrak{Q}
to the admissible blowup
\mathfrak{Q}^{\prime}\times_{\mathfrak{Q}}\mathfrak{P}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}
. This gives rise to a category
\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}/\mathfrak{Q}}})
of inductive systems of quasi-coherent complexes on
\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}/\mathfrak{Q}}
as above, and restriction along
I_{\mathfrak{Q}}\rightarrow I_{\mathfrak{P}}
induces a ‘forgetful’ functor

\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}}})\rightarrow\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}/\mathfrak{Q}}})

Of course, the morphism of diagrams
u\colon\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}/\mathfrak{Q}}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{Q}}
induces a functor

{\bf R}u_{*}\colon\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}/\mathfrak{Q}}})\rightarrow\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{Q}}}).

5.3.4 Lemma
.

The diagram

\textstyle{\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\bf R}u_{*}}
\textstyle{\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}/\mathfrak{Q}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\bf R}u_{*}}
\textstyle{\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{Q}}})}

commutes upto
2
-isomorphism.

Proof.

This is simply a matter of unwinding the definitions.
∎

I end this section with a brief discussion of the compatibility of
{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}
with tensor products. If
\mathfrak{P}
is a flat formal scheme, and
\pi:\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}
is an admissible blowup, there is a natural map

\mathbf{L}\hat{\pi}^{*}\mathcal{M}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}}^{\mathbf{L}}\mathbf{L}\hat{\pi}^{*}\mathcal{N}\rightarrow\mathbf{L}\hat{\pi}^{*}\mathcal{M}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}}^{\mathbf{L}}\mathbf{L}\hat{\pi}^{*}\mathcal{N}=\mathbf{L}\hat{\pi}^{*}(\mathcal{M}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}^{\mathbf{L}}\mathcal{N}).

Applying
\mathrm{sp}^{-1}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}
gives

\mathrm{sp}^{-1}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}\mathbf{L}\hat{\pi}^{*}\mathcal{M}\otimes_{\mathrm{sp}^{-1}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}}^{\mathbf{L}}\mathrm{sp}^{-1}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}\mathbf{L}\hat{\pi}^{*}\mathcal{N}\rightarrow\mathrm{sp}^{-1}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}\mathbf{L}\hat{\pi}^{*}(\mathcal{M}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}^{\mathbf{L}}\mathcal{N}),

and passing to the colimit in
\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}
gives a map

\mathbf{L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}\mathcal{M}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{+}_{K}}}^{\mathbf{L}}\mathbf{L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}\mathcal{N}\rightarrow\mathbf{L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}(\mathcal{M}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}^{\mathbf{L}}\mathcal{N})

in
{\bf D}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}})
. Tensoring with
{\mathbb{Q}}
therefore gives, for any
\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N}\in{\bf D}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})
, a map

(5.3.2)

\mathbf{L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}\mathcal{M}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}}}^{\mathbf{L}}\mathbf{L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}\mathcal{N}\rightarrow\mathbf{L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}(\mathcal{M}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}^{\mathbf{L}}\mathcal{N})

in
{\bf D}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}})
. Passing to the colimit gives a similar map whenever
\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N}\in\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})
, I leave it to the reader to give a precise construction of this map. In general (
5.3.2
) won’t be an isomorphism, however, I will show later on that this will be the case whenever
\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N}
underly overholonomic
\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}
-modules.

5.4.
Functions with overconvergent singularities

Now suppose that
\mathfrak{P}
is smooth, and consider a divisor
D\subset P
. In this situation, Berthelot has defined in
[
Ber02
, §4.4]
the
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}
-algebra
\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{P}}(D,r)
for any
r\in{\mathbb{N}}
. Locally, if there exists some
f\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}
such that
D=P\cap V(f)
, then

\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{P}}(D,r)=\frac{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}\langle X\rangle}{(f^{r}X-p)}.

5.4.1 Lemma
.

\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{P}}(D,r)
is quasi-coherent as a complex of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}
-modules.

Proof.

Quasi-coherence can be checked locally, by Remark
5.1.7
, so I can assume that
\mathfrak{P}
is affine and that
D\subset P
is the vanishing locus of a single function
f\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}
, which is a non-zero divisor in
\mathcal{O}_{P}
. In this case

\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{P}}(D,r)=\frac{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}\langle X\rangle}{(f^{r}X-p)}

is
p
-torsion-free, and hence flat over
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}
. Thus

\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}\frac{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}\langle X\rangle}{(f^{r}X-p)}\cong\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}\frac{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}\langle X\rangle}{(f^{r}X-p)}=\frac{\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}[X]}{(f^{r}X-p)}

is quasi-coherent, and the transition maps
\frac{\mathcal{O}_{P_{n+1}}[X]}{(f^{r}X-p)}\rightarrow\frac{\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}[X]}{(f^{r}X-p)}
are surjective. Hence

\mathbf{R}\underset{n}{\mathrm{lim}}\,\frac{\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}[X]}{(f^{r}X-p)}\cong\underset{n}{\mathrm{lim}}\,\frac{\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}[X]}{(f^{r}X-p)}\cong\frac{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}\langle X\rangle}{(f^{r}X-p)}

as required.
∎

Berthelot then defines
\widehat{\mathcal{B}}^{(m)}_{\mathfrak{P}}(D):=\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{P}}(D,p^{m+1})
, which form an inductive system as
m
varies. This gives rise to an object

\widehat{\mathcal{B}}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}}(D)\in\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}).

5.4.2 Theorem
.

Let
\mathfrak{P}
be a smooth formal scheme,
D\subset P
a divisor, and
U:=P\setminus D
. Suppose that
\mathcal{M}\in\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})
. Then the morphism

\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{B}}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}}(D)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\bf L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}\mathcal{M}

induces an isomorphism

j_{U}^{\dagger}{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}\mathcal{M}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}(\widehat{\mathcal{B}}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}}(D)\widehat{\otimes}^{\bf L}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}\mathcal{M})

in
{\bf D}^{(}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}})
.

Proof.

Set
r_{m}=p^{m+1}
,
\lambda_{m}:=p^{-1/r_{m}}
, let
\left]D\right[_{\lambda_{m}}\subset\mathfrak{P}_{K}
be the open tube of radius
\lambda_{m}
,
\overline{D}_{m}
its closure in
\mathfrak{P}_{K}
, and write
V_{m}:=\mathfrak{P}_{K}\setminus\overline{D}_{m}
. Let
j_{m}:V_{m}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}_{K}
denote the inclusion. Thus, if
D
is defined by
f=0
inside
P
, for some
f\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}
, then
V_{m}
is defined inside
\mathfrak{P}_{K}
by
\left|f\right|\geq\lambda_{m}
. Therefore, for any complex
\mathscr{K}
on
\mathfrak{P}_{K}
,

j_{U}^{\dagger}\mathscr{K}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\underset{m}{\mathrm{colim}}\,{\bf R}j_{m*}j_{m}^{-1}\mathscr{K}.

The map

{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}(\widehat{\mathcal{B}}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}}(D)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\bf L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}\mathcal{M})

is the colimit of the maps

{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}(\widehat{\mathcal{B}}^{(m)}_{\mathfrak{P}}(D)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\bf L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}\mathcal{M}).

It therefore suffices to show that

{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}(\widehat{\mathcal{B}}^{(m)}_{\mathfrak{P}}(D)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\bf L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}\mathcal{M})

factors through an isomorphism

{\bf R}j_{m*}j_{m}^{-1}{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}(\widehat{\mathcal{B}}^{(m)}_{\mathfrak{P}}(D)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\bf L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}\mathcal{M})

(which is then necessarily unique). To prove this, consider the admissible blowup

\pi\colon\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}:=\mathrm{Bl}_{(p,f^{r_{m}})}\mathfrak{P}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}.

There exists a unique open subset
j_{m}\colon\mathfrak{V}_{m}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}
such that
V_{m}=\mathrm{sp}^{-1}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{V}_{m}^{\prime})
. Now set
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:={\bf L}\hat{\pi}^{*}\mathcal{M}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{B}}^{(m)}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}(D):={\bf L}\hat{\pi}^{*}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}^{(m)}_{\mathfrak{P}}(D)
, thus what I need to show is that the map

{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\rightarrow{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}(\widehat{\mathcal{B}}^{(m)}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}(D)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\bf L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}),

factors through an isomorphism

{\bf R}j_{m*}j_{m}^{-1}{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\rightarrow{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}(\widehat{\mathcal{B}}^{(m)}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}(D)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\bf L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}}\mathcal{M}^{\prime})

(again, this isomorphism is then necessarily unique). Applying Theorem
5.3.2
, it therefore suffices to prove that the map

\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{B}}^{(m)}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}(D)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\bf L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}

factors through an isomorphism

{\bf R}j_{m*}j_{m}^{-1}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{B}}^{(m)}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}(D)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\bf L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}

in
\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}})
. By Lemma
5.1.10
I can reduce to the case
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}
, in other words I need to show that

\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{B}}^{(m)}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}(D)

factors through an isomorphism

{\bf R}j_{m*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{V}_{m}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{B}}^{(m)}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}(D)

in
{\bf D}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}})
. To see this, I will construct the inverse isogeny

\widehat{\mathcal{B}}^{(m)}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}(D)\rightarrow{\bf R}j_{m*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{V}_{m}^{\prime}}.

Locally on
\mathfrak{P}
,

\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}=\mathbf{Proj}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}\left(\frac{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}[X,Y]}{(f^{r_{m}}X-pY)}\right),

where
D=P\cap V(f)
. This is covered by the two open formal subschemes

\displaystyle\mathfrak{V}_{m}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Spf}\left(\frac{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}\langle X\rangle}{(f^{r_{m}}X-p)}\right)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{U}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Spf}\left(\frac{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}\langle Y\rangle}{(f^{r_{m}}-pY)}\right)

In particular, locally on
\mathfrak{P}
,
\mathfrak{V}_{m}^{\prime}=D(Y)
is the complement of a hypersurface in
\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}
, and hence
{\bf R}j_{m*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{V}^{\prime}_{m}}=j_{m*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{V}^{\prime}_{m}}
is a module (and not just a complex). Thus I am allowed to work locally on
\mathfrak{P}
, and therefore assume that there is indeed such an
f
.

I can then calculate

\widehat{\mathcal{B}}^{(m)}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}(D)\!\mid_{\mathfrak{V}^{\prime}_{m}}={\bf R}\underset{n}{\mathrm{lim}}\,\frac{\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}[T]}{(f^{r_{m}}T-p)}\otimes^{{\bf L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}}\frac{\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}[X]}{(f^{r_{m}}X-p)}.

Since
f
is not a zero divisor in
\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}
, a direct calculation then shows that

\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}[X]\overset{f^{r_{m}}X-p}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}[X]

is a flat resolution of
\frac{\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}[X]}{(f^{r_{m}}X-p)}
. Thus

\frac{\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}[T]}{(f^{r_{n}}T-p)}\otimes^{{\bf L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}}\frac{\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}[X]}{(f^{r_{m}}X-p)}=\left[\frac{\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}[X,T]}{(f^{r_{m}}T-p)}\overset{f^{r_{m}}X-p}{\longrightarrow}\frac{\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}[X,T]}{(f^{r_{m}}T-p)}\right].

Since each
\frac{\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}[X,T]}{(f^{r_{m}}T-p)}
is a quasi-coherent
\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}
-module, and the transition maps

\frac{\mathcal{O}_{P_{n+1}}[X,T]}{(f^{r_{m}}T-p)}\rightarrow\frac{\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}[X,T]}{(f^{r_{m}}T-p)}

are surjective,

{\bf R}\underset{n}{\mathrm{lim}}\,\frac{\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}[X,T]}{(f^{r_{m}}T-p)}=\underset{n}{\mathrm{lim}}\,\frac{\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}[X,T]}{(f^{r_{m}}T-p)}=\frac{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}\langle X,T\rangle}{(f^{r_{m}}T-p)}.

Thus

\widehat{\mathcal{B}}^{(m)}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}(D)\!\mid_{\mathfrak{V}^{\prime}_{m}}=\left[\frac{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}\langle X,T\rangle}{(f^{r_{m}}T-p)}\overset{f^{r_{m}}X-p}{\longrightarrow}\frac{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}\langle X,T\rangle}{(f^{r_{m}}T-p)}\right].

A direct calculation shows that this map is injective, thus

\widehat{\mathcal{B}}^{(m)}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}(D)\!\mid_{\mathfrak{V}^{\prime}_{m}}=\frac{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}\langle X,T\rangle}{(f^{r_{m}}T-p,f^{r_{m}}X-p)}.

On
\mathfrak{V}^{\prime}_{m}
, the factorisation

\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}\rightarrow{\bf R}j_{m*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{V}^{\prime}_{m}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{B}}^{(m)}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}(D)

I am after is simply the inclusion

\frac{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}\langle X\rangle}{(f^{r_{m}}X-p)}\rightarrow\frac{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}\langle T,X\rangle}{(f^{r_{m}}T-p,f^{r_{m}}X-p)}.

The inverse map

\widehat{\mathcal{B}}^{(m)}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}(D)\rightarrow{\bf R}j_{m*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{V}^{\prime}_{m}}

is then the multiplication

\frac{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}\langle T,X\rangle}{(f^{r_{m}}T-p,f^{r_{m}}X-p)}\rightarrow\frac{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}\langle X\rangle}{(f^{r_{m}}X-p)}.

which is surjective, with kernel
(T-X)
annihilated by
p
. Similarly, on
\mathfrak{U}
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{B}}^{(m)}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}(D)\!\mid_{\mathfrak{U}}

\displaystyle={\bf R}\underset{n}{\mathrm{lim}}\,\frac{\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}[T]}{(f^{r_{m}}T-p)}\otimes^{{\bf L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}}\frac{\mathcal{O}_{P_{n}}[Y]}{(f^{r_{m}}-pY)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}\langle T,Y\rangle}{(f^{r_{m}}T-p,f^{r_{m}}-pY)}.

The inverse map

\widehat{\mathcal{B}}^{(m)}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}(D)\rightarrow{\bf R}j_{m*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{V}^{\prime}_{m}}

is therefore the map

\frac{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}\langle T,Y\rangle}{(f^{r_{m}}T-p,f^{r_{m}}-pY)}\rightarrow\frac{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}\langle Y,Y^{-1}\rangle}{(f^{r_{m}}-pY)}

given by
T\mapsto Y^{-1}
. Again, this map is surjective, with kernel
(1-TY)
annihilated by
p
.

Putting this together, I have constructed a map

\widehat{\mathcal{B}}^{(m)}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}(D)\rightarrow j_{m*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{V}^{\prime}_{m}}

which is surjective, and whose kernel is annihilated by
p
. It follows that it is an isogeny, and hence induces an isomorphism

\widehat{\mathcal{B}}^{(m)}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}(D)\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}{\bf R}j_{m*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{V}^{\prime}_{m}}

in
{\bf D}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}})
. To identify this with the inverse of an isomorphism

{\bf R}j_{m*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{V}^{\prime}_{m}}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}^{(m)}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}(D)

factoring the natural map

\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}^{(m)}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}(D)

I can therefore restrict to
\mathfrak{V}^{\prime}_{m}
, in which case everything has already been worked out explicitly.
∎

6.
Rigidification of
\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}
-modules and constructibility

Suppose now that the formal scheme
\mathfrak{P}
is smooth over
\mathcal{V}
. Thus Berthelot defined in
[
Ber02
, §4.2]
the category

\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}})

of inductive systems of quasi-coherent complexes of
\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}}
-modules, up to isogeny. In this section, my goal is to upgrade
{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}
to a functor

{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}\colon\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\widehat{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{(\bullet)})\rightarrow{\bf D}(\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}}),

and prove that if
\mathcal{M}
is an overholonomic complex of Frobenius type, then
{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}\mathcal{M}
is a constructible complex on
\mathfrak{P}
.

6.1.
\boldsymbol{\mathscr{D}^{(k,0)}}
-modules on admissible blowups

The functor
{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}_{\mathfrak{P}}
passes through the category of quasi-coherent complexes of
\mathcal{O}
-modules on the diagram of all admissible blowups of
\mathfrak{P}
, and the same will be therefore need to be true for its
\mathscr{D}
-module enhancement. In trying to do this directly, one quickly runs into the following problem: if
\pi\colon\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}
is an admissible blowup of a smooth formal scheme
\mathfrak{P}
, then
\pi^{-1}\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{(0)}
does not necessarily act on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}
. Thus
\pi^{*}\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{(0)}
does not have any natural ring structure over which the pullback
\pi^{*}\mathcal{M}
of a
\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathfrak{P}}
-module
\mathcal{M}
could be a module.

To get around this problem, I will need to use the rings of differential operators with congruence level from
[
HSS21
]
. In that article, the authors define, for any
k\geq 0
, a subring

\mathscr{D}^{(k,0)}_{\mathfrak{P}}\subset\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathfrak{P}}

consisting of ‘differential operators of congruence level
k
’. In terms of local co-ordinates
z_{1},\ldots,z_{d}
, with corresponding derivations
\partial_{1},\ldots,\partial_{d}
, there is the usual description

\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathfrak{P}}=\left\{\left.\sum_{\underline{i}=(i_{1},\ldots,i_{d})\in{\mathbb{N}}^{d}}a_{\underline{i}}\partial_{1}^{i_{1}}\ldots\partial_{d}^{i_{d}}\;\right|\;a_{\underline{i}}\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}},\;\text{only finitely many }\neq 0\right\},

and then
\mathscr{D}^{(k,0)}_{\mathfrak{P}}
consists of those elements for which
a_{\underline{i}}\in\mathfrak{m}^{k\left|\underline{i}\right|}=\mathfrak{m}^{k(i_{1}+\ldots+i_{d})}
. In particular,
\mathscr{D}^{(k,0)}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}=\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}
.

If
\pi\colon\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}
is an admissible blowup, then
\pi^{-1}\mathscr{D}^{(k,0)}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}=\pi^{-1}\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}
acts on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}{\mathbb{Q}}}
. Since
\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}
is flat over
\mathcal{V}
, it therefore makes sense to ask whether or not this induces an action of
\pi^{-1}\mathscr{D}^{(k,0)}_{\mathfrak{P}}
on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}
. In
[
HSS21
, Corollary 2.1.15]
the authors show that this will be the case for all sufficiently large
k
. In particular, this will be true if
\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}={\rm Bl}_{\mathcal{I}}(\mathfrak{P})
and
\varpi^{k}\in\mathcal{I}
.

6.1.1 Definition
.

If
\pi\colon\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}
is an admissible blowup, with
\mathfrak{P}
smooth, define
k_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}:=\min\{k\mid\pi^{-1}\mathscr{D}^{(k,0)}_{\mathfrak{P}}\text{ acts on }\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}\}
. If
\rho\colon\mathfrak{P}^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}
is a morphism of admissible blowups, define
k_{\rho}:=\max\{k_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}},k_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime\prime}}\}
.

Thus, for any
k\geq k_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}
it is possible to form the sheaf of rings
\mathscr{D}^{(k,0)}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}:=\pi^{*}\mathscr{D}^{(k,0)}_{\mathfrak{P}}
. If
\mathcal{M}
is a
\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathfrak{P}}
-module, then
\pi^{*}\mathcal{M}
is naturally a
\mathscr{D}^{(k,0)}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}
-module, for all
k\geq k_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}
. More generally, if
\rho\colon\mathfrak{P}^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}
is a morphism of admissible blowups, and
\mathcal{M}
is a
\mathscr{D}^{(k_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}},0)}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}
-module, then
\rho^{*}\mathcal{M}
is naturally a
\mathscr{D}^{(k,0)}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}
-module, for all
k\geq k_{\rho}
.

6.1.2 Definition
.

Let
\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}}
denote the diagram of admissible blowups of
\mathfrak{P}
. A (left)
\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}}}
-module consists of:

•

for each admissible blowup
\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}
, a (left)
\mathscr{D}^{(k_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}},0)}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}
-module
\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}
;

•

for each morphism
\rho\colon\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}
, a morphism
\chi_{\rho}\colon\rho^{*}\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime\prime}}
of (left)
\mathscr{D}^{(k_{\rho},0)}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime\prime}}
-modules,

subject to the condition that, for any commutative triangle

\textstyle{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau}
\scriptstyle{\rho\circ\tau}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}

of admissible blowups,
\chi_{\rho\circ\tau}=\chi_{\tau}\circ\tau^{*}\chi(\rho)
.

Note that the condition
\chi_{\rho\circ\tau}=\chi_{\tau}\circ\tau^{*}\chi(\rho)
can be checked on the underlying
\mathcal{O}
-modules, hence does not depend on the congruence level of any of the terms. There is, of course, an analogous category of
\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathcal{B}_{P_{\bullet}}}
-modules, consisting of projective systems of
\mathscr{D}^{(k_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}})}_{P^{\prime}_{n}}
-modules on each
\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}
,

The major difficulty in working with this category comes from the fact that the varying rings
\mathscr{D}^{(k_{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime},0)}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}
do
not
form a sheaf of rings on
\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}}
, thus a
\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}}}
-module is not strictly speaking a module over a sheaf of rings on a site in any natural way. Despite this, it is nevertheless easy to extend the formalism of
K
-flat and
K
-injective resolutions to the category of
\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}}}
-modules, and therefore do homological algebra as if we were just working in the category of modules over a sheaf of rings on
\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}}
. For example:

6.1.3 Lemma
.

The category of left
\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}}}
-modules is an abelian category with enough injectives.

Proof.

∎

I will denote by
{\bf D}(\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}}})
the derived category of
\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}}}
-modules, and
{\bf D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}}})
its full subcategory on quasi-coherent objects, that is, objects
\mathcal{M}
such that each
\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}
is quasi-coherent as a complex of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}
-modules. For such objects, the maps
\chi_{\rho}
extend to morphisms

{\bf L}\hat{\rho}^{*}\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime\prime}}

of quasi-coherent complexes of
\mathscr{D}^{(k_{\rho},0)}
-modules. There is of course a similar category
{\bf D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathcal{B}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}}})
of inductive systems of such objects, as well as its localisation
\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}}})
. Exactly as in the case of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}
-modules, I can then define a functor

{\bf L}\hat{\pi}^{*}\colon{\bf D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathfrak{P}^{(\bullet)}})\rightarrow{\bf D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathcal{B}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}}}).

Since
\mathscr{D}^{(k_{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime},0)}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}{\mathbb{Q}}}=\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}*}\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}}
, I can also define a functor

{\bf L}\mathrm{sp}^{*}\colon{\bf D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathcal{B}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}}})\rightarrow{\bf D}(\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}}),

which, roughly speaking, applies
\mathrm{sp}^{-1}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}
on each admissible blowup
\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}
, then takes the colimit over both
I_{\mathfrak{P}}^{\rm op}
and
{\mathbb{N}}
, and then finally tensors with
{\mathbb{Q}}
.

6.1.4 Definition
.

The functor

{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}={\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}_{\mathfrak{P}}\colon\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(\bullet)}_{{\mathfrak{P}}})\rightarrow{\bf D}(\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}})

is defined to be the composite

{\bf D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}})\overset{\rm forget}{\longrightarrow}{\bf D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathfrak{P}^{(\bullet)}})\overset{{\bf L}\hat{\pi}^{*}}{\longrightarrow}{\bf D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathcal{B}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}}})\overset{{\bf L}\mathrm{sp}^{*}}{\longrightarrow}{\bf D}(\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}}).

I then define the shifted functor
\mathrm{sp}^{!}=\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{!}:={\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}[-\dim\mathfrak{P}]
.

It is straightforward to check that
{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}
(and therefore the shifted version
\mathrm{sp}^{!}
) descends to the localisation
\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(\bullet)}_{{\mathfrak{P}}})
of
{\bf D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}})
.

6.2.
Compatibility with de Rham pushforwards

Now let
u\colon\mathfrak{P}\rightarrow\mathfrak{Q}
be a smooth morphism of smooth formal
\mathcal{V}
-schemes. Since
u
is in particular flat, the strict transform of an admissible blowup
\mathfrak{Q}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathfrak{Q}
is just the fibre product
\mathfrak{Q}^{\prime}\times_{\mathfrak{Q}}\mathfrak{P}
.

6.2.1 Lemma
.

Let
u\colon\mathfrak{P}\rightarrow\mathfrak{Q}
be a smooth morphism of smooth formal schemes, and
\mathfrak{Q}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathfrak{Q}
an admissible blowup. Then
k_{\mathfrak{Q}^{\prime}\times_{\mathfrak{Q}}\mathfrak{P}}\leq k_{\mathfrak{Q}^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

Straightforward calculation.
∎

I now consider the functor
I_{\mathfrak{Q}}\rightarrow I_{\mathfrak{P}}
given by taking the fibre product over
\mathfrak{Q}
with
\mathfrak{P}
, and restrict the diagram
\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}}
along this functor to produce a diagram
\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}/\mathfrak{Q}}\colon I_{\mathfrak{Q}}\rightarrow{\bf FSch}
taking
\mathfrak{Q}^{\prime}
to
\mathfrak{Q^{\prime}}\times_{\mathfrak{Q}}\mathfrak{P}
. I then consider the following variant of Definition
6.1.2
.

6.2.2 Definition
.

A (left)
\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}/\mathfrak{Q}}}
-module consists of:

•

for each admissible blowup
\mathfrak{Q}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathfrak{Q}
, a (left)
\mathscr{D}^{(k_{\mathfrak{Q}^{\prime}},0)}_{\mathfrak{Q}^{\prime}\times_{\mathfrak{Q}}\mathfrak{P}}
-module
\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{Q}^{\prime}}
;

•

for each morphism
\rho\colon\mathfrak{Q}^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow\mathfrak{Q}^{\prime}
, a morphism
\chi_{\rho}\colon\rho^{*}\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{Q}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{Q}^{\prime\prime}}
of (left)
\mathscr{D}^{(k_{\rho},0)}_{\mathfrak{Q}^{\prime\prime}\times_{\mathfrak{Q}}\mathfrak{P}}
-modules,

subject to the condition that, for any commutative triangle

\textstyle{\mathfrak{Q}^{\prime\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau}
\scriptstyle{\rho\circ\tau}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{Q}^{\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{Q}^{\prime}}

of admissible blowups,
\chi_{\rho\circ\tau}=\chi_{\tau}\circ\tau^{*}\chi(\rho)
.

I can then define the transfer module
\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{Q}}}
as a left
\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}/\mathfrak{Q}}}
-module by taking its restriction to each
\pi\colon\mathfrak{Q}^{\prime}\times_{\mathfrak{Q}}\mathfrak{P}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}
to be
\pi^{*}\mathscr{D}^{(k_{\mathfrak{Q}^{\prime}},0)}_{\mathfrak{P}\rightarrow\mathfrak{Q}}
. The usual side switching operations therefore give a right
\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}/\mathfrak{Q}}}
-module
\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{Q}}\leftarrow\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}}}
, which also comes with the usual structure of a ‘left
u^{-1}\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{Q}}}
-module on
\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}/\mathfrak{Q}}
’, in a sense that I leave to the reader to make precise. I can therefore define a functor

\displaystyle u_{+}\colon{\bf D}(\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathcal{B}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}/\mathfrak{Q}}})

\displaystyle\rightarrow{\bf D}(\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathcal{B}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{Q}}})

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}

\displaystyle\mapsto{\bf R}u_{*}(\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{Q}}\leftarrow\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}}}\otimes^{{\bf L}}_{\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}}}}\mathcal{M})

in the usual way. Finally, I define

u_{+}\colon{\bf D}(\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathcal{B}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}}})\rightarrow{\bf D}(\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathcal{B}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{Q}}})

by composing with the‘restriction’ functor
{\bf D}(\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathcal{B}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}}})\rightarrow{\bf D}(\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathcal{B}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}/\mathfrak{Q}}})
, which exists by Lemma
6.2.1
.

Since
u
is smooth, the Spencer resolutions for
\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathfrak{Q}\leftarrow\mathfrak{P}}
and
\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(m)}_{\mathfrak{Q}\leftarrow\mathfrak{P}}
show that the natural map

\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathfrak{Q}\leftarrow\mathfrak{P}}\otimes_{\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathfrak{P}}}\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(m)}_{\mathfrak{P}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(m)}_{\mathfrak{Q}\leftarrow\mathfrak{P}}

is an isogeny
[
Ber02
, §3.5.5]
. Hence the diagram

\textstyle{\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u_{+}}
\textstyle{\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathfrak{P}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u_{+}^{(0)}}
\textstyle{\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{Q}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathfrak{Q}})}

commutes up to natural isomorphism. Now using the facts that
\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{Q}}\leftarrow\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}},{\mathbb{Q}}}=\pi^{*}\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathfrak{Q}\leftarrow\mathfrak{P},{\mathbb{Q}}}
, and
\mathrm{sp}^{-1}_{\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}/\mathfrak{Q}}}\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{Q}}\leftarrow\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{P}},{\mathbb{Q}}}=\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{Q}_{K}\leftarrow\mathfrak{P}_{K}}
, I obtain a natural base change map

{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}_{\mathfrak{Q}}^{*}\circ u^{(0)}_{+}\rightarrow u_{+}\circ{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{*}

of functors
\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathfrak{P}})\rightarrow{\bf D}(\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{Q}_{K}})
. Note that since
u
is smooth, say of relative dimension
d
, the functor
u_{+}
on the RHS here is what I have previously called
{\bf R}u_{\mathrm{dR}*}[d]
.

6.2.3 Proposition
.

Let
u\colon\mathfrak{P}\rightarrow\mathfrak{Q}
be a smooth morphism of smooth formal schemes of relative dimension
d
, and
\mathcal{M}\in\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathfrak{P}})
. Then the base change map

{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}_{\mathfrak{Q}}u_{+}^{(0)}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathbf{R}u_{\mathrm{dR}*}{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}_{\mathfrak{P}}\mathcal{M}[d]

is an isomorphism. Thus the diagram

\textstyle{\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u_{+}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{!}}
\textstyle{{\bf D}(\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\bf R}u_{\mathrm{dR}*}[2d]}
\textstyle{\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{Q}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{Q}}^{!}}
\textstyle{{\bf D}(\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{Q}_{K}})}

commutes up to natural isomorphism.

Proof.

By taking the stupid filtration on

\mathscr{D}^{(0)}_{\mathfrak{Q}\leftarrow\mathfrak{P}}\otimes^{\bf L}_{\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{(0)}}\mathcal{M}=\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathfrak{P}/\mathfrak{Q}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}\mathcal{M},

I cam reduce to showing that the base change map

{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}_{\mathfrak{Q}}{\bf R}u_{*}(\Omega^{n}_{\mathfrak{P}/\mathfrak{Q}}\widehat{\otimes}^{\bf L}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}\mathcal{M})\rightarrow\mathbf{R}u_{*}{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}_{\mathfrak{P}}(\Omega^{n}_{\mathfrak{P}/\mathfrak{Q}}\widehat{\otimes}^{\bf L}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}\mathcal{M})

is an isomorphism for each fixed
n
. Thanks to Lemma
5.3.4
, this follows from Theorem
5.3.2
.
∎

For example, taking
\mathfrak{Q}=\mathrm{Spf}\left(\mathcal{V}\right)
shows that the natural map

{\bf R}\Gamma_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathfrak{P},\mathcal{M})\rightarrow{\bf R}\Gamma_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathfrak{P}_{K},{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}_{\mathfrak{P}}\mathcal{M})

is an isomorphism in
{\bf D}(K)
. Of course, this case could equally well be deduced slightly more directly from Theorem
5.3.2
.

6.3.
Constructibility of
\bm{\mathrm{sp}^{!}}

I now come to the first main result concerning
\mathrm{sp}^{!}
, namely that it sends dual constructible overholonomic complexes on
\mathfrak{P}
to constructible isocrystals on
\mathfrak{P}_{K}
.

6.3.1 Theorem
.

Let
\mathfrak{P}
be a smooth formal scheme, and
\mathcal{M}\in\mathbf{DCon}_{F}(\mathfrak{P})\subset{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})
. Then
\mathrm{sp}^{!}\mathcal{M}\in\mathbf{Isoc}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(\mathfrak{P})\subset{\bf D}(\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}})
is a constructible isocrystal on
\mathfrak{P}
. If
\mathcal{M}
is supported on some locally closed subscheme
X\hookrightarrow P
, then so is
\mathrm{sp}^{!}\mathcal{M}
.

Before diving into the proof, I need a preparatory lemma, and a special case.

6.3.2 Lemma
.

Let

\textstyle{X^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{Y^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{P}}

be a morphism of l.p. frames such that:

•

u
is smooth and proper, of relative dimension
d
;

g
is proper, and
f
is finite étale;

•

X
is locally (on
Y
) the complement of a hypersurface in
Y
.

Let
\mathcal{M}\in\mathbf{DCon}_{F}(\mathfrak{P})
be a dual constructible module supported
X
. If
\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}\mathbf{R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{X^{\prime}}u^{!}\mathcal{M}
is a constructible isocrystal supported on
X^{\prime}
, then
\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{!}\mathcal{M}
is a constructible isocrystal supported on
X
.

6.3.3 Remark
.

Of course, if I am considering
\mathcal{M}
as an object of
\mathbf{DCon}_{F}(X,Y)
, then
\mathbf{R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{X^{\prime}}u^{!}\mathcal{M}
is simply
f^{!}\mathcal{M}\in\mathbf{DCon}_{F}(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime})
.

Proof.

The question is local on
\mathfrak{P}
, so I can therefore suppose that
\mathfrak{P}
is affine and
X=D(s)
for some
s\in\Gamma(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y})
. I am therefore in the situation of Theorem
3.4.2
. Write
\mathcal{N}:=\mathbf{R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{X^{\prime}}u^{!}\mathcal{M}
, this is a dual constructible module on
\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}
. By Proposition
6.2.3
,

\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\mathfrak{P}}u_{+}\mathcal{N}={\bf R}u_{\mathrm{dR}*}\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}\mathcal{N}[2d]

where
\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}\mathcal{N}
is a constructible isocrystal on
\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}
, supported on
X^{\prime}
. I then consider the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\left]X^{\prime}\right[_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\left]f\right[}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u}
\textstyle{\left]X\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{P}_{K}}

Since
u
is proper, I can identify
{\bf R}u_{\mathrm{dR}!}={\bf R}u_{\mathrm{dR}*}
as functors

{\bf D}^{b}(\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}_{K}})\rightarrow{\bf D}^{b}(\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}}).

Setting
\mathscr{F}:=\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}\mathcal{N}|_{\left]X^{\prime}\right[_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}}\in\mathbf{Isoc}_{\mathrm{cons}}(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime},\mathfrak{P}^{\prime})
, I therefore have

{\bf R}u_{\mathrm{dR}*}\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}\mathcal{N}[2d]=i_{!}{\bf R}\!\left]f\right[_{\mathrm{dR}!}\mathscr{F}[2d].

Now applying Theorem
3.4.2
I deduce that
\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\mathfrak{P}}u_{+}\mathcal{N}
is a constructible isocrystal supported on
X
. Since
\mathcal{M}
is a direct summand of
u_{+}\mathcal{N}
(see Remark
4.5.6
), I can conclude.
∎

6.3.4 Lemma
.

Let
\mathfrak{P}
be a smooth formal scheme,
D\subset P
a divisor, and let
j\colon U:=P\setminus D\rightarrow P
be the complementary open immersion. Suppose that
\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{Isoc}_{F}(U,P)
, and let
\mathcal{M}=\mathrm{sp}_{U!}\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{DCon}_{F}(\mathfrak{P})
.
16
16

16

See §
4.3
and Example
4.4.6
.
Then
\mathrm{sp}^{!}\mathcal{M}\cong j_{*}\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{Isoc}_{\mathrm{cons}}(\mathfrak{P})
.

Proof.

Given the shifts involved, an equivalent way of stating the conclusion is that

{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{*}\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{U+}\mathscr{F}\cong j_{*}\mathscr{F}

in
{\bf D}^{b}(\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}})
. Also, since
j_{*}\mathscr{F}
is a module (not just a complex) the claim is local on
\mathfrak{P}
, which I may therefore assume to be affine. I can also assume that there exists
f\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}
which is a non-zero divisor in
\mathcal{O}_{P}
, such that
D=V(f)\cap P
.

Let
j_{m}\colon V_{m}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}_{K}
be as in the proof of Theorem
5.4.2
, recall that
V_{m}
is affinoid. For
m
large enough,
\mathscr{F}
extends to a vector bundle with integrable connection
\mathscr{F}_{m}
on
V_{m}
, and

j_{*}\mathscr{F}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\underset{m}{\mathrm{colim}}\,j_{m*}\mathscr{F}_{m}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\underset{m}{\mathrm{colim}}\,{\bf R}j_{m*}\mathscr{F}_{m}.

In this case,
\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{U+}\mathscr{F}
is explicitly defined in
[
Ber96b
, §4.4]
by showing that each

\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}*}j_{m*}\mathscr{F}_{m}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}{\bf R}\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}*}{\bf R}j_{m*}\mathscr{F}_{m}

is a coherent
\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{(m)}(D)
-module, for which the
\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{(0)}
-module structure extends uniquely to a continuous
\widehat{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{(m)}
-module structure compatible with that on
\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{(m)}(D)
. Then

\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{U+}\mathscr{F}=\{\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}*}j_{m*}\mathscr{F}_{m}\}_{m\in{\mathbb{N}}}\in\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}}).

It therefore suffices to show that

(6.3.1)

{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{*}{\bf R}\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}*}{\bf R}j_{m*}\mathscr{F}_{m}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}{\bf R}j_{m*}\mathscr{F}_{m}=j_{m*}\mathscr{F}_{m}

in
{\bf D}^{b}(\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}})
. Indeed, if this is the case then everything in sight is just a module, rather than a complex, and so showing compatibility in
m
is straightforward. To prove (
6.3.1
), let
\pi\colon\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}
be an admissible blowup such that the open immersion
j_{m}\colon V_{m}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}_{K}
arises from an open immersion
j_{m}\colon\mathfrak{V}_{m}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}
of formal schemes. I then claim that the natural morphism

{\bf L}\hat{\pi}^{*}{\bf R}\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}*}{\bf R}j_{m*}\mathscr{F}_{m}\rightarrow{\bf R}\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}*}{\bf R}j_{m*}\mathscr{F}_{m}

is an isogeny. To see this, since
V_{m}
is affinoid, and
\mathscr{F}_{m}
is a vector bundle on
V_{m}
, it is in fact a direct summand of a trivial vector bundle. Hence I can reduce to the case
\mathscr{F}_{m}=\mathcal{O}_{V_{m}}
. In this case, the given map can be identified with

{\bf L}\hat{\pi}^{*}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}^{(m)}_{\mathfrak{P}}(D)\rightarrow{\bf R}j_{m*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{V}_{m}^{\prime}},

which was shown to be an isogeny during the proof of Theorem
5.4.2
. I can now compute

\displaystyle{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{*}{\bf R}\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}*}{\bf R}j_{m*}\mathscr{F}_{m}

\displaystyle\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}^{*}{\bf L}\hat{\pi}^{*}{\bf R}\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}*}{\bf R}j_{m*}\mathscr{F}_{m}

\displaystyle\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}^{*}{\bf R}\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}*}{\bf R}j_{m*}\mathscr{F}_{m}

\displaystyle\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}{\bf R}j_{m*}{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}_{\mathfrak{V}_{m}^{\prime}}^{*}{\bf R}\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{V}^{\prime}_{m}*}\mathscr{F}_{m}

\displaystyle\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}{\bf R}j_{m*}\mathscr{F}_{m}

where the first isomorphism follows simply from the definitions, the second is the isogeny proved above, the third follows from Theorem
5.3.2
, and the last from the fact that
\mathscr{F}_{m}
is a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{V_{m}}
-module.
∎

6.3.5 Remark
.

The particular isomorphism
\mathrm{sp}^{!}\mathcal{M}\cong j_{*}\mathscr{F}
constructed can be identified as follows: if we set
\mathfrak{U}=\mathfrak{P}\setminus D
, then on
\mathfrak{U}_{K}
it is simply the shift of the counit
\mathrm{sp}^{*}_{\mathfrak{U}}\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{U}*}\mathscr{F}\rightarrow\mathscr{F}
of the adjunction between
\mathrm{sp}^{*}
and
\mathrm{sp}_{*}
(note that since
\mathcal{M}\!\!\mid_{\mathfrak{U}}
is locally projective,
{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}_{\mathfrak{U}}=\mathrm{sp}^{*}_{\mathfrak{U}}
in this case).

Proof of Theorem
6.3.1
.

The question is local on
\mathfrak{P}
, which I can therefore assume to be affine, in particular
\mathfrak{P}
admits a locally closed immersion into a smooth and proper formal
\mathcal{V}
-scheme. This then means that every frame encountered during the proof will be an l.p. frame. By dévissage, that is, by Proposition
4.3.3
, every
\mathcal{M}\in\mathbf{DCon}_{F}(\mathfrak{P})
is an iterated extension of objects of the form
\mathrm{sp}_{X!}\mathscr{F}
with
X\hookrightarrow P
a smooth locally closed subscheme, with closure
Y
in
P
, and
\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{Isoc}_{F}(X,Y)
. I can therefore assume that
\mathcal{M}
is of this form.

Now, by de Jong’s alterations
[
dJ96
]
, there exists a projective, generically étale morphism
g\colon Y^{\prime}\rightarrow Y
with
Y^{\prime}
smooth. By Noetherian induction on
X
, I can therefore assume that
g^{-1}(X)\rightarrow X
is finite étale, and that
X
is the complement of a hypersurface in
Y
. I then have a morphism of frames of the form

\textstyle{X^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{Y^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}=\widehat{{\mathbb{P}}}^{d}_{\mathfrak{P}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{P}}

with left hand square Cartesian,
Y^{\prime}
smooth,
f
finite étale, and
u
the projection. Appealing to Lemma
6.3.2
, I can replace
(X,Y,\mathfrak{P})
by
(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime},\mathfrak{P}^{\prime})
, in other words I can also assume that
Y
is smooth. Of course, I have now lost the assumption that
\mathfrak{P}
is affine, but I can then further localise on
\mathfrak{P}
to restore it.

Since
\mathfrak{P}
is affine, and
Y
is smooth, there exists a closed immersion of smooth formal schemes
\mathfrak{Y}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{P}
lifting the closed immersion
Y\hookrightarrow P
. Now applying Lemma
6.3.2
to the morphism of frames

\textstyle{\mathfrak{Y}\times_{\mathcal{V}}\mathfrak{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{P}}

(where the diagonal map
Y\rightarrow\mathfrak{Y}\times_{\mathcal{V}}\mathfrak{P}
is the product of the two given immersions, and the right hand vertical map is the second projection), it suffices to prove the claim with
\mathfrak{P}
replaced by
\mathfrak{Y}\times_{\mathcal{V}}\mathfrak{P}
. In other words, I can assume that the closed immersion
\mathfrak{Y}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}
is a section of a smooth morphism
\pi\colon\mathfrak{P}\rightarrow\mathfrak{Y}
.

Let
c
be the codimension of
\mathfrak{Y}
in
\mathfrak{P}
. By further localising on
\mathfrak{P}
if necessary, I can choose functions
t_{1}\ldots,t_{c}\in\Gamma(\mathfrak{P},\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})
such that
\mathfrak{Y}=V(t_{1},\ldots,t_{c})
. For any subset
I\subset\{1,\ldots,c\}
set
\mathfrak{U}_{I}:=\cap_{i\in I}D(t_{i})
. I also set
\mathcal{N}:=\pi^{!}\pi_{+}\mathcal{M}\in\mathbf{DCon}_{F}(\mathfrak{P})
, thus, by the discussion in §
4.3
,
\mathcal{N}=\mathrm{sp}_{\pi^{-1}(X)!}\pi^{*}\mathscr{F}
where
\pi^{*}\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{Isoc}_{F}(\pi^{-1}(X),P)
. Let
j
also denote the inclusion
\pi^{-1}(X)\rightarrow P
, and write
\mathscr{G}:=j_{*}\pi^{*}\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{Isoc}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(\mathfrak{P})
.

There is then the following exact sequence of dual constructible
\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}
-modules on
\mathfrak{P}
:

0\rightarrow\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathcal{N}\rightarrow\bigoplus_{i}\mathbf{R}\underline{\Gamma}_{U_{i}}^{\dagger}\mathcal{N}\rightarrow\ldots\rightarrow\mathbf{R}\underline{\Gamma}_{U_{\{1,\ldots,c\}}}^{\dagger}\mathcal{N}\rightarrow 0,

whose exactness can be seen by applying the t-exact and conservative family of functors
\mathbf{R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{x}
, for
x\in P
a closed point, together with the inclusion-exclusion principle.

It follows from Theorem
5.4.2
and Lemma
6.3.4
that applying
\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{!}
to all except the first term in this exact sequence results in the complex

\mathscr{G}\rightarrow\bigoplus_{i}j_{U_{i}}^{\dagger}\mathscr{G}\rightarrow\ldots\rightarrow j_{U_{\{1,\ldots,c\}}}^{\dagger}\mathscr{G}

of
\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}}
-modules. I therefore deduce that

\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{!}\mathcal{M}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\left[\mathscr{G}\rightarrow\bigoplus_{i}j_{U_{i}}^{\dagger}\mathscr{G}\rightarrow\ldots\rightarrow j_{U_{\{1,\ldots,c\}}}^{\dagger}\mathscr{G}\right]\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}i_{!}i^{*}\mathscr{G},

by using
[
Ber96a
, Proposition 2.1.8]
. This completes the proof.
∎

6.4.
Consequences

I now gather several important consequences of Theorem
6.3.1
, starting with:

6.4.1 Corollary
.

Let
\mathfrak{P}
be a smooth formal scheme. Then
\mathrm{sp}^{!}
induces a functor

\mathrm{sp}^{!}\colon{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})\rightarrow{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(\mathfrak{P})

which is t-exact for the dual constructible t-structure on the source, and the natural t-structure on the target.

Examining the proof of Theorem
6.3.1
, I have also shown the following.

6.4.2 Corollary
.

Let
\mathfrak{P}
be a smooth formal scheme,
i\colon X\rightarrow P
be a smooth, locally closed subscheme, with closure
Y
,
\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{Isoc}_{F}(X,Y)
, and
\mathcal{M}=\mathrm{sp}_{X!}\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{DCon}_{F}(\mathfrak{P})
. Then
\mathrm{sp}^{!}\mathcal{M}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}i_{!}\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{Isoc}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(\mathfrak{P})
.

6.4.3 Remark
.

It’s not straightforward to do explicitly, but the particular isomorphism
\mathrm{sp}^{!}\mathcal{M}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}i_{!}\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{Isoc}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(\mathfrak{P})
thus constructed can be identified by following through the various steps in the proof of Theorem
6.3.1
and reducing to the case considered in Lemma
6.3.4
.

The functor
\mathrm{sp}^{!}
is compatible with many of the cohomological functors introduced so far. Explicitly, if
U\hookrightarrow P\hookleftarrow Z
are complementary open and closed immersions, and
\mathcal{M}\in{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})
, then repeatedly applying Theorem
5.4.2
gives rise to an isomorphism

j_{U}^{\dagger}\mathrm{sp}^{!}\mathcal{M}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\mathrm{sp}^{!}\mathcal{M}(^{\dagger}Z)

in
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(\mathfrak{P})
, which is natural in
\mathcal{M}
. Moreover, this is compatible with the unit of the two adjunctions, in the sense that the diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{sp}^{!}\mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{j_{U}^{\dagger}\mathrm{sp}^{!}\mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{sp}^{!}\mathcal{M}(^{\dagger}Z)}

commutes. Similarly, there exists an isomorphism

{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}_{Z}^{\dagger}\mathrm{sp}^{!}\mathcal{M}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\mathrm{sp}^{!}{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}_{Z}^{\dagger}\mathcal{M},

natural in
\mathcal{M}
, which is again compatible with the counit of the two adjunctions in the sense that the diagram

\textstyle{{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}_{Z}^{\dagger}\mathrm{sp}^{!}\mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{sp}^{!}\mathcal{M}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{sp}^{!}{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}_{Z}^{\dagger}\mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

commutes. This implies that for any locally closed subscheme
i\colon X\rightarrow P
, there is a canonical isomorphism

\mathrm{sp}^{!}{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}_{X}^{\dagger}\mathcal{M}\cong i_{!}i^{-1}\mathrm{sp}^{!}\mathcal{M}

in
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(\mathfrak{P})
.

If now
u\colon\mathfrak{P}\rightarrow\mathfrak{Q}
is a smooth and proper morphism of smooth formal schemes, of relative dimension
d
, and
\mathcal{M}\in{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})
, then Proposition
6.2.3
provides a canonical isomorphism

\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{Q}}^{!}u_{+}\mathcal{M}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}{\bf R}u_{\mathrm{dR}*}\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{!}\mathcal{M}[2d]

inside
{\bf D}^{b}(\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{Q}})
, in particular the RHS is in fact in
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(\mathfrak{Q})
.

Compatibility of
\mathrm{sp}^{!}
with
u^{!}
(and therefore with tensor product) will need to wait until later on, but I can at least record a special case for now. Consider a morphism of l.p. frames

\textstyle{X^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{Y^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{P}}

such that
u
is smooth and proper, of relative dimension
d
,
g
is proper, and
f
is finite étale. By the support claim in Theorem
6.3.1
, I can view
\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\mathfrak{P}}
as a functor

{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X,Y,\mathfrak{P})\rightarrow{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(X,Y,\mathfrak{P}),

and similarly for
\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}
. Thanks to Theorem
3.4.2
, the isomorphisms

\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{!}u_{+}\mathcal{M}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}{\bf R}u_{\mathrm{dR}*}\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}^{!}\mathcal{M}[2d]

induce an isomorphism

\chi_{f}\colon\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{!}\circ f_{+}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}{\bf R}\!\left]f\right[_{\mathrm{dR}*}\circ\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}^{!}

of functors

{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime},\mathfrak{P}^{\prime})\rightarrow{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(X,Y,\mathfrak{P}).

The adjunctions
(f^{!},f_{+})
and
(f^{*},{\bf R}\!\left]f\right[_{\mathrm{dR}*})
mean that I can define a morphism

\psi_{f}\colon f^{*}\circ\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{!}\rightarrow\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}^{!}\circ f^{!}

of functors

{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X,Y,\mathfrak{P})\rightarrow{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime},\mathfrak{P}^{\prime})

by taking the adjoint of the morphism

\displaystyle\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{!}

\displaystyle\rightarrow{\bf R}\!\left]f\right[_{\mathrm{dR}*}\circ\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}^{!}\circ f^{!}

\displaystyle\overset{\chi_{f}^{-1}}{\longrightarrow}\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{!}\circ f_{+}\circ f^{!}

obtained by applying
\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{!}
to the morphism
{\rm id}\rightarrow f_{+}\circ f^{!}
.

6.4.4 Proposition
.

The morphism
\psi_{f}
is an isomorphism, and gives rise to a commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\rm Hom}(f^{!}\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{f}}
\textstyle{{\rm Hom}(\mathcal{M},f_{+}\mathcal{N})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\chi_{f}}
\textstyle{{\rm Hom}(f^{*}\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{!}\mathcal{M},\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}^{!}\mathcal{N})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{{\rm Hom}(\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{!}\mathcal{M},{\bf R}\!\left]f\right[_{\mathrm{dR}*}\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}^{!}\mathcal{N})}

for any
\mathcal{M}\in{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X,Y)
,
\mathcal{N}\in{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime})
.

Proof.

The fact that the diagram commutes just follows from the definition of
\psi_{f}
via adjunction, the key point is to show that
\psi_{f}
is an isomorphism. But again, by compatibility of
\mathrm{sp}^{!}
with
\underline{{\bf R}\Gamma}^{\dagger}
, this reduces to the case when
X
is a point, which is straightforward.
∎

7.
Logarithmic variations

Before I can prove my main result, I will need some elementary logarithmic versions of some of the results proved in previous sections. I have not tried especially hard to frame these results in the most general possible content, but have instead concentrated on giving quick proofs of the specific results that I need.

7.1.
Log convergent isocrystals

The general theory of log convergent isocrystals was developed in
[
Shi02
]
, I will recall here some of the fundamental definitions and results that I need. Throughout I will equip the bases
\mathrm{Spec}\left(k\right)
and
\mathrm{Spf}\left(\mathcal{V}\right)
with the trivial log structure.

A fine log variety (over
k
) will mean a fine log scheme over
k
whose underlying
k
-scheme is a variety. If
X
is smooth variety over
k
, and
D\subset X
is a normal crossings divisor, I will let
M(D)
denote the log structure on
X
associated to
D
, and
(X,M(D))
the corresponding log scheme. I will use similar notation
(\mathfrak{X},M(\mathfrak{D}))
in the case of a relative crossings divisor
\mathfrak{D}\subset\mathfrak{X}
inside a smooth formal scheme over
\mathcal{V}
.

7.1.1 Definition
.

Let
(X,M)
be a fine log variety. Then an
enlargement
of
(X,M)
is a fine log scheme
(Z,M_{Z})\rightarrow(X,M)
, together with an exact closed immersion
(Z,M_{Z})\rightarrow(\mathfrak{T},M_{\mathfrak{T}})
into a fine log formal scheme over
\mathcal{V}
, such that
T_{\rm red}\subset Z
.

There is the obvious notion of a morphism of enlargements of
(X,M)
.

7.1.2 Definition
.

A convergent log isocrystal on
(X,M)
consists of the following data:

(1)

for every enlargement
(\mathfrak{T},M_{\mathfrak{T}})
of
(X,M)
, a coherent
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{T}{\mathbb{Q}}}
-module
\mathscr{F}_{\mathfrak{T}}
;

(2)

for every morphism of enlargements
g\colon(\mathfrak{T}^{\prime},M_{\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}})\rightarrow(\mathfrak{T},M_{\mathfrak{T}})
a ‘transition’ isomorphism
g^{*}\mathscr{F}_{\mathfrak{T}}\rightarrow\mathscr{F}_{\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}}
of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}{\mathbb{Q}}}
-modules,

The transition isomorphisms are required to satisfy an appropriate cocycle condition. In fact, Shiho considers two variations, where
\mathscr{F}
is considered as a sheaf on
\mathfrak{T}
with respect to the Zariski and étale topologies. He then shows in
[
Shi02
, Proposition 2.1.21]
these give rise to equivalent theories.

There is an obvious notion of a morphism of convergent log isocrystals on
(X,M)
, and I will denote the resulting category by
\mathbf{Isoc}^{\circ}(X,M)
.

7.1.3 Remark
.

My choice of notation here reflects the fact that I am using
\mathbf{Isoc}(X)
, and not
\mathbf{Isoc}^{\dagger}(X)
, to denote the category of overconvergent isocrystals on a variety
X
. Thus, when the log structure
M
is trivial,
\mathbf{Isoc}^{\circ}(X)
is the category of convergent isocrystals on
X
, which has previously been denoted
\mathbf{Isoc}(X,X)
.

As with the case of ordinary (that is, non-logarithmic) convergent isocrystals, these objects can be described in terms of their ‘realisations’ in the world of rigid analytic geometry. Following Shiho, suppose that there is an exact closed immersion

(X,M)\rightarrow(\mathfrak{P},L)

of fine log formal schemes over
\mathcal{V}
, such that
(\mathfrak{P},L)
is log smooth over
\mathcal{V}
.
17
17

17

In fact, Shiho works in much greater generality than this, but this special case is technically simpler and will suffice for me.
Then the tube
\left]X\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}
admits a natural log structure coming from the log structure induced by
L
on
\mathfrak{P}_{K}
, and is log smooth over
K
. In
[
Shi02
, §2.1,§2.2]
Shiho constructs a functor

\displaystyle\mathbf{Isoc}^{\circ}(X,M)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathbf{Coh}_{\nabla}(\left]X\right[_{\mathfrak{P}},L)

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathscr{F}_{\left]X\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}}

from the category of log convergent isocrystals on
(X,M)
to the category
\mathbf{Coh}_{\nabla}(\left]X\right[_{\mathfrak{P}},L)
of coherent
\mathcal{O}_{\left]X\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}}
-modules with integrable log connection. In general, it is not clear whether or not this ‘realisation on
(\mathfrak{P},L)
’ functor is fully faithful, in order to obtain full faithfulness it is necessary to impose local freeness conditions on convergent isocrystals.

7.1.4 Definition
.

Let
(X,M)
be a fine log variety over
k
, and
\mathscr{F}
a convergent log isocrystals on
(X,M)
. Then
\mathscr{F}
is locally free if, for all enlargements
(\mathfrak{T},M_{\mathfrak{T}})
of
(X,M)
, the coherent
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{T}{\mathbb{Q}}}
-module
\mathscr{F}_{\mathfrak{T}}
is locally projective.

It is then a consequence of
[
Shi02
, Corollary 2.3.9]
that the restriction of the realisation functor to locally free isocrystals is fully faithful. Moreover, if we are given
\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{Isoc}^{\circ}(X,M)
we can actually test whether or not it is locally free by taking its realisation on
(\mathfrak{P},L)
(this follows from
[
Shi02
, Proposition 2.2.7]
).

7.1.5.
Frobenius structures

Since log convergent isocrystals are clearly functorial in
(X,M)
, there is a well-defined Frobenius pullback functor
F^{*}\colon\mathbf{Isoc}^{\circ}(X,M)\rightarrow\mathbf{Isoc}^{\circ}(X,M)
, which makes it possible to talk about Frobenius structures on convergent log isocrystals. The category of convergent log
F
-isocrystals on
(X,M)
, that is, the category of convergent log isocrystals on
(X,M)
equipped with a Frobenius structure, will be denoted
F\text{-}\mathbf{Isoc}^{\circ}(X,M)
. In certain cases, the presence of a Frobenius structure is enough to guarantee local freeness.

7.1.6 Lemma
.

Let
X
be a smooth variety, and
D\subset X
a normal crossings divisor. Then every convergent log
F
-isocrystal
\mathscr{F}
on
(X,M(D))
is locally free.

Proof.

It suffices to prove the claim over some étale cover of
X
, so I can therefore assume that the pair
(X,D)
lifts to a pair
(\mathfrak{X},\mathfrak{D})
consisting of a smooth formal scheme
\mathfrak{X}
over
\mathcal{V}
and a strict relative normal crossings divisor
\mathfrak{D}\subset\mathfrak{X}
. In fact, I can assume that
\mathfrak{X}=\mathrm{Spf}\left(R\right)
is affine and connected, and that there exists an étale morphism
\mathfrak{X}\rightarrow\widehat{{\mathbb{A}}}^{d}_{\mathcal{V}}
, with co-ordinates
z_{1},\ldots,z_{d}
on the target, such that
\mathfrak{D}
is (the pullback of) the strict normal crossings divisor
V(z_{1}\ldots z_{c})
for some
c\leq d
. In particular, the standard lift of Frobenius on
\widehat{{\mathbb{A}}}^{d}_{\mathcal{V}}
extends to an endomorphism of
\mathfrak{X}
lifting the absolute (
q
-power) Frobenius on
X
. Let
\sigma:\colon R\rightarrow R
denote the ring homomorphism induced by the Frobenius lift. It is then enough to prove the local freeness and of the realisation of
\mathscr{F}
on
\mathfrak{X}_{K}
.

The proof of this is a standard fitting ideal argument: it suffices to show that the only ideals
I\subset R_{K}
stable under
\sigma
, in the sense that
\sigma(I)R_{K}=I
, are the zero and unit ideals. Setting
I^{+}=I\cap R
, this is a
\pi
-adically saturated ideal of
R
, such that
\sigma(I^{+})R\subset I^{+}
. Since
\sigma
is finite flat, it follows that
\sigma(I_{0})R
is also saturated, hence in fact
\sigma(I^{+})R=I^{+}
. Again, it suffices to show that the only any such ideals are the zero and unit ideals. Let
I_{0}=I^{+}\otimes_{\mathcal{V}}k
be the reduction of
I^{+}
modulo
\pi
, this is therefore a Frobenius stable ideal in
R_{k}
, in the sense that
F(I_{0})R_{k}=I_{0}
