, and it is enough to show that the only such ideals are the zero and unit ideals.

But if
F(I_{0})R_{k}=I_{0}
then
I_{0}=I_{0}^{n}
for all
n\geq 1
, and so
I_{0}=\bigcap_{n}I_{0}^{n}
. Since
R_{k}
is smooth, connected and Noetherian, it follows from Krull’s intersection theorem
[
Eis95
, Corollary 5.4]
that either
I_{0}=\{0\}
or
I_{0}=R
.
∎

7.2.
A vanishing result in log rigid cohomology

The main result I will need in log rigid cohomology is a certain vanishing result, appearing as Theorem
7.2.1
below. It will take a little bit of effort to setup and formulate this result, but this is mostly an issue of keeping track of notation, the underlying geometry is actually rather simple.

Anyway, Theorem
7.2.1
will apply in the situation when I have a smooth formal scheme
\mathfrak{P}
, together with a strict relative normal crossings divisor
\mathfrak{D}\subset\mathfrak{P}
. In this case, I will let
\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}
denote the log scheme
(\mathfrak{P},M(\mathfrak{D}))
. It’s generic fibre
\mathfrak{P}_{K}^{\sharp}
is therefore a logarithmic analytic variety over
K
, and I will let
\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}_{K}}\subset\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}}
denote the corresponding sheaf of logarithmic (algebraic) differential operators. For example, if
z_{1},\ldots,z_{d}
are local co-ordinates on
\mathfrak{P}
, such that
\mathfrak{D}
is defined by
z_{1}\ldots z_{c}=0
, and
\partial_{1},\ldots,\partial_{d}
are the corresponding derivations, then
\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}_{K}}
is generated as a sub-
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}}
-algebra of
\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}}
by
z_{1}\partial_{1},\ldots,z_{c}\partial_{c},\partial_{c+1},\ldots,\partial_{d}
. In particular, any constructible isocrystal on
\mathfrak{P}
can be viewed a
\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}_{K}}
-module via restriction of scalars along
\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}^{\sharp}}\rightarrow\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}}
.

Let
\mathfrak{D}_{1},\ldots,\mathfrak{D}_{c}
denote the irreducible components of
\mathfrak{D}
, with special fibres
D_{1},\ldots,D_{c}
. For each
J\subset\{1,\ldots,c\}
, set

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}_{J}=\cap_{j\in J}\mathfrak{D}_{j},\;\;

\displaystyle\;\;D_{J}=\mathfrak{D}_{J,k}=\cap_{j\in J}D_{j}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}^{(J)}=\cup_{j\notin J}\mathfrak{D}_{j},\;\;

\displaystyle\;\;D^{(J)}=\mathfrak{D}_{k}^{(J)}=\cup_{j\notin J}D_{j}.

The log structure
M\mathfrak{D})
on
\mathfrak{P}
can be pulled back to each
\mathfrak{D}_{J}
, giving rise to a log formal scheme
\mathfrak{D}_{J}^{\sharp}
. Similarly, I let
\left]D_{J}\right[_{\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}}
denote the tube of
D_{J}
equipped with the log structure obtained by pulling back the log structure
M(\mathfrak{D}_{K})
from
\mathfrak{P}_{K}
of the log structure
M(\mathfrak{D}_{K})
. I will let
\mathscr{D}_{\left]D_{J}\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}^{\sharp}}
denote the ring of (algebraic) log differential operators on
\left]D_{J}\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}^{\sharp}
, or in other words the restriction of
\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}^{\sharp}}
to
\left]D_{J}\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}
.

There is also a second is natural log structure on
\mathfrak{D}_{J}
, namely that induced by the strict normal crossings divisor

\mathfrak{D}_{J}\cap\mathfrak{D}^{(J)}\subset\mathfrak{D}_{J}.

I will denote the resulting log formal scheme by
\mathfrak{D}_{J}^{\flat}=(\mathfrak{D}_{J},M(\mathfrak{D}_{J}\cap\mathfrak{D}^{(J)}))
, and denote the ring of (algebraic) log differential operators on its generic fibre
\mathfrak{D}_{J,K}^{\flat}
by
\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{D}_{J,K}^{\flat}}
.

Now, if
\mathscr{F}
is a locally free log convergent isocrystal on the special fibre
P^{\sharp}
of
\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}
, I can take its realisation as a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}}
-module with integrable log connection, which I will also denote by
\mathscr{F}
. There is then an induced integrable log connection on the twist
\mathscr{F}(\mathfrak{D}_{K})
of
\mathscr{F}
, coming from the canonical log connection on the line bundle
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}}(\mathfrak{D}_{K})
. In particular,
\mathscr{F}(\mathfrak{D}_{K})
is a
\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}^{\sharp}}
-module, which can then be restricted to any tube
\left]D_{J}\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}
.

7.2.1 Theorem
.

Let
\mathscr{F}\in F\text{-}\mathbf{Isoc}^{\circ}(P^{\sharp})
be a log convergent
F
-isocrystal on
P^{\sharp}
, and let
\mathscr{G}\in\mathbf{Isoc}_{\mathrm{cons}}(\mathfrak{P})
a constructible isocrystal on
\mathfrak{P}
. Then, for any
J\subset\{1,\ldots,c\}
,

{\bf R}{\rm Hom}_{\mathscr{D}_{\left]D_{J}\right[^{\sharp}_{\mathfrak{P}}}}(\mathscr{F}(\mathfrak{D}_{K}),\mathscr{G})=0.

7.2.2 Remark
.

The crucial observation of the proof is that the presence of a Frobenius structure forces
\mathscr{F}
to have nilpotent residues along
\mathfrak{D}_{K}
, which then implies that the residues of the twist
\mathscr{F}(\mathfrak{D}_{K})
along
\mathfrak{D}_{K}
are in fact
isomorphisms
.

Proof.

The question is local on
\mathfrak{P}
, so I can assume that there exists an étale map
\mathfrak{P}\rightarrow\widehat{{\mathbb{A}}}^{d}_{\mathcal{V}}
, with co-ordinates
z_{1},\ldots,z_{d}
on the target, such that
\mathfrak{D}=V(z_{1}\ldots z_{c})
for some
c\leq d
, and
\mathfrak{D}_{J}=V(z_{1},\ldots,z_{b})
for
b=\#J\leq c
. The functions
z_{b+1},\ldots,z_{d}
induce an étale map
\mathfrak{D}_{J}\rightarrow\widehat{{\mathbb{A}}}^{d-b}_{\mathcal{V}}
, and adding in the remaining functions
z_{1},\ldots,z_{b}
gives an étale map
\mathfrak{D}_{J}\times_{\mathcal{V}}\widehat{{\mathbb{A}}}^{b}_{\mathcal{V}}\rightarrow\widehat{{\mathbb{A}}}^{d}_{\mathcal{V}}
. I now let
\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}
be the fibre product

\textstyle{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{D}_{J}\times_{\mathcal{V}}\widehat{{\mathbb{A}}}^{b}_{\mathcal{V}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widehat{{\mathbb{A}}}^{d}_{\mathcal{V}}.}

The closed immersion
\mathfrak{D}_{J}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}
canonically extends to a closed immersion
\mathfrak{D}_{J}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}
whose composition with the projection
\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathfrak{D}_{J}\times_{\mathcal{V}}\widehat{{\mathbb{A}}}^{b}_{\mathcal{V}}
is the identity on the first factor, and the zero section on the second. This gives rise to a section of the étale map
u^{-1}(\mathfrak{D}_{J})\rightarrow\mathfrak{D}_{J}
, so there exists an open subspace of
\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}
on which
u^{-1}(\mathfrak{D}_{J})\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\mathfrak{D}_{J}
.

The upshot of all of this is that after replacing
\mathfrak{P}
by this open subspace of
\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}
, I can assume that there exists a smooth morphism
\mathfrak{P}\rightarrow\mathfrak{D}_{J}
of which the canonical inclusion is a section. Hence by the weak fibration theorem, there exists an isomorphism

\left]D_{J}\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{D}}^{b}_{\mathfrak{D}_{J,K}}(0;1^{-})

identifying
\mathfrak{D}_{J,K}\rightarrow\left]D_{J}\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}
is identified with the zero section of
{\mathbb{D}}^{b}_{\mathfrak{D}_{J,K}}(0;1^{-})
. In fact, this isomorphism can be upgraded to include log structures as follows. As above, let
\mathfrak{D}_{J}^{\flat}
denote the log formal scheme given by
\mathfrak{D}_{J}
equipped with the log structure coming from the strict normal crossings divisor
\mathfrak{D}_{J,K}\cap\mathfrak{D}_{K}^{(J)}
, and let
\mathfrak{D}_{J,K}^{\flat}
denote its special fibre. If
z_{1},\ldots,z_{b}
are co-ordinates on
{\mathbb{D}}^{b}_{K}(0;1^{-})
, then
{\mathbb{D}}^{b}_{K}(0;1^{-})
is equipped with the log structure coming from the strict normal crossings divisor
V(z_{1}\ldots z_{b})
. Then the above isomorphism can be upgraded to an isomorphism

\left]D_{J}\right[^{\sharp}_{\mathfrak{P}}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\mathfrak{D}_{J,K}^{\flat}\times_{K}{\mathbb{D}}^{b}_{K}(0;1^{-})

of log analytic varieties. Let
\pi
denote the first projection
\left]D_{J}\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}^{\sharp}\rightarrow\mathfrak{D}_{J,K}^{\flat}
.

I now want to apply
[
Shi10
, Proposition 2.12]
to show that
\mathscr{F}|_{\left]D_{J}\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}}
is an iterated extension of pullbacks of locally free log integrable connections on
\mathfrak{D}_{J,K}^{\flat}
along
\pi
, at least after possibly localising to ensure that
\mathfrak{D}_{J}
is affine and connected. For Shiho’s result to apply, I need to check two conditions: firstly, that the supremum norm on
\Gamma(\mathfrak{D}_{J,K},\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{D}_{J,K}})
is multiplicative, and secondly, that
\mathscr{F}
has nilpotent residues along
\mathfrak{D}_{K}
. The first of these simply follows from the fact that
\mathfrak{D}_{J}
is smooth over
\mathcal{V}
, and the second from the fact that
\mathscr{F}
has a Frobenius structure.

Therefore, I can assume that there exists a locally free log integrable connection
\mathscr{F}_{0}
on
\mathfrak{D}_{J,K}^{\flat}
such that
\mathscr{F}|_{\left]D_{J}\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}}=\pi^{*}\mathscr{F}_{0}
. In a similar vein, it follows from Theorem
3.2.1
there exists a constructible isocrystal
\mathscr{G}_{0}
on
\mathfrak{D}_{J}
such that

\mathscr{G}|_{\left]D_{J}\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}}=\pi^{*}\mathscr{G}_{0}.

I now set
\mathscr{H}_{0}:=\mathscr{G}_{0}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{D}_{J,K}}}\mathscr{F}_{0}^{\vee}(-\mathfrak{D}_{J,K}\cap\mathfrak{D}_{K}^{(J)})
, which is thus a
\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{D}^{\flat}_{J,K}}
-module, and write
\mathscr{T}
for the divisor
V(z_{1}\ldots z_{b})\subset\left]D_{J}\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}
arising via pullback from
{\mathbb{D}}^{b}_{K}(0;1^{-})
. Now I can calculate

\displaystyle{\bf R}{\rm Hom}_{\mathscr{D}_{\left]D_{J}\right[^{\sharp}_{\mathfrak{P}}}}(\mathscr{F}(\mathfrak{D}_{K}),\mathscr{G})

\displaystyle={\bf R}{\rm Hom}_{\mathscr{D}_{\left]D_{J}\right[^{\sharp}_{\mathfrak{P}}}}(\mathcal{O}_{\left]D_{J}\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}}(\mathscr{T}),\pi^{*}\mathscr{H}_{0})

\displaystyle={\bf R}{\rm Hom}_{\mathscr{D}_{\left]D_{J}\right[^{\sharp}_{\mathfrak{P}}}}(\mathcal{O}_{\left]D_{J}\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}},\pi^{*}\mathscr{H}_{0}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\left]D_{J}\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}}}\mathcal{O}_{\left]D_{J}\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}}(-\mathscr{T}))

\displaystyle={\bf R}{\rm Hom}_{\mathfrak{D}_{J,K}^{\flat}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{D}_{J,K}},{\bf R}\pi_{\mathrm{dR}*}(\pi^{*}\mathscr{H}_{0}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\left]D_{J}\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}}}\mathcal{O}_{\left]D_{J}\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}}(-\mathscr{T}))).

I will show that in fact

{\bf R}\pi_{\mathrm{dR}*}(\pi^{*}\mathscr{H}_{0}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\left]D_{J}\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}}}\mathcal{O}_{\left]D_{J}\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}}(-\mathscr{T}))=0

which clearly suffices. To show this, note that since
\mathscr{H}_{0}
is a constructible locally free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{D}_{J,K}}
-module, Proposition
2.1.6
implies that, after replacing

\pi\colon\left]D_{J}\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}={\mathbb{D}}^{b}_{\mathfrak{D}_{J,K}}(0;1^{-})\rightarrow\mathfrak{D}_{J,K}

by the projection

\pi_{\rho}\colon{\mathbb{D}}^{b}_{\mathfrak{D}_{J,K}}(0;\rho)\rightarrow\mathfrak{D}_{J,K}

from the closed polydisc of radius
\rho<1
, the projection formula

{\bf R}\pi_{\rho\mathrm{dR}*}(\pi^{*}\mathscr{H}_{0}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\left]D_{J}\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}}}\mathcal{O}_{\left]D_{J}\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}}(-\mathscr{T}))=\mathscr{H}_{0}\otimes^{{\bf L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{D}_{J,K}}}{\bf R}\pi_{\rho\mathrm{dR}*}\mathcal{O}_{\left]D_{J}\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}}(-\mathscr{T}).

holds. Since

{\bf R}\pi_{\mathrm{dR}*}(\pi^{*}\mathscr{H}_{0}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\left]D_{J}\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}}}\mathcal{O}_{\left]D_{J}\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}}(-\mathscr{T}))={\bf R}\underset{\rho}{\mathrm{lim}}\,{\bf R}\pi_{\rho\mathrm{dR}*}(\pi^{*}\mathscr{H}_{0}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\left]D_{J}\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}}}\mathcal{O}_{\left]D_{J}\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}}(-\mathfrak{T}_{K})),

it is enough to show that the pro-system

{\bf R}\pi_{\rho\mathrm{dR}*}\mathcal{O}_{\left]D_{J}\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}}(-\mathscr{T})

has zero transition maps. Letting

\pi_{\rho^{-}}\colon{\mathbb{D}}^{b}_{\mathfrak{D}_{J,K}}(0;\rho^{-})\rightarrow\mathfrak{D}_{J,K}

denote the projection from the open polydisc, it is then enough to show that

{\bf R}\pi_{\rho^{-}\mathrm{dR}*}\mathcal{O}_{\left]D_{J}\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}}(-\mathscr{T})=0.

To see this, I can replace the log structure on
\mathfrak{D}_{J,K}
by the trivial one, and the log structure on
{\mathbb{D}}^{b}_{\mathfrak{D}_{J,K}}(0;\rho^{-})
by that coming from the strict normal crossings divisor
\mathscr{T}=V(z_{1}\ldots z_{b})
. Replacing
\mathfrak{D}_{J,K}
be an arbitrary smooth analytic variety
V
makes it possible to argue via induction and reduce to the case
b=1
, that is, the case of the projection

\pi_{\rho^{-}}\colon{\mathbb{D}}^{1}_{V}(0;\rho^{-})\rightarrow V.

for a smooth analytic variety
V
. The target here has the trivial log structure, and the source the log structure coming from the zero section. Thus, arguing locally on
V
, and appealing to Theorems A and B for non-Archimedean quasi-Stein spaces
[
Kie67
, Satz 2.4]
, I can reduce to calculating the cohomology of the complex

zR\left\{\rho^{-1}z\right\}\overset{z\partial_{z}}{\longrightarrow}zR\left\{\rho^{-1}z\right\}

where
R
is an affinoid algebra over
K
, and
R\left\{\rho^{-1}z\right\}
is the ring of functions on the open disc of radius
\rho
over
R
. It is then a straightforward calculation that this cohomology vanishes.
∎

7.3.
Rigidification of log
\boldsymbol{\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}}
-modules

For the logarithmic analogue of the
\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}
-module side of the picture, I will stick to log formal schemes coming from strict normal crossings divisors on smooth formal schemes. Thus I will let
\mathfrak{P}
be a smooth formal scheme,
\mathfrak{D}\subset\mathfrak{P}
a strict normal crossings divisor relative to
\mathcal{V}
, and
\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}=(\mathfrak{P},M(\mathfrak{D}))
the corresponding log formal scheme. There is therefore the logarithmic analogue
\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}{\mathbb{Q}}}
of Berthelot’s ring of overconvergent differential operators, as defined in
[
Mon02
]
and
[
Car09a
]
. As in the non-logarithmic case,

\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}{\mathbb{Q}}}:=\underset{m}{\mathrm{colim}}\,\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(m)}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathbb{Q}}

where
\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(m)}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}}
is the
p
-adic completion of the sheaf of level
m
differential operators on
\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}
. The isogeny category
\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}})
of ind-complexes of
\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}}
-modules is defined exactly as in the non-logarithmic case, and actually taking the colimit induces an equivalence

\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}^{b}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{coh}}(\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}})\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}{\mathbb{Q}}}),

see
[
CT12
, §1.2]
. The method of §
6
works
mutatis mutandis
in the logarithmic case to define

\mathrm{sp}^{!}=\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}}={\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}}^{*}[-\dim\mathfrak{P}]\colon\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}})\rightarrow{\bf D}(\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}^{\sharp}}),

where
\mathfrak{P}_{K}^{\sharp}
is the generic fibre of
\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}
, considered as a log analytic variety. It follows from the construction that
\mathrm{sp}^{!}
commutes with pullback along the morphism
\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}
. The approach of §
6
can also be followed through in the logarithmic case to prove the following.

7.3.1 Proposition
.

For any
\mathcal{M}\in\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}})
,
{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}}
induces a base change isomorphism

{\bf R}\Gamma_{{\rm log}\text{-}\mathrm{dR}}(\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp},\mathcal{M})\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}{\bf R}\Gamma_{{\rm log}\text{-}\mathrm{dR}}(\mathfrak{P}_{K}^{\sharp},{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{*}\mathcal{M})

which is natural in
\mathcal{M}
.

7.3.2 Remark
.

The definition of
{\bf R}\Gamma_{{\rm log}\text{-}\mathrm{dR}}(\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp},\mathcal{M})
is analogous to the non-logarithmic case, namely if
\mathcal{M}=\{\mathcal{M}^{(m)}\}_{m\in{\mathbb{N}}}
then

{\bf R}\Gamma_{\log\text{-}\mathrm{dR}}(\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp},\mathcal{M})=\underset{m}{\mathrm{colim}}\,{\bf R}\Gamma_{\log\text{-}\mathrm{dR}}(\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}^{(m)})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathbb{Q}}.

In will also need a very rudimentary logarithmic version of the specialisation functors considered in §
4.3
. Suppose, then, that
\mathscr{F}
is a locally free convergent log isocrystal on the special fibre
P^{\sharp}
of
\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}
, viewed as a module with integrable logarithmic connection on
\mathfrak{P}_{K}^{\sharp}
. Then
\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}*}\mathscr{F}={\bf R}\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}*}\mathscr{F}
is naturally an
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}
-coherent
\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}{\mathbb{Q}}}
-module, and is moreover coherent as an
\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}{\mathbb{Q}}}
-module by
[
Car09a
, Théorème 4.15]
. As in the non-logarithmic case, it is straightforward to verify that there is a canonical isomorphism

{\bf L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}^{*}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}}\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}*}\mathscr{F}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\mathscr{F}

of
\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}^{\sharp}}
-modules.

8.
The overconvergent Riemann–Hilbert correspondence

I can now finally prove the main result of this article.

8.0.1 Theorem
.

Let
\mathfrak{P}
be a smooth formal scheme. Then the functor

\mathrm{sp}^{!}\colon{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})\rightarrow{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(\mathfrak{P})

is an equivalence of categories.

As in §
3.2
, this reduces, via dévissage, to the following derived full faithfulness assertion.

8.0.2 Theorem
.

Let
\mathfrak{P}
be a smooth formal scheme,
j\colon X\rightarrow P
a locally closed subscheme, smooth over
k
, with closure
Y
in
P
. Suppose
\mathscr{F}\in F\text{-}\mathbf{Isoc}(X,Y)
, and set
\mathcal{M}:=\mathrm{sp}_{X!}\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{DCon}_{F}(\mathfrak{P})
. Then, for any
\mathcal{N}\in\mathbf{DCon}_{F}(\mathfrak{P})
, the map

{\bf R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}}(\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N})\rightarrow{\bf R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}}}(\mathrm{sp}^{!}\mathcal{M},\mathrm{sp}^{!}\mathcal{N})

is an isomorphism in
{\bf D}(K)
.

Proof.

To begin with, the question is local on
\mathfrak{P}
, which I can therefore assume to be affine. As in the proof of Theorem
6.3.1
, one consequence of this is that every formal scheme appearing in the proof will admit a locally closed embedding into a smooth and proper formal scheme.

The fact that
\mathcal{M}
is supported on
Y
, together with compatibility of
\mathrm{sp}^{!}
with the natural morphism

{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}_{Y}^{\dagger}\rightarrow\mathrm{id}

means that there is a commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\bf R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}}(\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\bf R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}}}(\mathrm{sp}^{!}\mathcal{M},\mathrm{sp}^{!}\mathcal{N})}
\textstyle{{\bf R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}}}(\mathrm{sp}^{!}\mathcal{M},{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}_{Y}^{\dagger}\mathrm{sp}^{!}\mathcal{N})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{{\bf R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}}(\mathcal{M},{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}_{Y}^{\dagger}\mathcal{N})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{{\bf R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}}}(\mathrm{sp}^{!}\mathcal{M},\mathrm{sp}^{!}{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}_{Y}^{\dagger}\mathcal{N})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\cong}

Hence, replacing
\mathcal{N}
with
\underline{{\bf R}\Gamma}^{\dagger}\mathcal{N}
, I can assume that
\mathcal{N}
is supported on
Y
.

Now, the semistable reduction theorem for
F
-isocrystals
[
Ked11
, Theorem 2.4.4]
shows that there exists a morphism of pairs
(f,g)\colon(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{Y})\rightarrow(X,Y)
such that:

•

\widetilde{Y}
is smooth;

•

g
is projective and generically étale;

•

\widetilde{X}=g^{-1}(X)
;

•

\widetilde{D}:=\widetilde{Y}\setminus\widetilde{X}
is a strict normal crossings divisor in
\widetilde{Y}
;

•

f^{*}\mathscr{F}\in F\text{-}\mathbf{Isoc}(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{Y})
extends to a convergent log
F
-isocrystal on the log smooth log scheme
(\widetilde{Y},M(\widetilde{D}))
.

Since
g
is projective, I can extend
(f,g)
to a morphism of frames

\textstyle{\widetilde{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{\widetilde{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathfrak{P}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{P}}

where
u
is smooth and projective. By Noetherian induction on
X
, I am free to replace
X
with an open subscheme, hence I can assume moreover that
f
is finite étale.

Now, by Proposition
4.5.4
,
\mathcal{N}\in{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X,Y,\mathfrak{P})\subset{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})
is a direct summand of
f_{+}f^{!}\mathcal{N}
, so I can replace
\mathcal{N}
by
f_{+}f^{!}\mathcal{N}
. Then, by Proposition
6.4.4
, combined with Proposition
6.2.3
and the preceding discussion, there is a commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\bf R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}}(\mathcal{M},f_{+}f^{!}\mathcal{N})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\bf R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}}}(\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\mathfrak{P}}\mathcal{M},\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\mathfrak{P}}f_{+}f^{!}\mathcal{N})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{{\bf R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}}}(\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\mathfrak{P}}\mathcal{M},{\bf R}\left]f\right[_{\mathrm{dR}*}\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{P}}}f^{!}\mathcal{N})}
\textstyle{{\bf R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{P}}{\mathbb{Q}}}}(f^{!}\mathcal{M},f^{!}\mathcal{N})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{{\bf R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{P}}_{K}}}(\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{P}}}f^{!}\mathcal{M},\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{P}}}f^{!}\mathcal{N})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{{\bf R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{P}}_{K}}}(f^{*}\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\mathfrak{P}}\mathcal{M},\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{P}}}f^{!}\mathcal{N})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\cong}

I can therefore replace
(X,Y,\mathfrak{P})
by
(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{Y},\widetilde{\mathfrak{P}})
, in other words, I can assume that
Y
is smooth, that
D:=Y\setminus X
is a strict normal crossings divisor, and that
\mathscr{F}
extends to a convergent log
F
- isocrystal
\mathscr{F}^{\sharp}
on the log scheme
Y^{\sharp}:=(Y,M(D))
.

Further localising on
\mathfrak{P}
, I can assume that
Y\hookrightarrow P
lifts to a closed immersion
i\colon\mathfrak{Y}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}
of smooth formal schemes. Set
\mathfrak{Q}=\mathfrak{P}\times_{\mathcal{V}}\mathfrak{Y}
, and let
\pi\colon\mathfrak{Q}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}
denote the projection, thus
\mathcal{M}\cong\pi_{+}\underline{{\bf R}\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Y}\pi^{!}\mathcal{M}
and similarly for
\mathcal{N}
. Since
\mathrm{sp}^{!}
is compatible with de Rham pushforward, by Proposition
6.2.3
, there is a commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\bf R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}}(\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\bf R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}}}(\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\mathfrak{P}}\mathcal{M},\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\mathfrak{P}}\mathcal{N})}
\textstyle{{\bf R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{Q}{\mathbb{Q}}}}(\underline{{\bf R}\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Y}\pi^{!}\mathcal{M},\underline{{\bf R}\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Y}\pi^{!}\mathcal{N})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{{\bf R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{Q}_{K}}}(\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\mathfrak{Q}}\underline{{\bf R}\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Y}\pi^{!}\mathcal{M},\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\mathfrak{Q}}\underline{{\bf R}\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{Y}\pi^{!}\mathcal{N})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}

where the vertical maps are isomorphisms by Proposition
4.2.3
and Theorem
3.2.1
respectively. Hence I can replace
\mathfrak{P}
by
\mathfrak{Q}
, and by the same argument I can then replace
\mathfrak{Q}
by
\mathfrak{Y}
. In other words, I can assume that
Y=P
.

With one final localisation on
\mathfrak{P}
if necessary, I can lift
D\subset P
to a strict normal crossings divisor
\mathfrak{D}\subset\mathfrak{P}
. I now let
\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}:=(\mathfrak{P},M(\mathfrak{D}))
denote the corresponding formal log-scheme, and set
d=\dim\mathfrak{P}
. Note that in this case
\mathscr{F}
is a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{\left]X\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}}
-module with connection, and
\mathcal{M}=\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}*}j_{*}\mathscr{F}[d]
, where
j\colon X\rightarrow P
is the given open immersion. The extension
\mathscr{F}^{\sharp}
of
\mathscr{F}
is a coherent
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}}
-module with integrable log connection, and I set
\mathcal{M}^{\sharp}:=\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}*}\mathscr{F}^{\sharp}[d]
to be the shift of the
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}
-coherent
\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}{\mathbb{Q}}}
-module associated to
\mathscr{F}^{\sharp}
. Thanks to Lemma
7.1.6
,
\mathscr{F}^{\sharp}
is locally free. Hence
\mathcal{M}^{\sharp}[-d]
is a locally projective
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}
-module.

The natural morphism

\mathscr{F}^{\sharp}(\mathfrak{D}_{K})\rightarrow j_{*}\mathscr{F}

of
\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}_{K}}
-modules induces a morphism

\mathcal{M}^{\sharp}(\mathfrak{D})\rightarrow\mathcal{M}

of
\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}{\mathbb{Q}}}
-modules, which in turn induces an
isomorphism

\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}\otimes^{{\bf L}}_{\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}{\mathbb{Q}}}}\mathcal{M}^{\sharp}(\mathfrak{D})\rightarrow\mathcal{M}

of
\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}
-modules by
[
CT12
, Theorem 2.2.9]
. This gives rise to the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\bf R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}}(\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{{\bf R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}}}(\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\mathfrak{P}}\mathcal{M},\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\mathfrak{P}}\mathcal{N})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\bf R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}}}(j_{*}\mathscr{F},\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\mathfrak{P}}\mathcal{N})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\bf R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}{\mathbb{Q}}}}(\mathcal{M}^{\sharp}(\mathfrak{D}),\mathcal{N})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\bf R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}_{K}}}(\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\mathfrak{P}}\mathcal{M}^{\sharp}(\mathfrak{D}),\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\mathfrak{P}}\mathcal{N})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\bf R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}_{K}}}(\mathscr{F}^{\sharp}(\mathfrak{D}_{K}),\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\mathfrak{P}}\mathcal{N}).}

It therefore suffices to show that the two maps

\displaystyle{\bf R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}}}(j_{*}\mathscr{F},\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\mathfrak{P}}\mathcal{N})

\displaystyle\rightarrow{\bf R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}_{K}}}(\mathscr{F}^{\sharp}(\mathfrak{D}_{K}),\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\mathfrak{P}}\mathcal{N})

\displaystyle{\bf R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}{\mathbb{Q}}}}(\mathcal{M}^{\sharp}(\mathfrak{D}),\mathcal{N})

\displaystyle\rightarrow{\bf R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}_{K}}}(\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\mathfrak{P}}\mathcal{M}^{\sharp}(\mathfrak{D}),\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\mathfrak{P}}\mathcal{N})

are isomorphisms. The second is a relatively straightforward consequence of the fact that the shift
\mathcal{M}^{\sharp}(\mathfrak{D})[-d]
is a locally projective
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}
-module. Indeed, letting
\mathcal{M}^{*}
denote the
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}
-dual of
\mathcal{M}^{\sharp}(\mathfrak{D})
, then
\mathcal{M}^{*}[d]
is a locally projective
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}
-module, and there is a commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\bf R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}{\mathbb{Q}}}}(\mathcal{M}^{\sharp}(\mathfrak{D}),\mathcal{N})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{{\bf R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}_{K}}}(\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{!}\mathcal{M}^{\sharp}(\mathfrak{D}),\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\mathfrak{P}}\mathcal{N})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{{\bf R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}{\mathbb{Q}}}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathcal{N}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}}\mathcal{M}^{*})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\bf R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}_{K}}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}},\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\mathfrak{P}}\mathcal{N}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}}}\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{!}\mathcal{M}^{*}).}

The bottom arrow is an isomorphism by Proposition
7.3.1
, and therefore the top arrow is also an isomorphism.

Finally, to see that

{\bf R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}}}(j_{*}\mathscr{F},\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\mathfrak{P}}\mathcal{N})\rightarrow{\bf R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}_{K}}}(\mathscr{F}^{\sharp}(\mathfrak{D}_{K}),\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\mathfrak{P}}\mathcal{N})

is an isomorphism, I will (after some set-up) apply Theorem
7.2.1
. First, note that the support
\left]X\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}
of
j_{*}\mathscr{F}
is disjoint from the divisor
\mathfrak{D}_{K}
. It therefore follows that the ‘restriction’ map

{\bf R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}}}(j_{*}\mathscr{F},\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\mathfrak{P}}\mathcal{N})\rightarrow{\bf R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}_{K}}}(j_{*}\mathscr{F},\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\mathfrak{P}}\mathcal{N})

is an isomorphism. Now write
i:D\rightarrow P
for the natural inclusion, thus the localisation exact sequence

0\rightarrow i_{!}i^{*}\mathscr{F}^{\sharp}(\mathfrak{D}_{K})\rightarrow\mathscr{F}^{\sharp}(\mathfrak{D}_{K})\rightarrow j_{*}\mathscr{F}\rightarrow 0

reduces to proving that

{\bf R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\sharp}_{K}}}(i_{!}i^{*}\mathscr{F}^{\sharp}(\mathfrak{D}_{K}),\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\mathfrak{P}}\mathcal{N})={\bf R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}_{\left]D\right[^{\sharp}_{\mathfrak{P}}}}(\mathscr{F}^{\sharp}(\mathfrak{D}_{K}),\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\mathfrak{P}}\mathcal{N})=0,

where I have written
\left]D\right[^{\sharp}_{\mathfrak{P}}
for
\left]D\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}
equipped with the log structure given by pulling back the given log structure on
\mathfrak{P}_{K}^{\sharp}
. Let
\mathfrak{D}_{1},\ldots,\mathfrak{D}_{n}
denote the irreducible components of
\mathfrak{D}
, with special fibres
D_{1},\ldots,D_{n}
. For each
J\subset\{1,\ldots,n\}
set

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}_{J}=\cap_{j\in J}\mathfrak{D}_{j},\;\;

\displaystyle\;\;D_{J}=\mathfrak{D}_{J,k}=\cap_{j\in J}D_{j}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}^{(J)}=\cup_{j\notin J}\mathfrak{D}_{j},\;\;

\displaystyle\;\;D^{(J)}=\mathfrak{D}_{k}^{(J)}=\cup_{j\notin J}D_{j}.

Using the open cover of
\left]D\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}
given by the various tubes
\left]D_{j}\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}
, I can therefore reduce to showing that

{\bf R}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}_{\left]D_{J}\right[^{\sharp}_{\mathfrak{P}}}}(\mathscr{F}^{\sharp}(\mathfrak{D}_{K}),\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\mathfrak{P}}\mathcal{N})=0

for each
J\subset\{1,\ldots,n\}
. Theorem
6.3.1
says that
\mathrm{sp}^{!}_{\mathfrak{P}}\mathcal{N}
is a constructible isocrystal on
\mathfrak{P}
, and
\mathscr{F}^{\sharp}
is a log convergent
F
-isocrystal on
P^{\sharp}
by assumption. Hence the required vanishing is precisely the content of Theorem
7.2.1
.
∎

Combining this with Proposition
6.2.3
, I obtain the following.

8.0.3 Corollary
.

Let
u\colon\mathfrak{P}\rightarrow\mathfrak{Q}
be a smooth and proper morphism of smooth formal schemes. Then the functor
{\bf R}u_{\mathrm{dR}*}
maps
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(\mathfrak{P})
into
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(\mathfrak{Q})
.

9.
Cohomological operations for constructible isocrystals

I can now show compatibility of
\mathrm{sp}^{!}
with the various cohomological functors for
\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}
-modules and constructible isocrystals. This will then enable me to deduce an analogue of Theorem
8.0.1
for pairs and for varieties.

9.1.
The case of formal schemes

I have already proved the compatibility of
\mathrm{sp}^{!}
with de Rham pushfoward along smooth morphisms
u\colon\mathfrak{P}\rightarrow\mathfrak{Q}
of smooth formal schemes in Proposition
6.2.3
. Similarly, if
\mathfrak{P}
is a smooth formal scheme, and
i\colon X\rightarrow P
is a locally closed immersion, I have already shown in §
6.4
that the diagram

\textstyle{{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{!}}
\scriptstyle{\underline{{\bf R}\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{X}}
\textstyle{{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(\mathfrak{P})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\underline{{\bf R}\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{X}=i_{!}i^{-1}}
\textstyle{{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{!}}
\textstyle{{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(\mathfrak{P}).}

commutes up to natural isomorphism, and that this is moreover compatible with the natural ‘adjunctions’ when
X
is either open or closed in
P
.

There are similar results for pullbacks and tensor products. For the case of pullbacks, suppose that
u\colon\mathfrak{P}\rightarrow\mathfrak{Q}
is a (not necessarily smooth) morphism of smooth formal schemes, and that
\mathcal{M}\in{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{Q})
. Then I can construct a natural (in
\mathcal{M}
) morphism

\psi_{u}\colon u^{*}\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{Q}}^{!}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{!}u^{!}\mathcal{M}

as follows. I first use the usual trick of taking the graph two divide into two separate cases: the first when
u
is smooth, and the second when
u
is a section of a smooth morphism
\pi\colon\mathfrak{Q}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}
.

In the first case, when
u
is smooth, of relative dimension
d
, say, giving a morphism

u^{*}\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{Q}}^{!}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{!}u^{!}\mathcal{M}

is equivalent to giving a morphism

\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{Q}}^{!}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow{\bf R}u_{\mathrm{dR}*}\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{!}u^{!}\mathcal{M}=\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{Q}}^{!}u_{+}u^{!}\mathcal{M}[-2d].

Since I am not assuming that
u
is proper, the functor
u_{+}
appearing on the RHS should be viewed as taking values in
\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}^{b}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{Q}})

It therefore suffices to construct a morphism

\mathcal{M}\rightarrow u_{+}u^{!}\mathcal{M}[-2d]

in
\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}^{b}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{Q}})
. Via the projection formula (which in the generality required here follows from
[
Car04
, Proposition 1.2.27]
as in
[
Car04
, §2.1.4]
) it suffices to consider the case
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Q}{\mathbb{Q}}}
. In this case
u^{!}\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}[d]
and the morphism I seek is one of the form

\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Q}{\mathbb{Q}}}\rightarrow u_{+}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}[-d].

After actually taking the colimit in the level
m
, and tensoring with
{\mathbb{Q}}
to land inside
{\bf D}(\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{Q}{\mathbb{Q}}})
, this will simply the morphism

\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Q}{\mathbb{Q}}}\rightarrow{\bf R}u_{\mathrm{dR}*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}

into the zeroeth relative de Rham cohomology group of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}
. To see that this morphism lifts to
\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}^{b}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{Q}})
simply amounts to making the same construction levelwise in
m
.

In the second case, suppose that
u
is a section of a smooth morphism
\pi\colon\mathfrak{Q}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}
, of relative dimension
d
, say. Then I can calculate

\displaystyle\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{!}u^{!}\mathcal{M}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{!}\pi_{+}u_{+}u^{!}\mathcal{M}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{!}\pi_{+}\underline{{\bf R}\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{P}\mathcal{M}

\displaystyle={\bf R}\pi_{\mathrm{dR}*}\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{Q}}^{!}\underline{{\bf R}\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{P}\mathcal{M}[2d]

\displaystyle={\bf R}\pi_{\mathrm{dR}*}\underline{{\bf R}\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{P}\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{Q}}^{!}\mathcal{M}[2d]

by compatibility of
\mathrm{sp}^{!}
with both de Rham pushfowards and the functor
\underline{{\bf R}\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{P}
of sections with overconvergent support. It therefore suffices to construct a morphism

u^{*}\rightarrow{\bf R}\pi_{\mathrm{dR}*}\underline{{\bf R}\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{P}[2d]

of functors from
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(\mathfrak{Q})
to
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(\mathfrak{P})
. Let

\left]\rm id\right[_{u}\colon\mathfrak{P}_{K}\rightarrow\left]P\right[_{\mathfrak{Q}},\;\;\;\;\left]u\right[_{\rm id}\colon\left]P\right[_{\mathfrak{Q}}\rightarrow\mathfrak{Q}_{K}

denote the natural inclusions, and

\left]\rm id\right[_{\pi}\colon\left]P\right[_{\mathfrak{Q}}\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}_{K}

the restriction of
\pi
. Thus
\underline{{\bf R}\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{P}=\left]u\right[_{\rm id!}\left]u\right[^{*}_{\rm id}
.

9.1.1 Lemma
.

The ‘forget supports’ map
{\bf R}\left]\rm id\right[_{\pi!}\rightarrow{\bf R}\pi_{*}\circ\left]u\right[_{\rm id!}
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Explicitly, if
\mathscr{I}
is an injective sheaf on
\left]P\right[_{\mathfrak{Q}}
, then
\left]\rm id\right[_{\pi!}\mathscr{I}
consists of sections of
\mathscr{I}
with compact support over
\mathfrak{P}_{K}
, and
\pi_{*}\circ\left]u\right[_{\rm id!}\mathscr{I}
consists of sections with compact support over
\mathfrak{Q}_{K}
. To show that
\left]\rm id\right[_{\pi!}\mathscr{I}=\pi_{*}\circ\left]u\right[_{\rm id!}\mathscr{I}
therefore amounts to showing that if
V\subset\mathfrak{P}_{K}
is open, and
S\subset\left]\rm id\right[_{\pi}^{-1}(V)
is a closed subset, then
S\rightarrow V
is proper if and only if
S\rightarrow\pi^{-1}(V)
is proper. Since
S\rightarrow V
is always partially proper, and
\pi^{-1}(V)\rightarrow V
is quasi-compact, this is clear.

To complete the proof, then, I need to show that
\left]u\right[_{\rm id!}\mathscr{I}
is a flasque sheaf on
\mathfrak{Q}_{K}
. But since
\left]u\right[_{\rm id!}
is partially proper, this was demonstrated in the proof of
[
AL20
, Corollary 3.4.7]
(note that the additional hypothesis there that the second morphism
g
is partially proper is not used to show that
f_{!}\mathscr{I}
is flasque).
∎

I am therefore looking to construct a morphism

u^{*}\rightarrow{\bf R}\left]\rm id\right[_{\pi\mathrm{dR}!}\left]u\right[^{*}_{\rm id}[2d],

and in fact an isomorphism between these two functors is provided by Remark
3.2.5
. This therefore gives rise to an
isomorphism

u^{*}\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{Q}}^{!}\mathcal{M}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{!}u^{!}\mathcal{M},

which is natural in
\mathcal{M}
.

9.1.2 Proposition
.

Let
u\colon\mathfrak{P}\rightarrow\mathfrak{Q}
be a morphism of smooth formal schemes, and
\mathcal{M}\in{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{Q})
. Then the morphism

\psi_{u}\colon u^{*}\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{Q}}^{!}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{!}u^{!}\mathcal{M}

is an isomorphism in
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})
.

Proof.

To show that
\psi_{u}
is an isomorphism, I can check that it is so after taking the pullback along any closed immersion
\mathrm{Spf}\left(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\right)\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}
where
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
is the ring of integers in a finite extension of
K
. After possibly making a finite base change (for which the result can be easily checked), I can in fact take such closed immersions with
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}=\mathcal{V}
. Thus the general case reduces to the special case when
u
is a section of a smooth morphism, in which case the given morphism which has already been shown to be an isomorphism.
∎

To show compatibility of
\mathrm{sp}^{!}
with tensor product, recall that in §
5.2
I constructed, for
\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N}\in\underrightarrow{{\bf LD}}_{{\mathbb{Q}},\mathrm{qc}}(\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(\bullet)}_{\mathfrak{P}})
, a morphism

\mathbf{L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{*}\mathcal{M}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}}}^{\mathbf{L}}\mathbf{L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{*}\mathcal{N}\rightarrow\mathbf{L}\hat{\mathrm{sp}}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{*}(\mathcal{M}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}^{\mathbf{L}}\mathcal{N})

in
{\bf D}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}})
. It is straightforward, though rather tedious, to upgrade this to a morphism in
{\bf D}(\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}})
. For
\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N}\in{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})
, I can therefore view it as a morphism

(9.1.1)

\mathrm{sp}^{!}\mathcal{M}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}}}\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{!}\mathcal{N}\rightarrow\mathrm{sp}^{!}(\mathcal{M}\widetilde{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}}\mathcal{N})

in
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(\mathfrak{P})
.

9.1.3 Corollary
.

The morphism (
9.1.1
) is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Again, this can be checked on stalks, in which case it follows from Proposition
9.1.2
together with the fact that both pullback functors
u^{!}
and
u^{*}
commute with the relevant tensor products.
∎

9.2.
The case of pairs and varieties

In Corollary
8.0.3
, the fact that
u
is proper implies that
{\bf R}u_{\mathrm{dR}*}={\bf R}u_{\mathrm{dR}!}
, so I could have equally well phrased the result in terms of compactly supported de Rham cohomology. It turns out that this version is the one that generalises to the case of pairs. In fact, I can use the extra flexibility of constructible isocrystals to remove the need for the formal schemes involved to be immersible in smooth and proper ones. To obtain a functor that is independent of the frame, I also need to include a shift.

9.2.1 Corollary
.

Let

\textstyle{X^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{Y^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{P}}

be a morphism of frames, such that
g
is proper,
\mathfrak{P}
is smooth around
X
, and
u
is smooth around
X^{\prime}
, of relative dimension
d
.

Then the functor
{\bf R}\!\left]f\right[_{\mathrm{dR}!}[2d]
maps
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime})
into
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(X,Y)
. The resulting functor only depends on the morphism of pairs
g\colon(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime})\rightarrow(X,Y)
. If
Y
is proper, then it only depends on the morphism of varieties
f\colon X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
.

Proof.

Let me first consider the claim that
{\bf R}\!\left]f\right[_{\mathrm{dR}!}[2d]
preserves constructible isocrystals. The key point is that I can use Theorem
3.2.1
repeatedly to replace our morphism of frames by one that is covered by Corollary
8.0.3
. Indeed, the question is local on
X^{\prime}
, which I can therefore assume to be affine, in particular
f
is quasi-projective. By Chow’s lemma, there is a morphism of varieties
g^{\prime}\colon Y^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow Y^{\prime}
such that
g^{\prime-1}(X^{\prime})\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}X^{\prime}
, and
g\circ g^{\prime}
is projective (therefore
g^{\prime}
is also projective). Alternately embedding
Y^{\prime\prime}
into either
\widehat{{\mathbb{P}}}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{N}
or
\widehat{{\mathbb{P}}}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}}^{N^{\prime}}
, and using Theorem
3.2.1
repeatedly, I can replace the original morphism of frames by one of the form

\textstyle{X^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{Y^{\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widehat{{\mathbb{P}}}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{N}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{P},}

and therefore apply Corollary
8.0.3
. Now the independence of
u
(and
g
when
Y
is proper) is a simple consequence of Theorem
3.2.1
, using transitivity of
{\bf R}\!\left]f\right[_{\mathrm{dR}!}[2d]
and the usual trick of taking products of frames.
∎

9.2.2 Definition
.

If
(f,g)\colon(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime})\rightarrow(X,Y)
is a morphism of weakly realisable pairs, I denote by

{\bf R}f_{\flat}\colon{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime})\rightarrow{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(X,Y)

the functor implicitly given by Corollary
9.2.1
. If
f\colon X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
is a morphism of weakly realisable varieties, I similarly denote

{\bf R}f_{\flat}\colon{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(X^{\prime})\rightarrow{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(X)

the functor obtained by choosing a suitable compactification of
f
.

9.2.3 Remark
.

Note that whenever
g
is a closed immersion, and
Y\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{P}
is a closed immersion into a formal scheme which is smooth around
X
, then
{\bf R}f_{\flat}
can be identified with the functor
f_{!}
of extension by zero along the induced morphism of tubes

f\colon\left]X^{\prime}\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}\rightarrow\left]X\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}.

If
(X,Y)
is a strongly realisable pair, and
(X,Y,\mathfrak{P})
is an l.p. frame enclosing it, I can also deduce from Proposition
6.2.3
and Theorem
8.0.1
that the composite (equivalence!)

{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X,Y)\subset{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})\overset{\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{!}}{\longrightarrow}{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(\mathfrak{P})\overset{{\rm res}}{\longrightarrow}{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(X,Y,\mathfrak{P})={\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(X,Y)

does not depend on
\mathfrak{P}
, and can therefore be denoted

\mathrm{sp}_{X}^{!}\colon{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X,Y)\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(X,Y).

Of course, there is a similar functor

\mathrm{sp}_{X}^{!}\colon{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X)\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(X).

whenever
X
is a strongly realisable variety. Moreover, these functors are t-exact for the dual constructible structure on the source, and the natural t-structure on the target.

9.2.4 Corollary
.

For any strongly realisable pair
(X,Y)
,
\mathrm{sp}_{X}^{!}
induces an equivalence of categories

\mathrm{sp}_{X}^{!}\colon\mathbf{DCon}_{F}(X,Y)\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{Isoc}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(X,Y).

For any strongly realisable variety
X
,
\mathrm{sp}_{X}^{!}
induces an equivalence of categories

\mathrm{sp}_{X}^{!}\colon\mathbf{DCon}_{F}(X)\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{Isoc}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(X).

9.2.5 Remark
.

Given that the category
\mathbf{Isoc}_{F}(X)
of locally free (overconvergent) isocrystals on
X
is the
p
-adic analogue of the category
\mathbf{Loc}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
of
\ell
-adic local systems on
X
, it may seem a little odd to see it appearing as a full subcategory of
\mathbf{Isoc}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(X)
, which I am promoting here as a
p
-adic analogue of the category
\mathbf{DCon}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
of
dual
constructible sheaves on
X
(not the category of constructible sheaves on
X
). But something similar does indeed happen in the
\ell
-adic theory. There is a fully faithful embedding

\displaystyle\mathbf{Loc}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathbf{DCon}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}

\displaystyle\mapsto{\bf D}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee})

where
(-)^{\vee}
is the dual functor for local systems, and
{\bf D}_{X}
is the Verdier dual functor. If
X
is smooth, then
{\bf D}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee})
is simply a shift of
\mathscr{F}
by
2\dim X
, but if
X
is singular this will generally
not
be a shift of a constructible sheaf. This at least goes some way to explaining why the proof of Theorem
10.0.2
below
has
to proceed via duality.

9.2.6 Corollary
.

Let
(f,g)\colon(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime})\rightarrow(X,Y)
be a morphism of strongly realisable pairs. Then the diagram

\textstyle{{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X,Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{sp}_{X}^{!}}
\scriptstyle{f^{!}}
\textstyle{{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(X,Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{*}}
\textstyle{{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{sp}_{X^{\prime}}^{!}}
\textstyle{{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime})}

commutes up to natural isomorphism. If
g
is proper, then so does

\textstyle{{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{sp}_{X^{\prime}}^{!}}
\scriptstyle{f_{+}}
\textstyle{{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\bf R}f_{\flat}}
\textstyle{{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X,Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{sp}_{X}^{!}}
\textstyle{{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(X,Y)}

Proof.

The statement for pushforwards follows from Proposition
6.2.3
. For pullbacks, it follows from Proposition
9.1.2
together with the fact that, for any smooth formal scheme
\mathfrak{P}
, and any locally closed immersion
i\colon X\rightarrow\mathfrak{P}
, there is an isomorphism

i_{!}i^{-1}\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{!}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{!}{\bf R}\underline{\Gamma}^{\dagger}_{X}.\qed

There is a similar statement for morphisms of strongly realisable varieties. Finally, I can show compatibility with tensor product using Corollary
9.1.3
.

9.2.7 Corollary
.

Let
(X,Y)
be a strongly realisable pair. Then there is a natural isomorphism

\mathrm{sp}_{X}^{!}(-)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathrm{sp}_{X}^{!}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\mathrm{sp}_{X}^{!}(-\widetilde{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}-)

of functors

{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X,Y)\times{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X,Y)\rightarrow{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(X,Y).

Naturally, there is an analogous statement for varieties.

9.2.8 Remark
.

Via the analogy between
\mathbf{Isoc}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(X,Y)
and
\mathbf{DCon}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
, the standard tensor product on constructible isocrystals corresponds to the dual tensor product

\mathscr{F}\widetilde{\otimes}_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}}\mathscr{G}:={\bf D}_{X}({\bf D}_{X}(\mathscr{F})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}}{\bf D}_{X}(\mathscr{G}))

for dual constructible
\ell
-adic sheaves. Note that this is t-exact for the dual constructible t-structure on
{\bf D}^{b}_{c}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
.

9.3.
Verdier duality

Of the six cohomological functors
f_{+},f^{+},f^{!},f_{!},{\bf D}_{X},\widetilde{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}
defined on
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}
, we have seen how to interpret three in terms of
{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}
, namely
f_{+}
,
f^{!}
and
\widetilde{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}
. This begs the question of how to interpret
f^{+},f_{!}
and
{\bf D}_{X}
in the theory of constructible isocrystals. Via the usual relations amongst
f_{!},f_{+}
,
f^{!}
and
f^{+}
, this amounts to describing the duality functor
{\bf D}_{X}
.

This is a subtle question that I don’t know how to answer at the moment. The main reason that I don’t yet have a good answer is the following: if
\mathfrak{P}
is a smooth and proper
\mathcal{V}
-scheme, say, then the composite functor

{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(\mathfrak{P})\overset{\cong}{\longleftarrow}{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})\overset{{\bf D}_{\mathfrak{P}}}{\longrightarrow}{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(\mathfrak{P})

cannot
be of a local nature on
\mathfrak{P}_{K}
. Indeed, if I denote by

{\bf D}_{\mathfrak{P}}\colon{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(\mathfrak{P})\rightarrow{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(\mathfrak{P})

the above composition, then clearly
{\bf D}_{\mathfrak{P}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}})=\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}}[-2\dim\mathfrak{P}]
. On the other hand, if
i\colon X\rightarrow P
is a smooth closed subscheme, then similarly
{\bf D}_{\mathfrak{P}}(i_{!}\mathcal{O}_{\left]X\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}})\cong i_{!}\mathcal{O}_{\left]X\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}}[-2\dim X]
. Since
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}}\!\!\mid_{\left]X\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}}=\mathcal{O}_{\left]X\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}}
, it follows that
{\bf D}_{\mathfrak{P}}
is not local on
\mathfrak{P}_{K}
. For example, this rules out any construction of the form
\mathbf{R}\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathcal{A}}(-,\mathscr{K})
where
\mathcal{A}
is a sheaf of rings on
\mathfrak{P}_{K}
and
\mathscr{K}
is a complex of
\mathcal{A}
-modules.

10.
Rigid cohomology of varieties

I will finish this article with a comparison theorem between rigid and
\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}
-module cohomology, at least for varieties
X
which admit an immersion
X\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{P}
into a smooth and proper formal scheme. In order to be able to state it properly, I need to extend (the shifted version of) Caro’s functor

\mathrm{sp}_{X+}\colon\mathbf{Isoc}_{F}(X)\rightarrow{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X)\subset{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})

from the case where
X
is smooth to the case of arbitrary
X
. In fact, this was achieved in
[
Abe19
, §3.8]
, by combining descent with the commutation of
\mathrm{sp}_{+}
with pullback. Note that the functor that I have been denoting
\mathrm{sp}_{+}
is denoted
\rho
in
[
Abe19
]
. I then define

\mathrm{sp}_{X!}:={\bf D}_{X}\circ\mathrm{sp}_{X+}\circ(-)^{\vee}\colon\mathbf{Isoc}_{F}(X)\rightarrow\mathbf{DCon}_{F}(X)\subset\mathbf{DCon}(\mathfrak{P})

where the functor
(-)^{\vee}
is simply the ordinary dual of locally free isocrystals.
18
18

18

Note that, since I am only considering locally free isocrystals here, this functor coincides with that of the same name define in
[
AL22
]
by
[
AL22
, Theorem 7.1.1]
.
Again, by using the fact that
\mathrm{sp}^{!}
is compatible with pullback, I can then remove the smoothness hypothesis on
X
appearing in Corollary
6.4.2
.

10.0.1 Lemma
.

Let
\mathfrak{P}
be a smooth formal scheme,
i\colon X\rightarrow P
be a locally closed subscheme, with closure
Y
,
\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{Isoc}_{F}(X,Y)
, and
\mathcal{M}=\mathrm{sp}_{X!}\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{DCon}_{F}(\mathfrak{P})
. Then
\mathrm{sp}^{!}\mathcal{M}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}i_{!}\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{Isoc}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(\mathfrak{P})
.

I can now state and prove my comparison result between rigid and
\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}
-module cohomology.

10.0.2 Theorem
.

Let
f:X\rightarrow\mathrm{Spec}\left(k\right)
be a strongly realisable
k
-variety,
19
19

19

Recall that this means
X
admits an immersion into a smooth and proper formal
\mathcal{V}
-scheme.
and
\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{Isoc}_{F}(X)
a locally free isocrystal on
X
of Frobenius type.
20
20

20

See Definition
3.3.1
.
Then
\mathrm{sp}_{X}^{!}
induces an isomorphism

\mathbf{R}\Gamma_{\mathrm{rig}}(X,\mathscr{F})\cong f_{+}\mathrm{sp}_{X+}\mathscr{F}

in
{\bf D}^{b}(K)
.

Proof.

Choose an immersion
i:X\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{P}
of
X
into a smooth and proper formal scheme. Set
d=\dim\mathfrak{P}
, and let
u:\mathfrak{P}\rightarrow\mathrm{Spf}\left(\mathcal{V}\right)
denote the structure morphism. Note that the claimed result is
not
an immediate consequence of Proposition
6.2.3
, since the functor
i_{!}\colon{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(X)\hookrightarrow{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(\mathfrak{P})
does not preserve rigid cohomology. Instead I will need to argue via duality.
21
21

21

Another explanation for why this is necessary is given in Remark
9.2.5
.

To start with, a direct calculation shows that
u^{!}\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Spf}\left(\mathcal{V}\right){\mathbb{Q}}}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}[d]
. Setting
\mathcal{M}=\mathrm{sp}_{X+}\mathscr{F}\in{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X)\subset{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})
, then
\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{!}({\bf D}_{X}\mathcal{M})=i_{!}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}\in{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(\mathfrak{P})
by Lemma
10.0.1
. I can therefore calculate

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}\Gamma_{\mathrm{rig}}(X,\mathscr{F})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}\mathrm{Hom}_{{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(X)}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee},\mathcal{O}_{\left]X\right[_{\mathfrak{P}}})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}\mathrm{Hom}_{{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{cons},F}(\mathfrak{P})}(i_{!}\mathscr{F}^{\vee},i_{!}i^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}_{K}})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}\mathrm{Hom}_{{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})}({\bf D}_{X}\mathcal{M},\mathbf{R}\underline{\Gamma}_{X}^{\dagger}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}{\mathbb{Q}}}[d])

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}\mathrm{Hom}_{{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})}({\bf D}_{X}\mathcal{M},\mathbf{R}\underline{\Gamma}_{X}^{\dagger}u^{!}\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Spf}\left(\mathcal{V}\right){\mathbb{Q}}})

using Theorem
8.0.1
. Now using the six operations formalism for overholonomic
\mathscr{D}^{\dagger}
-modules on varieties, I can rewrite this as

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}\mathrm{Hom}_{{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathfrak{P})}({\bf D}_{X}\mathcal{M},\mathbf{R}\underline{\Gamma}_{X}^{\dagger}u^{!}\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Spf}\left(\mathcal{V}\right){\mathbb{Q}}})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}\mathrm{Hom}_{{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X)}({\bf D}_{X}\mathcal{M},f^{!}\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Spec}\left(k\right)}^{\dagger})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}\mathrm{Hom}_{{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(X)}(f^{+}\mathcal{O}^{\dagger}_{\mathrm{Spec}\left(k\right)},\mathcal{M})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}\mathrm{Hom}_{{\bf D}^{b}_{\mathrm{hol},F}(\mathrm{Spec}\left(k\right))}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Spec}\left(k\right)}^{\dagger},f_{+}\mathcal{M})

\displaystyle=f_{+}\mathcal{M}.\qed

10.0.3 Remark
.

The analogous calculation for a lisse
\ell
-adic sheaf
\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{Loc}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
would be that

\displaystyle{\bf R}\Gamma_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X_{\bar{k}},\mathscr{F})

\displaystyle={\bf R}{\rm Hom}_{X_{\bar{k}}}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee},{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell,X})

\displaystyle={\bf R}{\rm Hom}_{X_{\bar{k}}}({\bf D}_{X}({\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell,X}),{\bf D}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee})),

see Remark
9.2.5
.
